# Near & Now — Full Codebase Deep Audit (2026-07-16, updated 2026-08-04)

Full-codebase pass across all 7 areas: `backend` (Express API), `frontend` (customer website), `admin` (admin panel), Supabase schema/migrations, and all three mobile apps (`near-now-store_owner`, `NAT_Near-Now_Rider-`, `nearandnowcustomerapp`). This supersedes/extends `supabase/SCHEMA_NOTES.md` (schema-focused). `bug_fixes_2026-07-15.md` (the original admin-approval-flow audit) has been merged into this file and deleted.

**Reading this doc:** `# Open Issues` comes first, grouped by area then severity, with each item's full original description so it's actionable without re-investigating. `# Fixed Issues` follows in compressed form (root cause + fix + date + any caveat that still matters, e.g. "not yet applied to the live DB") — enough to know what happened without the full investigation narrative. Reference material (pricing model, confirmed-not-bugs, build/test status, audit history) is at the very end.

---

# Open Issues

## Website frontend (`frontend/src`)

### Critical (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **"Create Account" link points to `/register`, a route that doesn't exist.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/layout/Header.tsx:495` — this link is in the always-rendered logged-out user dropdown; `App.tsx` has no matching route and no wildcard/404 route either, so every logged-out user who clicks it hits a blank page. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a real `/signup` route (`pages/SignupPage.tsx`) distinct from `/login` — both open the same underlying `AuthModal` (the phone+OTP flow is unified server-side; `verifyOTPCode` returns `isNewUser` and creates the account if needed), but each page now has its own copy/framing and a new `mode?: 'login' | 'signup'` prop on `AuthModal` swaps the step-1 heading/button text ("Login" vs. "Create Account"). Each page cross-links to the other ("New here? Create an account" / "Already have an account? Login").
- **"Add to Wishlist" button on every product page has no `onClick` handler at all.** Found 2026-08-04. `pages/ProductDetailPage.tsx:416-421` — sits directly under the primary "Add to Cart" CTA; clicking it does nothing, no toast, no state change, no wishlist feature exists anywhere in the app to back it. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by wiring the button to `showNotification()` (existing `NotificationContext` toast system) with a "Wishlist is coming soon!" message, so the click gives real feedback instead of silently doing nothing. A real wishlist feature is a separate, larger build — not done here.

### High (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **"Special Offers" mobile-menu link (`/offers`) has no matching route.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/layout/Header.tsx:722`. Not fixed.
- **5 Footer links are dead — appear on every page.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/layout/Footer.tsx:91-92` (`/contact`, `/faq`) and `:98-100` (`/returns`, `/payment-options`, `/delivery-info`) — none of these routes exist in `App.tsx`. Not fixed.
- **AboutPage's "Send Us a Message" contact form silently discards input.** Found 2026-08-04. `pages/AboutPage.tsx:216-256` — the `<form>` has no `onSubmit` and no `action`; the `type="submit"` button just triggers a native GET-to-self page reload, silently discarding whatever the user typed with zero feedback. Not fixed.
- **No stock/availability check on the Product Detail page.** Found 2026-08-04. `pages/ProductDetailPage.tsx` never reads `product.in_stock` — unlike `components/products/ProductCard.tsx` (lines 120,128,205-208), which correctly grays out and disables out-of-stock items on listing/search/home. A user who opens an out-of-stock product's own detail page (e.g. via a shared link) sees a fully enabled "Add to Cart" with no indication it's unavailable. Not fixed.
- **Same stock-check gap in the Quick View modal.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/products/QuickViewModal.tsx:184` hardcodes `"In Stock"` regardless of `product.in_stock`; "Add to Cart"/"Update Cart" is never disabled for out-of-stock items. Not fixed.
- **Three different, mutually inconsistent support contact details across the site.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/layout/Footer.tsx:249,258` (`nearandnowofficial2025@gmail.com`, `+91 94770 35649`, Kolkata) vs. `pages/HelpPage.tsx:77,84` (`support@nearandnow.com`, `+91 98765 43210`) vs. `pages/AboutPage.tsx:166-167,177,187` (a Bangalore address, `support@nearnow.com`, `+91 98765 43210`). A customer who emails/calls the number shown on Help or About may be contacting a nonexistent address, and the pages disagree with each other and with the Footer on the business's own city. Not fixed.
- **`ShopPage.tsx` renders a completely blank product area on a fetch error, with no retry.** Found 2026-08-04. Lines 35-72,337-379 — on failure, only a transient toast is shown; `loading` becomes `false` with `products=[]`, and if the user has a location set, none of the three empty-state branches (`noStoresNearby`, "no results from filters", "no products — no location") match, so the area renders nothing at all once the toast disappears. Not fixed.
- **`OrderTrackingPage.tsx`'s "Cancel Order" swallows failures entirely.** Found 2026-08-04. Lines 702-717 — on success it navigates to `/orders`, but any failure (`!d.success`, or the fetch throwing) only logs to console; no error message is ever shown, so a failed cancel looks like nothing happened. Contrast with `pages/ThankYouPage.tsx:95-126`'s correct `cancelError` state for the same action. Not fixed.

### Medium (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **"View All Reviews" (`components/products/QuickViewModal.tsx:231`) and both "Rate"/"Rate Your Experience" buttons (`pages/OrderTrackingPage.tsx:539-541,752-759`) have no `onClick` handlers** — no reviews feature exists anywhere in the app to back any of them. Found 2026-08-04. Not fixed.
- **Product cards always show 5 filled gold stars with "(0)"/"0 reviews" — fake ratings.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/products/ProductCard.tsx:156-169` and `QuickViewModal.tsx:141-149`. No real rating data exists, but the UI always visually implies a perfect, if unreviewed, product. Not fixed.
- **`WalletPage.tsx` has no transaction history** — only current balance + top-up; no record of past top-ups or wallet spend on orders is surfaced anywhere. Found 2026-08-04. Not fixed.
- **`HomePage.tsx` treats a fetch error identically to a genuinely empty catalog.** Found 2026-08-04. Lines 47-52,314-327 — falls through to the generic "No Products Yet" empty state with no retry button. Not fixed.
- **`OrdersPage.tsx`'s error banner has no retry action.** Found 2026-08-04. Lines 317-324 (`.op-error`) shows the error text but no "Try again" button, unlike `CategoryPage.tsx` (`cp-retry-btn`, lines 433-439), which does this correctly. Not fixed.
- **`OrderTrackingPage.tsx` conflates a genuine fetch/network error with "order not found."** Found 2026-08-04. Lines 191-195 — the fetch's catch block only `console.error`s, no `error` state exists, so a real API failure renders the same "Order Not Found" screen (lines 307-321) as a truly nonexistent order. Not fixed.
- **Product-detail breadcrumb link uses an un-encoded category name in the URL.** Found 2026-08-04. `pages/ProductDetailPage.tsx:312` — `to={`/category/${product.category}`}`, raw and unencoded, while `HomePage.tsx:195,273,300` and `Header.tsx:695` all correctly use `encodeURIComponent(category.name)`. A category name containing `&`, `#`, or `?` would break this specific link. Not fixed.

### Low (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **`/wishlist` link (`components/auth/UserDropdown.tsx:101`) and its containing component are dead code** — `UserDropdown.tsx`/`CartSidebar.tsx` are never imported anywhere; `Header.tsx` reimplements its own inline menu/cart link instead. Found 2026-08-04. Not fixed.
- **AboutPage's 3 social icons are all `href="#"`.** Found 2026-08-04. `pages/AboutPage.tsx:195,200,205`. Not fixed.
- **Cart icon in the header links straight into checkout, with no dedicated cart-review page.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/layout/Header.tsx:512-527,568-577` — likely intentional (no `/cart` route exists at all), worth confirming with product. Not fixed.
- **`SearchPage.tsx`'s search box bypasses React Router**, calling `window.history.pushState` directly + dispatching a synthetic `PopStateEvent` instead of `useNavigate`. Found 2026-08-04. `pages/SearchPage.tsx:49-60`. Works today but fragile/non-idiomatic, could silently break on a react-router upgrade. Not fixed.
- **`ThankYouPage.tsx` has a stale comment/constant mismatch** — comment says "auto-redirect after 7 seconds" but `THANK_YOU_DISPLAY_SEC = 3`. Found 2026-08-04. Lines 8,42. Cosmetic only. Not fixed.

### Low (new)

- **`eslint-plugin-react` was never actually installed, silently disabling every `eslint:react/recommended` rule and letting `npm run lint`/`npm run lint --workspaces` crash outright instead of reporting anything.** Found 2026-07-29 during a dependency-cleanup/vulnerability-audit pass, while re-running `npm run lint` after a clean reinstall. Root `.eslintrc.json` (lines 8, 20, 32) extends `plugin:react/recommended` and lists `"react"` in `plugins`, but `eslint-plugin-react` itself was never in any `package.json`/lockfile in this repo's history — confirmed via `git show <prior-commit>:package-lock.json | grep eslint-plugin-react`, zero hits before this session's change either, so this predates the dependency cleanup and isn't a regression from it. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding `eslint-plugin-react@^7.37.5` to root `devDependencies` (matching the existing `eslint@^8.45.0`/`eslint-plugin-react-hooks`/`eslint-plugin-react-refresh` versions already there). Once installed, lint actually ran for the first time and surfaced 141 real, previously-invisible problems (56 errors, 85 warnings) across `frontend/src` — see the two items below for what was fixed, and note the remainder (mostly `react/no-unescaped-entities` in the 4 static policy pages, and `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` scattered across `services`/`utils`) is a real, sizeable pre-existing lint debt, deliberately left open here rather than mass-fixed in one pass.
- **`services/supabase.ts`'s `getLocationFromStorage()` had a bare empty `catch {}` block (ESLint `no-empty`).** Surfaced by the `eslint-plugin-react` fix directly above (this project's ESLint config also enables `eslint:recommended`, which includes `no-empty`). The empty catch was intentional (malformed `localStorage` JSON should silently fall through to "no known location"), but with no comment inside, so ESLint flags it as looking like an accidentally-swallowed error. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding a one-line comment inside the catch block explaining the intentional swallow — ESLint's `no-empty` rule specifically exempts catch/finally blocks that contain only a comment, so this satisfies the rule with zero behavior change. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full test suite (26/26).
- **`utils/apiBase.ts`'s `getApiBase()` declared `base` with `let` despite never reassigning it (ESLint `prefer-const`).** Same surfacing as above. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by changing `let base = ...` to `const base = ...` — pure lint cleanup, zero behavior change (the value was already computed once and only read afterward). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full test suite (26/26).

## Supabase schema & migrations

### Low / informational

- The `coupons`/`product_images`/`product_attributes`/`product_details` grants added on 2026-07-18 (`SELECT` to `anon`/`authenticated` while RLS stays disabled on those tables) is exactly the trap `SCHEMA_NOTES.md` itself warns about — appears deliberate and carefully scoped (non-PII columns only, `product_reviews` explicitly excluded for its PII columns), but worth a second confirmation pass that RLS staying disabled there is intentional, and updating SCHEMA_NOTES to note the trap has since been (carefully) sprung rather than just theoretical.
- Two of three previously-dead RLS policies (`customer_own_orders`, `customer_own_record`) are still permanently dead code — they gate on `auth.uid()`, but the customer app doesn't use Supabase Auth (uses its own phone-OTP JWT), so `auth.uid()` is always NULL from that frontend, documented in an unrelated migration's own comment. **Correction 2026-07-27: the "not exploitable (fails closed)" claim below no longer holds** — see Critical above; a separate misconfigured policy on `app_users` bypasses RLS checking for that table regardless of whether `customer_own_record` itself ever evaluates true. Misleading either way — a future developer could believe there's a working customer-self-access RLS path when there isn't. **Consequence confirmed 2026-07-23:** the customer mobile app's Order Detail screen (`app/order/[id].tsx`) relies on exactly this dead `customer_own_orders` policy for its realtime subscription and has no polling fallback at all — see Customer app → Medium (new). **The third, `partner_own_record` on `delivery_partners`, is no longer dead** — see Rider app → High (fixed 2026-07-25): the backend now mints riders a real Supabase Auth bridge session specifically so this policy has a working `auth.uid()` to evaluate.
- **`stores.updated_at` doesn't reflect a verification-document upload for a not-yet-approved store — only bumped as a side effect of the suspend-on-edit path, which only fires once a store is already approved.** Found 2026-07-29, during the "Updated On" investigation above (Admin panel → Fixed). `stores` has a real `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`update_stores_updated_at`, migration `20260813000000`) that stamps `updated_at := now()` on any `UPDATE` — but it only fires when a row in `stores` itself is updated, and neither `store_images` nor `store_verification_documents` inserts/deletes touch the `stores` row directly. `saveVerificationDocument`'s only write to `stores` is via `suspendStoreIfApprovedAndGetName()`, which itself is a no-op unless `stores.is_approved` is currently `true` — so uploading documents during the normal pre-approval onboarding flow (the overwhelmingly common case) never bumps `stores.updated_at` at all. `deleteVerificationDocument` happens to always bump it (its own unconditional `UPDATE ... stores SET verification_submitted_at = null` triggers it as a side effect, not an intentional design). `addStoreImage`/`deleteStoreImage` do correctly bump it, via `syncStoreCoverImage`'s explicit `stores` write. **Not fixed — no visible symptom today**, confirmed via grep that `admin/src` fetches `stores.updated_at` (`StoresPage.tsx:467`, typed at line 32) but never actually renders it anywhere (only `created_at`, labeled "Joined," is shown) — the admin "Updated On" column reads from `store_verification_documents`/`store_images`/`store_profile_change_requests` directly (see the fix above), not from this column. Worth fixing before anything ever starts relying on `stores.updated_at` as a "last touched" signal, since right now it's silently incomplete for the most common document-upload case.

## Store-owner app (`near-now-store_owner`)

### High (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **"My Submissions" (custom-product review status) is unreachable from anywhere a shopkeeper can actually navigate.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/product-submissions.tsx` is a fully-built screen (pending/approved/rejected status + rejection reason), but its only entry point is `app/add.product.tsx:210`, which itself is never linked to from any tab/button/screen in the live app (confirmed via a full `router.push`/`router.replace` grep) — the "Add Custom" flow shopkeepers actually use lives inline in `app/(tabs)/stock.tsx`'s `AddCustomSection`, which has no link to submission status at all. A shopkeeper who submits a custom product has no way to ever check whether it was approved/rejected, or read a rejection reason. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a "My Submissions" row to `app/settings.tsx`'s new "Store" section, linking to `/product-submissions`.
- **Billing/bank-details screen is unreachable once a store is approved.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/billing-info.tsx` is only reachable via `components/VerificationNavBar.tsx`, exclusively part of the pre-approval onboarding flow. Once approved and in `(tabs)/home`, no menu item in Settings (`app/settings.tsx:99-102`) or Profile (`app/profile.tsx:640-666`) links back to it. A shopkeeper needing to correct a wrong bank account/IFSC post-approval has no in-app path to do so. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a "Billing Details" row alongside "My Submissions" in `app/settings.tsx`'s new "Store" section, linking to `/billing-info`.
- **Accepting a partial item selection on an order still shows the full original item list, not just what was accepted.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/previous-orders.tsx:355-383` (`acceptAllocation`) — the per-item accept/reject checkboxes (`AllocationCard`, lines 94-190) POST only the selected `itemIds`, but the local state patch on success (366-374) only flips `alloc_status` to `"accepted"` without trimming `alloc.items`. The "Active" tab (577-661) then renders every originally-requested item including ones the shopkeeper explicitly excluded — misleading about what to actually pack/hand off. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by filtering `a.items` down to the accepted `ids` in the same `setAllocations` patch, so the Active tab now only ever shows what was actually accepted.

### Medium (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Pull-to-refresh shows no spinner on all three Orders tabs.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/previous-orders.tsx:557,583,693` — `incoming`/`active`/`previous` lists all hardcode `refreshing={false}` on `FlatList`/`SectionList` despite wiring a real `onRefresh` — the fetch works, but the native refresh spinner snaps back immediately with no visual feedback. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding `refreshingActive`/`refreshingPrevious` state plus `handleRefreshActive`/`handleRefreshPrevious` wrappers around the poll-shared `fetchActiveOrders`/`fetchPreviousOrders` (which stay silent for `useSmartPoll`/focus-effect background calls) — pull-to-refresh now sets the flag true for the duration of the fetch on all three lists.
- **"Enable" push notifications button fails silently on permission denial.** Found 2026-08-04. `components/NotificationSettings.tsx:29-36` — `registerForPushNotifications()` returns `null` on many legitimate failure paths (`lib/notifications.ts:109-168`) with only a `__DEV__` console log; `handleEnable` does nothing visible when the token is falsy, no `Alert`, no error text. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a `lastRegistrationError` field to `NotificationService` (`'expo-go' | 'not-device' | 'permission-denied' | 'token-failed' | 'unknown'`, set right before each existing `return null` — no behavior change to the method itself) plus a `getLastRegistrationError()` getter; `handleEnable` now shows a tailored `Alert` — "enable in device settings" for permission denial, "not available in this environment" for Expo Go/simulator, generic retry message otherwise.
- **A single transient background-fetch error wipes the shopkeeper's entire product list to "No products yet."** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx:471-483` (`fetchStoreProducts`)'s catch block unconditionally calls `setStoreProducts([])` regardless of whether the call was silent (called from the realtime subscription, `useFocusEffect`, or after a toggle) — a transient network hiccup during any background refresh wipes "Your Stock" even though the store has real, unaffected inventory. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by only clearing `storeProducts` on a genuine (non-`silent`) foreground load; a silent background refresh that throws now leaves the existing list untouched instead of wiping it — stale-but-real data beats a false "no products" state.
- **No item price shown on incoming/active order cards.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/previous-orders.tsx:30-36` — `AllocationItem.price` is typed but never rendered in `AllocationCard` (106-190) or the Active tab item list (638-647). A shopkeeper accepting/preparing an order has no order value visible until later opening the invoice. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by rendering `item.price` when present — inline as `· ₹{price}` appended to the item name text in `AllocationCard` (incoming tab), and as a separate right-aligned `₹{price}` text in the Active tab's item list.
- **Notifications never deep-link to the related order.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/notification-inbox.tsx:192-210` — tapping a card only calls `markOneRead`, never reads `item.data` (which for `new_order` almost certainly carries an order/allocation id) to navigate there. A "New Order" notification is a dead end. **Fixed 2026-08-04** with an `openNotification` handler: `new_order` now navigates to `/(tabs)/previous-orders`. Note this app has no single-order detail screen at all — orders only exist as rows within the Orders tab's incoming/active/previous lists (backed by `allocation_id`, not the `orderId`/`storeId` the notification persists) — so the deep link is "open the tab that shows it" (which defaults to the Incoming sub-tab, matching where a `new_order` always starts) rather than a specific order screen.
- **Help FAQ gives an incorrect navigation path.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/help.tsx:20` says "Go to Settings > Store Settings" to change store name/address — no "Store Settings" entry exists in `app/settings.tsx`; the real path is Profile → Edit. **Fixed 2026-08-04** — corrected to "Settings > Profile > Edit". While fixing, found the FAQ text also claimed delivery radius was editable there; it isn't (confirmed via grep — `delivery_radius_km` only ever renders read-only in `profile.tsx:634`, no edit path anywhere in the app), so that claim was dropped rather than carried forward into the fix. Also added a note that name/address changes go through admin review before going live (`profile.tsx:59-60`), since that wasn't previously mentioned and could otherwise look like a bug ("I changed my address and nothing happened").

### Low (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **`app/add.product.tsx` is an orphaned near-duplicate of `stock.tsx`'s `AddCustomSection`**, unreachable from any live navigation path (see "My Submissions" above) — will silently drift out of sync (already using a deprecated `ImagePicker` API the live copy already updated). Found 2026-08-04. Not fixed.
- **`receiver_address` is collected but never shown** on the Active order card (only `receiver_name`/`receiver_phone` render, `app/(tabs)/previous-orders.tsx:50,624-635`). Found 2026-08-04. Likely low-impact since pickup happens at the store. Not fixed.
- **`products.quantity` is fetched (and an update function exists) but no screen renders or edits it** — `lib/storeProducts.ts:124-154,392-406` suggests this may be an intentionally-deprecated stock-count feature; if not, it's a missing capability. Found 2026-08-04. Not fixed.

## Rider app (`NAT_Near-Now_Rider-`)

### High (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Notifications list has no error/retry state — indistinguishable from "genuinely empty."** Found 2026-08-04. `app/notifications.tsx:46-56` — `fetchNotifications` catches all errors and just does `setNotifications([])`, no `loadError` flag at all (unlike `orders.tsx`/`earnings.tsx`, which both have a proper "Couldn't load — Try Again" state). A rider whose notifications fail to load sees the same "No notifications yet" as someone with genuinely zero, with no way to know it failed. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a `loadError` flag and a "Couldn't load notifications / Try Again" empty state, matching `orders.tsx`'s existing pattern (including its `retryBtn` styling).
- **Tapping a notification never navigates to the related order.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/notifications.tsx:143-159` — `onPress` only calls `markOneRead(item.id)`, never reads `item.data` to deep-link into `/delivery/[orderId]`. The OS push-notification tap handler (`_layout.tsx:114-119`) does route correctly when `data.orderId` is present, but the in-app notification list has no equivalent action. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding an `openNotification` handler: `new_order` notifications (which persist `{ orderId }`, per `backend/src/services/notification.service.ts:201`) now deep-link to `/delivery/[orderId]`; `new_order_offer` notifications (which persist `{ orderIds }`, a set, not a single order) route to Home where offers actually surface. Correction while fixing: the OS push-tap handler (`_layout.tsx:114-119`) does **not** actually deep-link either — it just does `router.push("/(tabs)/home")` regardless of `data.orderId` — the original audit note claiming it "routes correctly" was wrong; left as-is since only the in-app list was in scope here, but flagged for a future pass.
- **Vehicle Number field on Profile looks like a tappable link but isn't.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:429-434` — when unset, displays `"Set in Documents → Vehicle Registration"` phrased as an instruction, but the `FieldRow` is plain `Text`, no `TouchableOpacity`, no navigation, no visual cue (no chevron/color) that it's inert. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by giving `FieldRow` an optional `onPress` prop (wraps in `TouchableOpacity`, adds accent color + chevron when present); Vehicle Number now navigates to `/documents` when `vehicle_number` is unset.

### Medium (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Home offer card's "call" text has much lower visual affordance than Accept/Ignore buttons.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx:973-982,1005-1013` — plain small text next to a phone icon, no border/background, easy to miss for a rider under time pressure. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by turning `offerStoreCallBtn`/`offerStoreCallText` into a real chip (background, border, padding, bolder text) instead of bare inline text.
- **No connectivity/staleness warning anywhere except Home.** Found 2026-08-04. Home has a well-built "having trouble reaching the server" state (`pollingDegraded`, lines 443-447), but Orders/Earnings/`delivery/[orderId]`/Profile only show a spinner/blank on first load and swallow subsequent background-poll failures silently — e.g. a rider mid-delivery on a flaky connection has no way to tell the on-screen pickup-sequence/OTP state is stale. **Partially fixed 2026-08-04**: `app/delivery/[orderId].tsx` (the safety-critical one called out in the original finding — pickup-sequence/OTP state) now tracks consecutive silent-poll failures via `consecutivePollFailuresRef` and shows a "Connection issue — this screen may be showing outdated info" banner after 2 in a row, mirroring Home's `pollingDegraded` pattern; `orders.tsx` and `earnings.tsx` already tracked `loadError` on every fetch (including background/focus refetches) but only ever surfaced it via the empty-list state, so it was invisible once real data had loaded — both now show a small persistent banner (`staleBanner`) whenever `loadError` is true and the list/order array is non-empty. **`Profile` intentionally left out** of this pass — its only interval poll (`loadPendingChangeRequest`, 20s) is a low-stakes pending-changes check, not comparable in risk to the delivery/orders/earnings screens; a staleness indicator there is lower priority.
- **Documents screen has no per-document "this one failed" indicator distinct from the global batch alert.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/documents.tsx:233-260,344-355` — `handleSaveAll` continues to the next document after one fails mid-batch; the only feedback is a single generic end-of-batch alert, with no lingering failed/red state on the specific card that needs retrying. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a `failedKeys: Set<DocKey>` state, set in `saveOne` whenever `res.ok` is false (or the call throws) and cleared on a successful save or when the rider picks a new file for that key (`applyPickedImage`/`pickPdf`). The affected card now shows a red "Failed to save — tap below to retry" banner (reusing the existing rejection-banner styling) plus a red-tinted upload-area border, distinct from an admin `rejected` status.
- **Earnings screen's "Avg ₹/delivery" silently includes pre-payout-fix orders as ₹0 with no explanation.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/earnings.tsx:32-33,100-105,219-223` — `orderEarning()` treats `payout_amount == null` as `0` and shows "—" for those rows, but they're still counted in the deliveries count/average divisor — could look like underpayment to the rider with zero context given. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by dividing by `payoutKnownOrders.length` (orders with a real, non-null `payout_amount`) instead of `filteredOrders.length` — the numerator (`filteredEarnings`) was already unaffected since null-payout orders contribute exactly ₹0 to the sum, so only the divisor needed fixing. Also added a small note under the summary card ("N deliveries shown as ₹0 — payout data unavailable, excluded from the average") whenever `legacyOrderCount > 0`, so the ₹0 rows aren't left unexplained.

### Low (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Home's "Ignore" button disables globally, not per-offer, while any offer's Accept is in flight.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx:1020-1031` — `disabled={!!accepting}` blocks ignoring offer B for the ~1s round-trip while accepting unrelated offer A. Minor friction, not a real bug. Not fixed.

### Low (new — accepted edge case from a same-day fix)

- **`home.tsx`'s `isApproved` state never refreshes once `driverStatus` reaches `"active"`.** Found 2026-07-28. The only two places that call `setIsApproved` are the one-time mount fetch and the "poll while not active" effect — which explicitly tears itself down (stops polling, unsubscribes) the moment `driverStatus === "active"`. No other poll or realtime subscription on this screen refetches the full profile (`fetchOffers`/`fetchActiveOrder` hit different endpoints; the `delivery_partners` realtime channel only acts on status transitions, not `is_approved`). **Failure scenario:** if `is_approved` flips to `false` server-side while `status` stays `"active"` (a currently-hypothetical inconsistency — no known mutation path causes this today) and the rider doesn't background/foreground or remount Home, `canGoOnline` would stay stale-`true`. Self-documented as an accepted limitation at the time of the original fix, not a fresh oversight — left open rather than fixed, since closing it properly would mean either polling `/delivery-partner/profile` continuously even while active (extra load for a currently-hypothetical case) or wiring this screen into `useRiderVerificationGate`'s own AppState-driven re-check instead of maintaining a second, independent `isApproved` state. Not fixed. Fix direction: reuse `useRiderVerificationGate`'s `verified`/`isVerified` (already AppState-refreshed as of today's other fix) instead of this screen's own separate `isApproved` state, once that hook's extra document-fetch cost is judged acceptable for this screen too.

## Customer app (`nearandnowcustomerapp`)

### Medium (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Share icon on saved-address rows is a literal no-op.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/select-location.tsx:287-293` — `onPress={() => {}}`. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by wiring it to React Native's `Share.share()` with the address's label/address/landmark joined into a message.
- **The three "Quick Help" category cards in Support have no `onPress` at all.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/settings/support.tsx:31-33` — `SupportAction` components render with no `onPress` prop passed. **Fixed 2026-08-04** — no dedicated support-ticket/category flow exists anywhere in the app (confirmed via search; the only real support channels on this screen are `mailto:`/`tel:` links), so each card now opens the same `mailto:support@nearandnow.app` channel already used for "Email Us"/"Escalate an Issue", pre-filled with a category-specific subject line ("Order Issue" / "Payment or Refund Issue" / "Delivery Problem") rather than inventing a new ticketing system out of scope for this fix.
- **Product detail screen never shows rating/review count**, even though the exact same `Product` objects render `<StarRating>` on category-browse and search list cards. Found 2026-08-04. `app/product/[id].tsx`. A customer sees a star rating on a product card, taps in, and it vanishes on the detail page. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by rendering the same `<StarRating>` component under the product name, using `product.avgRating`/`product.reviewCount` — already returned by `getProductById()` (via `MASTER_PRODUCT_FIELDS`, which includes `rating`/`rating_count`), so no data-layer change was needed. Shown only when `avgRating > 0`, unlike the category cards which render it unconditionally (with a `(0)` count) — deliberately different here to avoid this fix introducing the exact "fake full rating" look already tracked as a separate open Website bug.
- **"Who is this order for" (receiver name/phone/address, captured for gift orders at checkout) is never shown anywhere post-purchase.** Found 2026-08-04. Not on order detail, tracking, or invoice screens — only implicitly present on the generated PDF. **Partially fixed 2026-08-04**: `app/order/[id].tsx` (the primary post-purchase screen) now shows an "Ordered for {name} · {phone}" row alongside the delivery address, using `order.receiver_name`/`receiver_phone` (already present on the `Order` type end-to-end, no data-layer change needed). `app/order/track/[id].tsx` (live tracking) was left as-is — `useOrderTracking.ts`'s result type doesn't carry receiver fields at all, so wiring it in would mean extending that hook's data shape, a larger change than this pass covered.
- **Notifications list has no error state distinct from "genuinely empty."** Found 2026-08-04. `app/notifications.tsx:86-100` — `fetchNotifications` catches all errors via `logSilentFailure` and just stops loading, no `error` state, so a network failure renders the identical "No notifications yet" as a real empty inbox, with no retry. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a `loadError` flag (set alongside the existing `logSilentFailure` call, not replacing it) and a "Couldn't load notifications / Try Again" empty state, matching the pattern already used in the store-owner and rider apps' equivalent screens.

### Low (new — 2026-08-04 frontend deep dive)

- **Bookmark icon on checkout's "Did you forget?" recommendation cards has no `onPress` handler.** Found 2026-08-04. `app/support/checkout.tsx:767-772`. Not fixed.
- **"Terms" and "Privacy Policy" in the pre-login consent line aren't tappable** — plain styled `Text`, no pressable wrapper, no navigation. Found 2026-08-04. `app/phone.tsx:128-130`. Not fixed.
- **GSTIN/business name entered at checkout is never shown on the order detail screen**, only implicitly present on the generated PDF invoice. Found 2026-08-04. `app/order/[id].tsx`. Not fixed.
- **`app/profile-setup.tsx` is orphaned/unreachable** — confirmed via repo-wide grep that nothing navigates to `/profile-setup`; the live flow is `otp.tsx` → `verify-email.tsx` → `onboarding.tsx`. Found 2026-08-04. Dead code, not a live bug, but duplicates onboarding's purpose. Not fixed.

### Low (new)

- **Unused `react-native-webview` dependency added.** Found 2026-07-31 reviewing Rounak Jana's `913e3dc` ("Update payment options and enhance checkout functionality"). Added to `package.json`/`package-lock.json` but never imported anywhere in the app — dead weight in every build. Not fixed.
- **Default payment selection doesn't visually match any of the new per-UPI-app rows on a fresh session.** Found 2026-07-31, same commit. `lib/paymentSelection.ts`'s `DEFAULT` is now `{ label: "UPI", ... }`, but `payment-options.tsx`'s new `UPI_APPS` rows are labeled `"Google Pay"`, `"PhonePe"`, `"Paytm"`, `"CRED UPI"`, `"BHIM UPI"`, `"Other UPI Apps"` — `isSelectedRail()` matches on exact `label`, so none of the six rows renders as pre-selected the first time a customer opens payment-options, even though the default rail is functionally UPI. Cosmetic only — checkout's pay button still reads `sel.mode`/`method` correctly regardless. Not fixed. (Checked and ruled out: the six rows all share `method: "upi"`, but `isSelectedRail()` also compares `label`, so picking one doesn't cause the others to falsely show as selected too — no cross-row confusion bug here.)
- **Minor/lower-confidence items, same audit:** `location/select-map.tsx`'s 300ms prediction-debounce `setTimeout` has no unmount cleanup (a quick back-navigation right after typing can call `setPredictions` on an unmounted screen); `useOrderTracking.ts`'s `driverLocations` state is merged and never cleared per `orderId` (inert stale-entry growth, harmless in practice); `order/confirmation/[id].tsx`'s 30s "add more items" window has no client-side lock preventing a second independent checkout from starting on the same cart before the window's own addition-payment flow completes (not confirmed to double-charge — depends on backend idempotency, out of scope here); `location/edit.tsx`'s Save button has no `disabled`/dimmed state while saving (internally guarded, so not exploitable, just visually unresponsive-looking, unlike `add.tsx`/`add-details.tsx`); `verify-email.tsx` has no auto-submit-on-4th-digit, inconsistent with `otp.tsx`'s auto-submit-on-6th-digit. None fixed.

---

# Fixed Issues

## Backend (`backend/src`)

### Critical

- **`supabaseAdmin` silently fell back to the anonymous client when the service-role key was missing/malformed.** Any of ~108 call sites expecting service-role RLS bypass would instead silently run as `anon`, degrading into confusing empty-result/permission-denied bugs. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by replacing the anon fallback with a `Proxy` that throws a clear error the instant anything actually tries to use it, closing the gap for every call site at once without touching them individually.
- **`requireRider` never checked `is_approved` on `rejectOrder`/`markPickedUp`/`markDelivered`** (only `acceptOrder`/`acceptOffer` did), so a just-signed-up unapproved rider could still call them. **Fixed 2026-07-23** with a new `requireApprovedRider(riderId)` helper called at the top of all three, mirroring `acceptOrder`'s existing 403 response.
- **Anon-callable RPC `finalize_order_if_ready` had no caller-identity check.** Migration `20260720000000` had granted `EXECUTE` to `anon`; this `SECURITY DEFINER` function bypasses RLS and could force-advance any order to `ready_for_pickup` before any store accepted it. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by revoking `anon`/`authenticated` execute grants (`20260810000000_lock_down_anon_rpc_grants.sql`, applied to the live DB).
- **No ownership check on order-mutating rider endpoints.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `acceptOrder` now atomically claims only unassigned orders; `rejectOrder`/`markPickedUp`/`markDelivered` now filter by `assigned_driver_id === req.riderId`.
- **`processRefund` had zero authentication.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `/refund` now requires `requireAdmin`. (`/methods` and `/:paymentId` on the same router are still open — see Backend High → Fixed for the PII-leak scope of that router.)
- **`placeCheckoutOrder` trusted client-supplied prices/total/payment_status** — the `order_total: 1, payment_status: "paid"` exploit. **Fixed 2026-07-16** — item prices are recomputed server-side from `master_products` (GST-aware, per the two-layer pricing model), `subtotal_amount` is recomputed from those trusted prices, `order_total` is floor-checked, and `payment_status` is always inserted as `'pending'`, only flippable via the signature-verified payment-verify path.
- **`signupComplete` (store owner + delivery partner) never verified OTP was actually completed.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** with a signed, short-lived HMAC "signup ticket" issued by `verifyOTP` and required by both `signupComplete` handlers.
- **`POST /api/orders/place` had zero authentication, trusting `user_id` in the body.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — now requires `requireCustomer`; `user_id` is always overwritten from the session.
- **Payment `/verify` endpoint didn't bind the Razorpay payment to the internal order it was verifying — a real captured payment could be replayed to mark a different (or higher-value) order as paid, and `/create`/`/verify` had no auth at all.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original exploit writeup). **Fixed 2026-07-29** in `payment.controller.ts`'s `verifyPayment()`: added a `payment.notes.internal_order_id === internalOrderId` check to `strictChecksPassed` (mirroring the already-proven `verifyTopup`/`verifyAdditionPayment` pattern elsewhere in this codebase), added `requireCustomer` to both `/create` and `/verify` in `payment.routes.ts`, and added an explicit `orderCtx.customer_id !== customerId` ownership check to both handlers (required adding `customer_id` to `getOrderPaymentContext`'s select/return, including its column-missing fallback branch, which turned out to be the one actually in use on the live DB — `customer_orders.razorpay_order_id` doesn't exist there yet). The website's `paymentGateway.ts` was never sending an auth header at all — updated to use the existing `getAuthHeaders()`/`authedFetch()` helpers (already the established pattern for other `requireCustomer`-gated calls); the customer mobile app's equivalent (`razorpayService.ts`) already went through a shared `apiFetch` wrapper that attaches the token automatically, so it needed no change. Verified live against real orders/customers (temporary session token, restored after): unauthenticated calls to both endpoints now 401; a validly-HMAC-signed `/verify` call against another customer's `internalOrderId` now 403s instead of succeeding; a real `/create` call for the caller's own order still succeeds end-to-end (created a genuine test-mode Razorpay order). The `notes.internal_order_id` mismatch path itself couldn't be exploit-tested the same way (would require an actual completed Razorpay transaction to replay), but is implemented identically to `verifyTopup`'s already-production-proven check. `vitest` 17/17 still passing (one test's mock updated for the new `customer_id` field).

### High

- **Shopkeeper signup crashed with a raw Postgres unique-constraint error on repeat phone.** Root cause: `stores.phone` had a `UNIQUE` constraint that conflicted with the real business model (one shopkeeper can own multiple stores on the same phone). **Fixed 2026-07-25** by dropping `stores_phone_key` (migration `20260816000000`) and returning friendly, detailed error messages (`describeSignupDbError`) instead of raw Postgres errors. Two same-session follow-ups: the friendly-generic-message version had to be made to specifically explain the known "migration not yet applied" case, and later expanded into a full technical-detail-plus-plain-English format per user request. Migration applied to the live DB same day.
- **Driver broadcast dispatch searched for nearby drivers relative to the customer's delivery address instead of the store/pickup location.** **Fixed 2026-07-24** — both `shopkeeper.controller.ts`'s auto-dispatch and `delivery.controller.ts`'s admin-manual broadcast now center the search on the order's actual pickup store instead of the drop-off address.
- **`deleteStoreProduct` had its own unfiltered role lookup (latent IDOR), unlike every other `storeOwner.controller.ts` handler.** **Fixed 2026-07-24** by routing it through the same shared `resolveShopkeeperFromToken()` helper the other 11 handlers already use.
- **Admin's `updateOrderStatus` had no state-machine validation** — could move a delivered order back to any earlier status, re-triggering customer notifications. **Fixed 2026-07-24** — rejects (409) any status change once an order is `order_delivered`/`order_cancelled`; also added the missing `picking_up` value to `validStatuses`.
- **`cancelOrder` had no order-status guard** — a delivered order could be "cancelled" and wrongly auto-refunded. **Fixed 2026-07-24** — throws if already delivered or already cancelled.
- **Shopkeeper "complete allocation" endpoint could mark an order delivered with no rider or OTP involved, and was silently auto-called on every real order 2 minutes after acceptance** (leftover simulation logic from before the real delivery flow existed) — could override a real in-progress delivery's status. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by removing the endpoint and the shopkeeper app's auto-call timer entirely; confirmed nothing else depends on it. (An Open Issues entry describing this same client-only timer as "should be a server-side scheduled job instead" survived as a stale duplicate until 2026-07-27, when a fresh grep of `previous-orders.tsx` found no timer/auto-complete logic left anywhere in the app — removed rather than re-fixed.)
- **No approval notification when admin approves a store or rider.** Admin approval is a direct Supabase write from the admin panel, with no Node code path to hook into. **Fixed 2026-07-23** via two new admin-only backend endpoints that re-verify approval server-side and fire the notification (reusing the existing persist+push pattern), called fire-and-forget from the admin panel after the approve write succeeds.
- **`getNotificationPreferences`/`updateNotificationPreferences` were a hardcoded stub and, once fixed, would have been an IDOR** (trusted `req.params.userId` instead of session). **Fixed 2026-07-23** — new `app_users.notification_preferences JSONB` column, real read/write, scoped to `req.customerId`. **DB migration not yet applied to the live database.**
- **Unauthenticated `/api/orders/create` trusted client-supplied `unit_price`**, allowing arbitrary-priced orders attributed to any customer. **Fixed 2026-07-23** — now requires `requireCustomer`, derives `customer_id` from the session, and reprices every item server-side using the same GST-aware formula as `placeCheckoutOrder`.
- **`createOrder` never checked store approval/active status** — the same auth/repricing fix above closed the price-trust gap, but its product lookup still only filtered `products.is_active`, with no join or check against `stores.is_approved`/`is_active`. Found 2026-08-04 during a deep dive following commit `64cf747` (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding `stores!inner(is_active, is_approved)` to the product-price lookup query with `.eq('stores.is_active', true).eq('stores.is_approved', true)`, mirroring the same pattern the audited commit uses in `frontend/src/services/supabase.ts`. A product from an unapproved/offline store now simply isn't found by the lookup, which already throws `"Product ... is not available at this store"` — an error string `createOrder`'s own catch block already maps to a 400, so no other change was needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Delivery/pickup OTP and pickup codes were brute-forceable** (no lockout on 4-digit codes). **Fixed 2026-07-16** (Jira SCRUM-14) — rate-limited to 3 attempts/3 minutes, keyed per rider+order.
- **Tracking endpoints leaked any order's address/driver PII to any logged-in customer** (no ownership check beyond "some valid customer"). **Fixed 2026-07-16** (Jira SCRUM-12) — all four `database.service.ts` tracking methods now verify the order belongs to the calling customer. A related gap in `getAgentLocation` (no relationship check between caller and requested rider) was **fixed 2026-07-23**.
- **Riders could spoof another rider's GPS / write arbitrary order status** — `updateAgentLocation` didn't check `agentId === req.riderId`, and `addTrackingUpdate` accepted any string as status with no ownership check. **Fixed 2026-07-24** — location update now 403s on mismatch; status update validates against the real enum and checks `assigned_driver_id`.

### Medium

- **Tip was not independently verified server-side, and wasn't even a real structured field** — the website folded it into `order_total` untraced, the customer app's tip was silently dropped by an undeclared schema field. **Fixed 2026-07-25** as part of building the first real rider payout system: new `tip_amount` column, accepted/stored/verified end-to-end, and folded into the trusted floor check (`20260818000000_tip_amount_and_rider_payouts.sql`). See Rider app Medium for the payout side.
- **Coupon discount was validated but never actually computed, enforced, or persisted at checkout** — `discount_amount` was hardcoded `0` everywhere, and `placeCheckoutOrder` didn't even accept a `coupon_id`. **Fixed 2026-07-23** across backend + both frontends — new `computeCouponDiscount()` helper (flat/percent/first-order rules, capped and clamped), real persistence, `recordCouponUsage` now fires from the real checkout path, and the trusted-total floor check subtracts the discount. Website got a coupon UI built from scratch (it had none). **Not click-tested live in a real browser** — verified via `tsc`, test suites, and a prod build pass only.
- **`acceptOffer`'s post-RPC follow-up writes were redundant, duplicate updates** left over from before `accept_driver_offer()` was rewritten to already do them atomically. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by removing the dead duplicate writes.
- **Coupon `usage_count` increment was a non-atomic read-then-write race**, letting concurrent redemptions push a coupon past its usage limit. **Fixed 2026-07-23** via a new row-locking Postgres function (`increment_coupon_usage_if_available`), migration applied to the live DB.
- **`GET /api/coupons/` returned every coupon with no way to tell at a glance which were currently valid.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** by attaching a computed `is_currently_valid` boolean to each row rather than filtering rows out (admin needs to see expired/inactive coupons too).
- **Admin-facing coupon/delivery-partner endpoints had no schema validation.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** — added Zod schemas wired through the existing `validate()` middleware, same pattern as `orders.routes.ts`.
- **Google Places/Geocoding/Directions proxy was fully public with no rate limiting** (billing-DoS risk). **Fixed 2026-07-23** — 60 requests/minute per IP via `express-rate-limit`.
- **`express.json()` had no explicit body size limit.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** — capped at 1MB with a clean 413 response instead of a generic 500.
- **`getOrderById` returned 500 instead of 404 for a non-existent order** (`.single()` throws on no match). **Fixed 2026-07-23** by switching to `.maybeSingle()`.
- **Three independent haversine/distance implementations existed in the backend.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** — consolidated into `backend/src/utils/geo.ts`.
- **`delivery_fee` was not independently verified server-side.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** as a business decision — delivery is ₹0 for now (launch-goodwill promo); backend, website, and the legacy `createOrder` fallback (`PER_STORE_DELIVERY_FEE`) all now force a trusted 0. Old tiered pricing preserved in git history to restore later.
- **Admin delivery-simulation could hijack a real, currently-busy rider's location/assignment** (found 2026-07-23). `deliverySimulation.service.ts`'s `findRealDriver()` picked any `delivery_partners` row at every fallback tier with no check for whether that rider was already `assigned_driver_id` on another real, non-terminal order — then overwrote their `driver_locations` row with fabricated GPS and reassigned them to the simulated order. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by adding a `getBusyDriverIds()` check (any driver who is `assigned_driver_id` on a different order not yet `order_delivered`/`order_cancelled`) and excluding those drivers at all three fallback tiers, not just the last one. If every driver is busy, the simulation now fails closed ("no free delivery_partners found") instead of hijacking one.
- **Duplicate "ready for review" verification-document notification possible under concurrency.** **Fixed 2026-07-18** at the DB layer — `mark_verification_submitted_if_ready()` locks the store row `FOR UPDATE` before deciding (migration `20260803000000`), same pattern as `finalize_order_if_ready`.
- **No global Express error-handling middleware** — surfaced concretely by multer's oversized-file error bypassing route try/catch and returning a non-JSON response. **Fixed 2026-07-17** with a catch-all error handler returning proper JSON for `MulterError` and any other unhandled error.
- **`invoice.service.ts` taxed every line item at a flat hardcoded 5% instead of each product's real `gst_rate`.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `fetchOrderData` now joins through to `master_products` for the real per-item rate and reverse-computes the true pre-tax value; the flat GoI checkout-level 5% is applied once on the subtotal instead. **Not covered by a new automated test** — verified via existing tests + manual reconciliation only.
- **`tracking_events` table was fully dead** — zero insert or select call sites anywhere in the monorepo (backend or any frontend/app), only `order_status_history` was ever actually used for order-status tracking history. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by dropping the table (migration `20260828000000`, applied live) rather than wiring up a feature nothing needed — confirmed 0 rows on the live DB before dropping.
- **`getAgentSchedule` was a one-line stub** (`return []`, ignoring both params) with zero callers anywhere — no frontend ever hit `GET /api/delivery/agents/:agentId/schedule`, and no underlying schedule data model exists to wire it to. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by removing the route, controller method, and service stub entirely rather than inventing a schedule feature with no real requirement behind it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and the existing 17/17 vitest suite, both clean.
- **Order-offer push notifications were never persisted to the `notifications` table.** `shopkeeper.controller.ts`'s `dispatchReadyOrdersToDriver` sent the Expo push via a raw `fetch()` directly, unlike `notifyRiderNewOrder`/`notifyShopkeeperNewOrder` in `notification.service.ts`, which both persist + push — so a rider who dismissed or missed the push (app killed, DND, notification swiped away) had no record of the offer in their in-app Notifications screen. **Fixed 2026-07-26** with a new `notificationService.notifyRiderOrderOffer(riderId, orderIds)` method (persists a `new_order_offer` row via the existing `persistNotification` helper, then sends the push), replacing the inline `fetch()` call. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and the existing 17/17 vitest suite, both clean; not click-tested live.

### Low

- **`getStoreName`/`suspendStoreIfApproved` did two separate round-trips for the same row.** **Fixed 2026-07-18**, merged into `suspendStoreIfApprovedAndGetName`.
- **Stale "5 verification documents" doc-comment.** **Fixed 2026-07-18** (`adminStores.controller.ts:11`).
- **`requireShopkeeper` blocked even read-only profile access for unapproved shopkeepers.** `shopkeeper.routes.ts` applied a single blanket `router.use(requireShopkeeper)` to every route including `GET /profile`, which 403'd with "Your store is pending admin approval" for *any* request from a newly-signed-up shopkeeper whose store hadn't been approved yet — including just trying to check their own status. Confirmed this endpoint is genuinely live-used (not dead code): both the store-owner mobile app and the website's `ShopkeeperApp.tsx` call `GET /shopkeeper/profile` directly. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by splitting the middleware in two: a new `requireShopkeeperAuth` verifies the session token and attaches `shopkeeperId`/store ids (still 403s if the account has zero stores at all — a genuine data-integrity case, distinct from "store exists but isn't approved yet") without the approval gate, and `requireShopkeeper` now wraps it, adding the approval check on top via a new `shopkeeperHasApprovedStore` flag. `GET /profile` now uses `requireShopkeeperAuth` directly; `GET /orders` and both allocation accept/reject routes keep the full `requireShopkeeper` gate, unchanged. `invoice.routes.ts`'s import of `requireShopkeeper` (gating the store's own invoice fetch) is untouched — invoices only make sense for a real order, so keeping that gate there is correct. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean. Not deployed/live-tested — this backend needs a deploy step (Vercel) outside this session's reach; any live shopkeeper session token in the database would only exercise the currently-deployed (pre-fix) code.
- **Expo push sends never inspected per-message error responses — stale/invalid tokens were never cleaned up, and failures were completely invisible.** Expo's push API returns HTTP 200 even when an individual ticket fails (e.g. `DeviceNotRegistered` for an uninstalled app) — the real per-message status lives in the response body's `data.status`/`data.details.error`, which none of the ~11 send call sites across `notification.service.ts` (the shared `sendExpoPush` helper, used by 9 different notification methods), `delivery.controller.ts`, and `shopkeeper.controller.ts` (2 raw batch `fetch()` calls to the driver-broadcast dispatch) ever looked at — only network-level exceptions were caught. **Fixed 2026-07-31**: `sendExpoPush` now parses the response, logs any ticket error, and — given an optional `staleTokenTarget: { table, idColumn, idValue }` — nulls out the dead `expo_push_token` on `DeviceNotRegistered`; all 9 call sites updated to pass their own table/column (`stores`/`id`, `delivery_partners`/`user_id`, or `app_users`/`id`, matching whichever table each one already queried the token from). The 2 batch-broadcast call sites needed different handling — Expo's batch response returns one ticket per input message in the same order, so a new `sendExpoPushBatchToDrivers()` maps `ticket[i]` back to `partners[i].user_id` and bulk-clears every stale id in one `.in()` update; both `delivery.controller.ts`'s and `shopkeeper.controller.ts`'s raw `fetch()` calls now route through it instead of hand-rolling the same request twice. Confirmed via grep that zero raw `exp.host` fetches remain anywhere outside the two shared helpers. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17), both clean. Not deployed/live-tested (same reason as the fix above — needs a Vercel deploy this session can't reach).
- **The backend's remaining 6 Low items, fixed together (2026-07-31), by order of complexity, then re-reviewed as a batch for interaction bugs:**
  1. **`auth.controller.ts` called `crypto.randomUUID()` without importing `crypto`** — worked only via Node's unprefixed global, unlike every other file using the same call (`storeOwner.controller.ts`/`admin.controller.ts`/`deliveryPartner.controller.ts` all explicitly `import crypto from 'crypto'`). Fixed by adding the same import for consistency/robustness.
  2. **Duplicate `declare module 'express'` Request augmentation in `invoice.controller.ts`** — redeclared `customerId`/`shopkeeperId`/`adminId`, already declared authoritatively in `customerAuth.middleware.ts`/`shopkeeper.controller.ts`/`adminAuth.middleware.ts` respectively (TypeScript module augmentation merges globally regardless of which file declares it — confirmed by the fact this file also uses `req.riderId` with zero local declaration, sourced purely from `deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s). Removed the redundant block entirely, replaced with an explanatory comment.
  3. **`validateDocNumber` silently accepted an unvalidated `number` for `aadhaar_back`/`pan_back` uploads** (store) **and additionally `driving_license_back`** (rider, same utility duplicated for the two verticals) — these back-side doc types have no number field at all (the number's only printed on the front), but were being treated identically to `trade`/`driving_license_front`/`vehicle_registration`, which genuinely have no *fixed format* but are legitimate to submit. Both cases previously hit the same "no pattern → accept anything" fallback. Fixed in both `verificationDocuments.ts` and `deliveryPartnerVerificationDocuments.ts` with a new `DOC_TYPES_WITH_NO_NUMBER_FIELD` set that `validateDocNumber` now explicitly rejects a non-empty number against, plus a clearer `docNumberErrorMessage`. Harmless in practice today (neither app ever sends a number for these fields) but now actively rejected instead of silently stored if one ever is.
  4. **`getProductsWithDetails()` queried a Postgres relation that doesn't exist live** (`products_with_details` — confirmed via `information_schema.tables`, zero rows) and its two callers (`GET /api/products/products`, `GET /api/products/:id`) 500'd on every real request. Re-confirmed zero callers anywhere across every frontend/app before touching it — deleted the method, both controller endpoints, both route registrations, the now-unused `ProductsWithDetails` type (from `database.types.ts` too, not just the import), rather than trying to recreate a relation nothing actually needs.
  5. **`sendSMS` was a dead stub** (`// TODO: Twilio/AWS SNS`, always returned fake success) — confirmed zero callers anywhere (this app's real OTP SMS goes through Twilio's Verify API directly in `auth.controller.ts`, a separate mechanism). Deleted rather than implementing a real generic-SMS feature nobody uses, mirroring the earlier `getAgentSchedule` precedent.
  6. **`getNearbyStores` did a full table scan of every active store + JS-side haversine filter, on what was documented as "a hot customer-facing path."** Investigating this turned out to contradict its own premise: re-confirmed zero callers anywhere across every frontend/app for this backend endpoint either — the actual live "nearby stores" logic is a Postgres RPC, `get_nearby_store_ids`, called directly from `frontend/src/services/supabase.ts`, entirely bypassing this backend route (same dead-code shape as item 4 above). Initially added a lat/lon bounding-box pre-filter (using the existing `idx_stores_location_active` index) to the dead method before discovering this — reverted that in favor of deleting the method/controller/route entirely once confirmed dead, rather than optimizing code nothing calls. **New finding surfaced by this investigation, not yet fixed:** `get_nearby_store_ids` — the actual live function — exists only in the live database and has **no corresponding migration file anywhere in the repo** (same untracked-live-schema-drift pattern documented elsewhere in this doc), and it has the *same* underlying performance shape (`SELECT id FROM stores WHERE is_active = true AND haversine_km(...) <= radius_km`, no bounding box or spatial index) that this Low item was originally about — just realized in SQL instead of JS. Not fixed in this pass (would need a new migration to both retroactively track the function and add a bounding-box/PostGIS optimization, which felt like a separate, larger decision than "fix these 6 documented items") — flagging for a future pass.
  
  All 6 verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17), both clean. Re-reviewed the full diff afterward (all 6 items together, plus the earlier `requireShopkeeper`/Expo-push fixes from the same session) looking specifically for interaction bugs — none found; every stale-token target matches its query's table/column, the `requireShopkeeper`/`requireShopkeeperAuth` composition can't double-respond, and every dangling import (`haversineKm`, `ProductsWithDetails`) left behind by the deletions was caught and removed.
- **`GET /api/products/master-products` returned raw, unfiltered `master_products.*` rows with no auth and no store-approval/online/delivery-range filtering.** Found 2026-07-29 while re-confirming customer product listings only ever source from `products` (they do — `frontend/src/services/supabase.ts`'s `fetchProductRows()` is driven `FROM products`, filtered by `products.is_active`/`stores.is_active`, joining `master_products` only for display fields; same dead-code shape as `getProductsWithDetails`/`getNearbyStores` above). Confirmed zero callers anywhere across `frontend/src`, `admin/src`, or `backend/src` — the legitimate `master_products` browsing surface for shopkeepers/admins already exists separately via `adminStoreProducts.controller.ts`. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by deleting the route (`products.routes.ts`), controller method (`products.controller.ts`), and the now-unused `databaseService.getMasterProducts()` + its dangling `MasterProduct` type import (`database.service.ts`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Website `HomePage.tsx` showed every category (with an empty "Top picks" section per category) whenever zero products were returned** — e.g. when no shopkeeper is currently online/approved, since customer listings are correctly gated on `stores.is_active` (see above). Root cause: `uniqueCategories`'s filter had `const hasProducts = products.length === 0 || productCategories.has(category.name)` — the `products.length === 0` clause meant "no products at all" was treated as "show every category" instead of "show none," backwards from the intended behavior where a category should only ever appear once a shopkeeper has real `products` rows in it. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing the `products.length === 0 ||` fallback so `hasProducts` is always a real category match; with zero products, `categories` is now correctly empty and the page falls through to its existing "No Products Yet" empty state instead of rendering a category grid with nothing underneath it. Confirmed via grep this was the only occurrence of the pattern (`ShopPage.tsx`/`CategoryPage.tsx`/`SearchPage.tsx` don't have it). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`backend/package.json` never declared its own dependency on `razorpay`**, despite importing it in 9 files. It only worked because npm workspaces hoist root's copy into a shared `node_modules` — if root's entry were ever removed, every payment code path would break with no compile-time warning. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding `"razorpay": "^2.9.6"` directly to `backend/package.json`'s `dependencies` (matching the version already declared at root) and running `npm install` to update the lockfile — confirmed `backend`'s own entry now appears under its workspace in `package-lock.json`. No `@types/razorpay` package exists to add (razorpay's own imports are already typed fine without one). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17), both clean.
- **Webhook fallback for `payment.captured` didn't account for split-payment totals.** `processWebhookEvent` (`payment.service.ts:572`, previously) computed its expected amount from `orderCtx.total_amount` only, without the split-UPI adjustment `verifyPayment` (same file, ~line 129) and `createPaymentOrder` already apply — so for split-payment orders this fallback always logged a false "amount mismatch" and skipped updating `payment_status`, relying entirely on the synchronous `/verify` call having already succeeded. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding the same `isSplit`/`orderCtx.split_upi_amount` check `verifyPayment` uses, so the expected amount now correctly matches the UPI-only portion for split orders (full total otherwise) — identical logic, now shared in shape (not extracted into a helper, to keep this a minimal, low-risk fix) between the two call sites. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17), both clean.

## Multi-store order dispatch (`order_store_allocations` / `driver_order_offers`)

All items in this area are now fixed.

### Reallocation flow (`shopkeeper.controller.ts`)

- **Order permanently stalled if no nearby store stocked a reallocated item.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — search widens from 4km to 8km, then flags for an admin-approved item refund instead of stalling the whole order.
- **Order permanently stalled if the reallocated store never responded.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `expireStaleAllocations()` auto-rejects any allocation still `pending_acceptance` after 5 minutes and re-offers to the next store, piggybacked on the customer app's existing tracking poll (no new cron needed).
- **Race condition: two stores accepting near-simultaneously could double-broadcast to drivers.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** via a new row-locked Postgres function, `finalize_order_if_ready(p_order_id)`.
- **`acceptAllocation` had a check-then-act race with no atomic guard** — two concurrent accept requests for the same allocation could both pass the read-check and both run side effects. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by moving the guard onto the update's own `WHERE status = 'pending_acceptance'` clause; the loser gets a 409 instead of double-running side effects.

### Driver broadcast flow (`shopkeeper.controller.ts` / rider app)

- **Offline or suspended drivers could still accept a stale pending offer.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `accept_driver_offer()` now checks approval/online/active status inside the same row-locked transaction.
- **No cap on how many drivers get offered one order.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — capped to the nearest 20.
- **No staleness check on driver location** used for broadcast eligibility. **Fixed 2026-07-16** — filtered to locations updated within the last 5 minutes.
- **Up to 15s lag before an already-accepted order disappeared from other drivers' lists.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — rider app now also subscribes to `UPDATE` events on `driver_order_offers`, not just `INSERT`.

## Website frontend (`frontend/src`)

### Critical

- **Client fully computed the amount sent to Razorpay, apparently with no server-side recheck.** **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — `payment.service.ts` independently recomputes the trusted amount from the DB and never uses the client's number for the actual payment; the finding was stale.
- **Direct-from-browser order-write fallback path** (`services/supabase.ts`'s `createOrder`, active whenever `VITE_API_URL` was unset) wrote orders directly from the browser with no server-side recheck of totals/discount/payment status. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by removing the fallback entirely rather than hardening it — a missing `VITE_API_URL` now fails loudly instead of silently degrading.
- **`createOrder`'s backend-API path sent `POST /api/orders/place` with no `Authorization` header** — meaning every website checkout going through the backend-API path would 401. **Fixed 2026-07-23** with a one-line `getAuthHeaders()` addition.

### High

- **GSTIN/business name and "order for someone else" receiver info were captured but never attached to the actual order.** First pass (2026-07-25) folded both into free-text `notes`; **escalated same session** to real structured columns (`gstin`, `gstin_business_name`, `receiver_name`, `receiver_phone`, `receiver_address` on `customer_orders`, migration `20260817000000`), threaded through backend, website, and surfaced to shopkeeper app, rider app, and admin panel. A second follow-up found the **customer mobile app had not actually been fixed** despite an earlier claim — `orderService.ts` was still stripping these fields back into `notes`; fixed the same day. Migration applied to the live DB.
- **`useOrderTrackingRealtime.ts` stale-closure bug** — `refreshOrderAndHistory` closed over a parameter that wasn't refreshed by the effect's intentionally-narrow dependency array, so any order field not explicitly reassigned silently reverted and never updated again. **Fixed 2026-07-24** using a `orderRef` pattern (matching the file's existing `buildRef` precedent) instead of widening the dependency array.
- **Realtime + unconditional poll running simultaneously with no stale-response guard**, and driver location polled every 2s unconditionally. **Fixed 2026-07-24** — added monotonic sequence refs (matching the mobile app's existing pattern) and gated the fallback polls to only run when realtime isn't confirmed connected; also subscribed to a real `driver_locations` realtime channel that had been publication-ready since April but never used. **Not fully smoke-tested live** against a real assigned-driver order.
- **No global 401 handling on the customer-facing site** — an expired session left the UI looking logged-in while every call silently failed. **Fixed 2026-07-25** with a new `authedFetch()` wrapper (hard-redirects to `/login` on 401, since `ProtectedRoute.tsx` turned out to be dead code, unwired into any route) — migrated 16 call sites across the codebase, including 6 the original audit had missed.
- **Invoice download sent the customer's raw UUID as a Bearer token** — worse than documented, this meant the button never worked at all (`requireCustomer` looks up by `session_token`, never by `id`). **Fixed 2026-07-24** by switching to the shared `getAuthHeaders()` helper.
- **Products from stores toggled offline were still visible to customers whenever no delivery location was set** — found while investigating the store-owner app's `setAllProductsOffline`/`restoreActiveProductsOnline` no-ops (see Confirmed Not Bugs). The location-based browsing path (`ShopPage.tsx`/`HomePage.tsx`) correctly hides an offline store's products via `get_nearby_store_ids`' own `WHERE is_active = true` filter, but `services/supabase.ts::fetchProductRows()`'s no-location branch — hit by a fresh session with no location yet, geolocation denied, or a direct link into `ProductDetailPage.tsx`/`CategoryPage.tsx`/`SearchPage.tsx`/header search, all of which call `getAllProducts()`/`getProductsByCategory()`/`searchProducts()` with no coordinates — queried `products` with only `.eq('is_active', true)` on the product row itself, no check on the parent store at all. A customer in this path could order from a store that had deliberately gone offline. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by adding `stores!inner(is_active)` to that query's select with `.eq('stores.is_active', true)`, mirroring what the location-based path already gets for free from the RPC. Verified live against the real DB with the anon key: fetching an offline store's active product by ID returned 1 row before the fix, 0 after. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Logout didn't clear the cart** (shared, unscoped `localStorage` state — real leak risk on shared/kiosk devices). **Fixed 2026-07-24** via a `CartProvider` effect watching the `isAuthenticated` true→false transition (couldn't hook it into `AuthContext` directly due to a circular-dependency constraint). Same fix applied to `LocationContext`'s saved delivery location.
- **Split cash/UPI payment amounts were never validated before order placement — the "Balanced" indicator was cosmetic only.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-07-29** on both ends: `CheckoutPage.tsx`'s `handleSubmit` now guards on `Math.abs(Math.round(cashAmt + upiAmt) - finalOrderTotal) > 1` (mirroring the existing wallet-balance check right below it) before submitting. While adding the equivalent backend check, found a bigger, previously-unverified root cause: `backend/src/routes/orders.routes.ts`'s `placeCheckoutSchema` (Zod) never declared `split_cash_amount`/`split_upi_amount` at all — confirmed live via a direct Node test that `z.object()` silently **strips** unrecognized keys by default, and `validate()` middleware replaces `req.body` with that stripped result — so these fields were being discarded before ever reaching the controller, meaning the split feature had no server-side record of its split amounts at all (a live DB query for any order with split data in `notes` returned zero rows). Fixed by adding both fields to the Zod schema, then adding the actual sum-vs-total check in `database.service.ts`'s `placeCheckoutOrder` right after the existing trusted-total floor check. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing 17/17 backend + 26/26 frontend test suites still passing; a full live end-to-end order-placement test (real geocoded address + verified email + nearby store) wasn't possible against this DB snapshot (no email-verified customer accounts and no store within range of any previously-used test address existed to reuse) — the Zod-stripping root cause itself was verified directly and empirically, and the arithmetic check added is simple/deterministic.
- **`CheckoutPage.tsx`'s own quantity stepper (Order Summary panel) ignored loose/weighed items — stepped by a whole `1` instead of 0.25kg, and used the wrong removal threshold.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup and failure scenario). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by mirroring `CartItem.tsx`'s already-correct loose-item stepping logic (0.25 increment/floor, remove only once quantity would drop to/below the 0.25 floor) inline in this stepper's `+`/`-` handlers, and displaying loose quantities with a `kg` suffix to match `CartItem.tsx`'s own display convention (previously showed a bare number with no unit for loose items here). `tsc --noEmit` clean; existing test suite 26/26 still passing (no dedicated test added — not click-tested live in a browser).
- **`ProductDetailPage.tsx` and `CategoryPage.tsx` both had the same stale-fetch race already fixed in `SearchPage.tsx` — missed on both.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup and both failure scenarios). **Fixed 2026-07-29**: `ProductDetailPage.tsx`'s `fetchProductDetails` effect now uses the same `cancelled`-flag pattern as `SearchPage.tsx` (guards both the `setProduct`/`setRelatedProducts` calls and the `finally`'s `setLoading`), and the effect now also resets the `quantity` stepper to `1` on every `productId` change. `CategoryPage.tsx`'s `fetchProducts` uses a monotonic sequence ref instead of a plain cancelled flag, since it's a memoized `useCallback` also invoked directly from the "Try again" button, not just from the effect — each call captures its own sequence number and only applies its result if that number is still current when the request resolves. `tsc --noEmit` clean; existing test suite 26/26 still passing (no dedicated tests added for either — not click-tested live in a browser).
- **Checkout page charged a redundant, double-counted 5% GST on cart items — on top of prices that already include each product's own real per-product GST.** Found 2026-07-31 while investigating a customer-app GST-consistency question. `services/supabase.ts::transformProductRowToProduct()` already bakes each product's real `master_products.gst_rate` into the price shown/added to cart (via `priceWithGst`) — the same trusted price the backend independently re-derives at order-creation time. `utils/checkoutCalculations.ts`'s `calculateCheckoutTotals()` then added a *further* flat 5% (`DEFAULT_GST_RATE`) on top of that already-tax-inclusive cart subtotal to produce the "GST (5%)" bill row, meaning every website order was overcharged by an extra ~5% on item cost beyond the real, per-product GST. **Confirmed by design to be removed** — not a legitimate charge. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by removing the overlay: `itemsGST` is now a fixed zero (kept in the return type for callers, but no longer added into `itemsMRP`/`itemsTotal`/`grandTotal`), and the "GST (5%)" row was removed from `CheckoutPage.tsx`'s bill display (the remaining "Taxable Value" row was renamed to "Item Total" since it's now simply the full item price, not a pre-tax figure with GST added separately). This required a matching backend change, since `database.service.ts`'s `placeCheckoutOrder` had a hardcoded validation floor (`trustedFloor = trustedSubtotal * 1.05 + ...`) that explicitly expected the client to keep sending this exact 5% markup — left as-is, every website order (COD or online) would have started failing at checkout with "Order total does not match item prices." Removed the `* CHECKOUT_GST_MULTIPLIER (1.05)` term from that floor so it now matches the corrected client total. Platform Fee/Handling Fee's own embedded-GST breakdown (`FEE_GST_RATE`) and the backend's real tax-invoice generator (`invoice.service.ts`, which independently computes the actual CGST/SGST breakdown for the legal invoice PDF) were deliberately left untouched — this fix is scoped to the checkout page's own item-total calculation only.
