The customer mobile app has no equivalent item-level GST overlay today (its `constants/fees.ts` only ever computed GST on Platform Fee + Handling Fee, never on cart items), so no mobile-app change was needed for this specific issue. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend` and `frontend`; backend vitest 17/17 passing; frontend vitest 26/26 passing. Not click-tested live (would need a real end-to-end order placement to observe the corrected total and confirm payment verification still matches).

### Medium

- **`SearchPage.tsx` had no request cancellation/staleness guard** — a slower, older search response resolving after a newer one had already set state would silently overwrite the fresher results. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with the standard cancelled-flag pattern already used throughout this codebase. New `SearchPage.test.tsx` reproduces the exact race with a timing-controlled mocked `searchProducts` (an earlier query resolves after a later one); confirmed the test actually catches the bug by reverting the fix and observing it fail before re-applying. Full suite: 18/18 passing.
- **`NotificationContext.tsx`'s auto-dismiss timers reset for *all* visible toasts on every array change** — the removal `useEffect` was keyed on the whole `notifications` array, so adding or removing any toast re-derived a fresh timer for every currently-visible one, clearing and restarting each one's countdown from scratch; a burst of notifications could pile up or overstay far longer than configured. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by scheduling each toast's removal once, at creation, independent of any other toast's lifecycle. New `NotificationContext.test.tsx` uses fake timers to prove an earlier toast expires on its own schedule even when a second arrives partway through; confirmed the test catches the bug by reverting the fix and observing it fail. Full suite: 19/19 passing.
- **`HomePage.tsx` never re-fetched products when the user's delivery location changed** (unlike `ShopPage.tsx`, which already did) — the effect ran once on mount only and never even passed a location argument to `getAllProducts()`, leaving the "nearby store" listing stale until a full page reload. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by mirroring `ShopPage.tsx`'s already-correct pattern: watches `LocationContext`'s `userLocation`, re-fetches on change (rounded to a ~110m grid via a ref-guarded key to avoid re-fetching on GPS jitter). New `HomePage.test.tsx` confirms `getAllProducts` is called with updated coordinates after a location change; confirmed it fails against the old code before re-applying the fix. `LocationContext` exported for testing, matching the existing `NotificationContext` precedent. Full suite: 20/20 passing.
- **`CartContext.addToCart` mutated the cart item object in place** when bumping an already-in-cart item's quantity — `updatedItems[existingItemIndex].quantity += quantity` wrote through to the same object still referenced by `prevItems`, even though the array wrapping it was a fresh reference. Any `React.memo`-wrapped cart row relying on prop identity to skip re-renders could silently miss the update, or a stale-captured reference elsewhere in the tree could observe the new quantity early. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by cloning the item itself (`{...item, quantity: ...}`) instead of mutating it in place. New `CartContext.test.tsx` asserts a reference captured before the second `addToCart` call keeps its original quantity and that the updated array entry is a new object; confirmed it fails against the old code before re-applying the fix. Full suite: 21/21 passing.
- **`CheckoutPage.tsx` had no mount-time empty-cart guard** — only a submit-time check and a render-time "Your cart is empty" message inside the summary panel, so landing directly on `/checkout` (bookmark, back button, refresh) with an empty cart showed a fully rendered, unusable checkout form instead of redirecting. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding a redirect-to-`/shop` effect, gated on a newly-exposed `hasLoadedCart` flag from `CartContext` so a real cart that simply hasn't finished loading from `localStorage` yet doesn't get incorrectly kicked out (cart-loading and checkout-guard effects fire in a specific parent/child order, and naively checking `cartItems.length === 0` on mount would false-positive on every page load). New `CheckoutPage.test.tsx` covers all three cases (still-loading → no redirect, loaded-and-empty → redirect + toast, loaded-with-items → no redirect); confirmed the loaded-and-empty case fails against the old code before re-applying the fix. Full suite: 24/24 passing.
- **GSTIN "claim GST credit" UI copy was misleading** — "Claim GST credit up to 18% on the order" implied the platform itself grants a credit. Root cause originally cited (GSTIN dropped from the payload) is now stale — the earlier High-severity GSTIN fix already wires `gstin`/`gstin_business_name` through to `customer_orders` (`CheckoutPage.tsx:523-524`), so it does reach the invoice — but the copy was still wrong on its own terms: attaching a GSTIN gets the business a compliant invoice they can use to claim *their own* input tax credit with their tax authority; the platform doesn't grant any credit. **Fixed 2026-07-30** — copy changed to "Get a GST-compliant invoice for input tax credit claims." Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and full suite (26/26, no logic touched).
- **API-base-URL resolution logic was duplicated in 3 places** — `utils/apiBase.ts` (the canonical `getApiBase()`/`apiUrl()`), plus byte-for-byte copies re-implemented as local `getApiBase()` functions in `services/authService.ts` and `services/orderService.ts`. Confirmed `authService.ts` had already drifted in practice: it imported the shared `apiUrl()` for one call site while still using its own local copy + manual string concatenation for two others (`sendOTP`, `verifyOTP`) — exactly the kind of inconsistency the finding warned about. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by deleting both local copies and routing every call site through the shared `apiUrl()` helper (`orderService.ts`'s 3 fetches, `authService.ts`'s 2). No behavior change — both local copies were logically identical to the canonical implementation. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean) and full suite (26/26 passing, no test changes needed since this is a pure refactor with identical output).
- **`ThankYouPage.tsx` had no fallback path if `location.state` was lost** — order details (items, total, address, status) came only from React Router state passed at navigation time, with no recovery mechanism; refreshing the post-payment page (very common — checking email, app-switching) dropped all of it and showed a bare "Order ID: N/A". **Fixed 2026-07-30**: `CheckoutPage.tsx`'s two `navigate('/thank-you', ...)` calls now also encode the order ID in the URL (`/thank-you?orderId=...`) as a durable recovery path; `ThankYouPage.tsx` reads that as a fallback via `useSearchParams()` and, when state is missing, fetches the full order through a new `getOrderById()` in `services/supabase.ts` (maps the backend's raw `customer_orders` + joined `store_orders`/`order_items` shape — different column names than the frontend's flat `Order` type — into the same `Order` shape the page already renders). New `ThankYouPage.test.tsx` covers both the state-lost/URL-fallback-fetch case and the normal state-present/no-refetch case; confirmed the fallback-fetch case fails against the old code before re-applying the fix. Full suite: 26/26 passing.
- **OrderTrackingPage's "Invoice" button linked to a non-existent route** — a plain `<Link to="/api/invoices/${orderId}">` (always 404, confirmed live: `curl` returns `404`), instead of the real customer-invoice endpoint `/api/invoices/order/:orderId/customer`, which also requires an authed request and returns a signed URL rather than being directly linkable. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by replacing the dead link with an authed-fetch button mirroring the already-working `handleCustomerInvoiceDownload` pattern in `OrdersPage.tsx` (fetch with auth headers → `window.open(data.url)`), with a loading-disabled state. Verified live against the local backend: the new path returns `401` (route exists, auth-gated) where the old path returned `404` (route doesn't exist). Full suite: 24/24 passing (no new test file — this fix mirrors an existing, already-tested pattern verbatim rather than introducing new logic).
- **The wallet feature (built 2026-07-27 for the customer mobile app, see Customer app → Medium (new)) didn't exist on the website at all** — flagged directly by the user, who then asked for it to be built here too. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a website-native UI on the same shared backend (the balance and every transaction are keyed by `app_users.id`, identical account either surface): new `services/walletService.ts` (thin `authedFetch` wrapper over the same `/api/wallet/*` endpoints the mobile app uses) and `pages/WalletPage.tsx` (`/wallet` route, linked from a new "My Wallet" row on `ProfilePage.tsx`) with a real fetched balance and a real Razorpay top-up flow; `paymentGateway.ts` gained `openRazorpayCheckoutForOrder()` (opens the Razorpay sheet for an already-created order object, e.g. a wallet top-up, instead of creating one itself via `/api/payment/create` like the existing `openRazorpayCheckout()` does) and exported `loadRazorpayScript`/`RazorpaySuccessResponse` for reuse. `CheckoutPage.tsx` gained a "Near & Now Wallet" payment-method card (parallel to Cash on Delivery/Online Payment, distinct from the pre-existing Razorpay-aggregator "Online Payment" tab) — greyed out when the balance can't cover the order total — and a `formData.paymentMethod === 'wallet'` branch in `handleSubmit` that pays the just-created order via `payOrderWithWallet()` instead of opening Razorpay, following the exact same "order is saved either way, payment failure just means retry later" pattern the existing online-payment branch already uses. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full existing test suite (26/26 passing, unaffected — no new tests added since this mirrors already-covered patterns).
  - **Deep-dive of this extension caught a severe, live-breaking bug that also affected the original mobile-app version of this feature, not just the website.** `customer_orders.payment_method` turned out to be a real Postgres **ENUM type** with only two defined values, `'razorpay'` and `'cod'` — confirmed live via `pg_enum`. Neither the wallet migrations (`20260910000000`/`20260911000000`) nor the mobile app's own `payment_method: 'wallet'` value had ever added `'wallet'` to this enum. This meant `pay_order_with_wallet()`'s `UPDATE customer_orders SET payment_method = 'wallet'` — and, on the mobile app, the order-creation `INSERT` itself, which passes `payment_method` straight through with no whitelist — would have failed outright with a Postgres enum-violation error on every single real attempt to pay with the wallet, on **either** app. Nothing caught this earlier: `tsc --noEmit` only checks TypeScript types (a wallet payment_method is a perfectly valid string literal at that level), and the backend's unit tests mock the Supabase client entirely, never touching a real Postgres enum constraint — only an actual live write would have surfaced it, and neither wallet fix had been click-tested live yet. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a dedicated migration (`20260912000000`, containing only `ALTER TYPE payment_method ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'wallet'` — this statement cannot run inside a multi-statement transaction with other DDL/DML, so it's isolated in its own file), applied to production and verified live (`pg_enum` now lists all three values). Also fixed a related, quieter bug in the *website's* separate order-creation path (`placeCheckoutOrder` in `database.service.ts`, used only by the website, not the mobile app): its `payment_method` label-to-enum mapping (`pm.includes('online') ? 'razorpay' : 'cod'`, a substring match on a freeform label like "Cash on Delivery") had no wallet case at all, so a wallet order would have silently fallen through to the `'cod'` default at creation time — meaning a failed wallet debit (e.g. insufficient balance) would leave the order looking exactly like an ordinary unpaid cash-on-delivery order, with no "retry payment" signal anywhere, since COD orders are never expected to need one. Fixed by checking `pm.includes('wallet')` first. Separately confirmed (no fix needed) that `customer_payments`, a legacy per-order payment mirror table sharing the same enum column, has exactly one other consumer in the entire codebase (`invoice.service.ts`) and that consumer never actually reads the field it fetches from that table — so leaving it unsynced for wallet payments has zero present impact; not worth adding write-plumbing for a table with no live reader. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend, frontend, and the mobile app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17) plus the frontend's full suite (26/26), all clean.
  - **A second deep-dive pass (explicitly requested) on this same website extension found and fixed 3 more real bugs.** (1) **Logic bug** — `CheckoutPage.tsx`'s new wallet branch checked only `formData.paymentMethod === 'wallet'`, ignoring the pre-existing, fully independent "Split Payment" toggle (`splitEnabled`), which this codebase already allows to combine with *any* payment-method radio choice (the existing `online` branch already guards against this with its own `&& !splitEnabled`). A customer who selected Wallet and also enabled Split Payment would have had the full order total silently debited from their wallet while the order was labeled `"Split — Cash ₹X + UPI ₹Y"` — no cash/UPI split ever actually happened, and nothing prompted for it. Fixed by adding the same `&& !splitEnabled` guard to the wallet condition and restructuring the wallet/online branches into a single `if`/`else if` (matching this file's own established pattern of "only one payment path fires, split wins when enabled") instead of the early-return I'd originally written, which bypassed that precedence entirely. (2) **Robustness gap** — the new `openRazorpayCheckoutForOrder()` (`paymentGateway.ts`) assumed the caller had already called `loadRazorpayScript()` first, unlike the existing `openRazorpayCheckout()`, which is self-contained; a future caller forgetting that implicit step would hit a confusing "not a constructor" rejection instead of the library's own clear "Razorpay SDK not available" error. Fixed by making it load the script itself, same as its sibling. (3) **UX/consistency gap, found on both apps** — neither `CheckoutPage.tsx` (website) nor `checkout.tsx` (mobile app) re-checked the wallet balance at submit time, only at selection time on the payment-options screen; a customer who selected Wallet and then added more to their cart (pushing the total past their balance) without reselecting a method could still submit — the backend safely rejects it either way (no money-safety issue), but this created an order already known to fail before ever trying. Fixed on both apps with a fresh balance re-check immediately before order creation, short-circuiting with a clear message instead. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend, frontend, and the mobile app; the frontend's full suite (26/26) and backend's (17/17), both clean.
- **`getCurrentUserFromSession` used the browser's anon Supabase client and was blocked by RLS** — this app never signs in via `supabase.auth` (its own phone-OTP JWT instead), so `auth.uid()` is always NULL and every query this function ran (`app_users`, `customers`) came back empty, meaning the function always silently returned `null`. Its one real caller, `AuthContext.tsx`'s `updateUserProfile`, masked this completely with a fallback to an optimistic local merge — so a "successful" profile save on the website never actually re-synced with real server state, it just silently faked it every time. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by routing through the already-existing, already-authenticated `GET /api/customers/me` (`requireCustomer`-gated) instead — the same endpoint the mobile app already correctly uses for this. No backend changes needed for the route itself; `userId` is no longer meaningful as a parameter (identity now comes from the bearer token, never a client-supplied id) but was kept in the function signature so the one call site didn't need touching. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full existing test suite (26/26 passing).
  - **Deep-dive of this fix caught a real, currently-live bug on *both* apps — not something this fix introduced, but something it newly exposed by making the read path actually succeed for the first time.** `getCustomerProfile()` (`database.service.ts`, backing `GET/PATCH /api/customers/me` for both the website and the mobile app) never selected `email_verified_at` in its `app_users` query, even though both apps' `AppUser` type declares it and both read it directly to show a "Verified"/"Unverified" badge. On the website this was invisible before this fix (the query never succeeded at all, so the fallback path — which correctly spread-merges into the *existing* user object, preserving `email_verified_at` — always ran instead). On the **mobile app, this endpoint already works and is already called on every cold start, every background session-revalidation tick, and every profile save** (`AuthContext.tsx`) — each of which does a full `setUser(fresh.user)` replace, not a merge — meaning an already-verified customer's email would silently flip to showing "Unverified" in Settings on a completely ordinary basis, independent of anything from this session's work. Not a functional/blocking bug (the actual checkout email-gate re-verifies against the database directly server-side, never trusting the client's cached flag) — but a real, misleading, live UI bug. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding `email_verified_at` to `getCustomerProfile()`'s select — a single source of truth fix that resolves it for both `GET` and `PATCH /api/customers/me` (the latter calls the former internally) and for both apps at once. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), clean.
- **`utils/encryption.ts` was dead code** — client-side "AES encryption" with a bundle-embedded key (defaulting to a hardcoded weak string, `'default-key-change-in-production'`, if the env var was unset), providing no real confidentiality even if it had been wired up. Confirmed zero importers anywhere. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting the file outright rather than leaving it as a trap for whoever eventually reaches for "encrypt this" and finds a plausible-looking, already-imported-nowhere function. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`components/ProtectedRoute.tsx` was dead code** — confirmed zero importers anywhere (one stray mention was only in a comment in `authHeader.ts`, not an actual import); `CheckoutPage`, `OrdersPage`, and others each hand-roll their own slightly different inline auth-redirect guard instead of using it, producing inconsistent UX for the same guard condition. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting the file and updating the misleading comment in `authHeader.ts` that referenced it as if it were a live, wired-in guard. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean. Not fixed here: unifying the several hand-rolled inline guards into one real shared guard — that's a larger refactor than "delete the dead placeholder," and no single existing guard is obviously the "correct" one to standardize on without a closer look at each page's specific redirect conditions.
- **Stale/undocumented Vite env vars.** `VITE_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` was still documented in root `.env.example` even though nothing in `frontend/src` reads it anymore — actively misleading, since it could tempt an operator into reintroducing the exact leaked-service-role-key-in-the-browser-bundle risk already fixed elsewhere in this doc. `VITE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` was undocumented anywhere, its only reader being the now-deleted `utils/encryption.ts`. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by removing the `VITE_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` line from `.env.example` (with a comment explaining why it's gone, so it doesn't just look like an oversight to a future editor); `VITE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` needed no doc change since its only reader is already deleted, above.
- **Header's live search-suggestions dropdown had the same unguarded stale-response race already fixed for the dedicated Search page, and navigated via a full page reload instead of React Router.** `components/layout/Header.tsx`'s debounced suggestion fetch cleared the previous *timer* but not an in-flight *request* — clearing the timer only stops back-to-back keystrokes within the 300ms debounce window; once a request was actually in flight, an earlier (now-abandoned) query's response could still resolve after a later one and clobber the fresher suggestions on screen (the same bug class already fixed in `SearchPage.tsx`, but never applied here since this is a separate implementation). Separately, `handleSearch`/`handleSuggestionClick` used `window.location.href = ...` instead of `navigate()` (used everywhere else in this file) — every search or suggestion click forced a full page reload, a real user-visible flash/remount on the single most common navigation action on the site. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by mirroring `SearchPage.tsx`'s existing `cancelled`-flag pattern (declared inside the effect, checked before each `setSearchSuggestions`/`setSuggestionsLoading` call, set in the same cleanup that already clears the debounce timer — no new ref/counter needed since the effect already had a cleanup hook), and switching both handlers to `navigate()` (added `useNavigate` to the file's react-router import, previously only `Link`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, the full existing test suite (26/26 passing, unaffected), a targeted `eslint` pass (only pre-existing `react/no-unescaped-entities` findings well outside this diff), and a full `vite build`.
- **No app-level scroll-to-top on route change anywhere in the app.** Confirmed via repo-wide grep: `window.scrollTo` only appeared in the 4 static policy pages, each scrolling itself on its own mount — no location-driven `ScrollToTop`-style effect existed in `App.tsx`/`Layout.tsx`. Scrolling halfway down `/shop` then clicking a `ProductCard` link to `/product/:id` rendered the product page already scrolled to the same pixel offset instead of at the top — affected every internal navigation via `<Link>` (product cards, category links, footer links), the majority of navigation in the app. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a new `components/ScrollToTop.tsx` (`useLocation()` + `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` on every `pathname` change, renders `null`), mounted once directly inside `<Router>` in `App.tsx` — deliberately as a fixed sibling of the route tree, not inside a `Route`/`Layout`, so it also covers the two standalone routes (`/driver`, `/shopkeeper`) that don't go through the shared `Layout` wrapper, not just the main site. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, the full existing test suite (26/26 passing), and a full `vite build`.
- **Re-filed from a misfiled Website entry: "Two parallel, inconsistent admin-auth systems"** (`secureAdminAuth.ts`, `adminAuthService.ts` — both live in `admin/src`, not `frontend/src`, hence the original misfile). Investigating found the real, narrower shape of the issue: `adminAuthService.ts`'s `authenticateAdmin()` is the genuine, active, backend-routed login (`POST /api/admin/login`) — not a duplicate system in active use. The actual problem was inside `secureAdminAuth.ts` itself: an entire second, fully independent login/token-refresh architecture (`secureAdminLogin`, `refreshAccessToken`, `getAccessToken`, `secureAdminFetch`) calling a Supabase Edge Function (`supabase/functions/admin-auth`) that turned out to have **zero real callers anywhere in the app** — confirmed via repo-wide grep. Worse, the actively-used `isAdminAuthenticated()` (gates every admin route) called the dead `getAccessToken()` as its *first* check on every single auth check — guaranteed to always return `null` (nothing ever populates the `adminAccessToken`/`adminTokenExpiry` sessionStorage keys it reads), so this ran on every page load for zero benefit before falling through to the real `adminToken`-based check. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting the entire dead Edge Function login/refresh path and simplifying `isAdminAuthenticated()` down to just the real, working session-row-validated check; kept the three genuinely-used exports (`getCurrentAdmin`, `secureAdminLogout`, `isAdminAuthenticated`) in place, unchanged in behavior. The Supabase Edge Function source itself (`supabase/functions/admin-auth`) was left untouched — out of scope, a separate concern from the dead frontend code that could never reach it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the admin panel and a full `vite build`; not click-tested live.

### Low

- **`OrderTrackingPage.tsx` fired a `POST /api/delivery/simulate/{orderId}` on every single real order** (any time status was `pending_at_store`/`store_accepted`, gated only by a sessionStorage dedup key, no demo/test flag) — the endpoint requires a valid admin session, so this always 401ed and did nothing for real customers, but it was a doomed network call fired on every real order's tracking page load, and it wrote to the same tables the backend's own delivery-simulation hijack bug (see Backend Medium → Fixed) was found to be dangerous against. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by removing the vestigial `useEffect` and its now-unused `SIMULATION_STORAGE_KEY` import; the real simulate trigger remains admin-only via the backend route/admin panel. Left `frontend/src/services/deliverySimulation.ts` itself in place — see the still-open dead-code finding above (Low) for its own separate, unrelated client-side duplicate function.
- **Greedy O(n³) store-assignment algorithm in `createOrder`'s fallback path — found already resolved.** The entire fallback path this algorithm lived in (writing `customer_orders`/`store_orders`/`order_items` directly from the browser via an anon-key client, trusting client-supplied totals) had already been removed in an earlier fix — confirmed by the code's own comment at the call site: *"There used to be a fallback here... bypassing every safeguard `placeCheckoutOrder` enforces... Removed rather than fixed."* No code change needed.
- **`utils/dataMasking.ts` was confirmed dead code with zero importers.** **Fixed 2026-07-31** by deleting it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Security utilities (`sanitize.ts`, `csrf.ts`, `rateLimit.ts`) had zero importers anywhere in `frontend/src`.** **Fixed 2026-07-31** by deleting all three — re-confirmed zero references anywhere in the whole `frontend/` project (not just `src/`, including config files) before removing. This also left their only two consumers among the app's npm dependencies, `dompurify` and `crypto-js`, completely unused; removed both from `frontend/package.json` (`npm uninstall`, correctly scoped to the `frontend` workspace only — confirmed the shared root `package.json` and its own separate `dompurify`/`crypto-js` entries were untouched) and updated `vite.config.ts`'s `security` manualChunk (renamed to `validation`, now just `['zod']`, the one package in that list still actually used, by `src/schemas/*`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, the full test suite (26/26), and a full production build (using a temporary local workaround for the pre-existing, unrelated broken `admin` manualChunk entry — see Build/Test Status) confirming the removed packages no longer appear anywhere in the output.
- **Full admin CRUD (products/categories/order-status) shipped inside the customer-facing frontend bundle.** `services/adminService.ts` exported `createProduct`/`updateProduct`/`deleteProduct`, `createCategory`/`updateCategory`/`deleteCategory`, `updateOrderStatus`, and several other admin-only reads (`getAdminProducts`, `getOrders`, `getCustomers`, `getDashboardStats`, etc.) — all executed via the anon-key `supabaseAdmin` client directly from the browser. Currently blocked by RLS, not a live vuln today, but exactly the class of "duplicate admin panel in customer frontend" bug the 2026-08 security cleanup was meant to remove entirely. Re-verified every single export's real usage individually (not just the ones the original finding named) via repo-wide grep, catching one false-positive along the way — `getOrderById` appeared to have 3 real importers, but all 3 turned out to reference a same-named, unrelated function actually defined in `services/supabase.ts`, not this file. Confirmed only `getCategories` (and its `Category` type) has a real caller, `HomePage.tsx`. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by trimming the file down to just those two exports plus the `supabaseAdmin` import they need — deleting `uploadProductImage`/`deleteProductImage`/`getAdminProducts`/`getProductById`/`createProduct`/`updateProduct`/`deleteProduct`/`getCategoryById`/`createCategory`/`updateCategory`/`deleteCategory`/`getProductCountsByCategory`/`getOrders`/`getOrderById`/`updateOrderStatus`/`getCustomers`/`getCustomerById`/`updateCustomerStatus`/`getDashboardStats` and their private helpers, along with the now-fully-unused `Order`/`Customer` interfaces. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full test suite (26/26) — `HomePage.test.tsx`'s existing mock only ever stubbed `getCategories`, so nothing needed updating there.
- **`services/deliverySimulation.ts` was dead code** — `runDeliverySimulation()` wrote fabricated status/GPS directly to `customer_orders`/`store_orders`/`driver_locations` via the browser's anon-key `supabaseAdmin` client using 3 hardcoded fake driver UUIDs, with zero callers anywhere (its one remaining reference, the `SIMULATION_STORAGE_KEY` constant, was removed from `OrderTrackingPage.tsx` in the fix directly above, fully orphaning the file). **Fixed 2026-07-31** by deleting it outright. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **The client named `supabaseAdmin` was actually constructed with the anon key, not a service-role key** — the misleading name every entry above referring to "the anon-key `supabaseAdmin` client" is describing. Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by renaming it to `supabaseNoSession` across all 5 files that import it (`services/supabase.ts`, `services/adminService.ts`, `services/auditLog.ts`, `hooks/useOrderTrackingRealtime.ts`, `pages/OrderTrackingPage.tsx`) and rewriting its comment to state plainly what actually distinguishes it from the plain `supabase` client (no session persistence/auto-refresh, not elevated privilege — RLS still applies). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full test suite (26/26).
- **`fetchProductRows`'s no-location branch had no snapshot isolation across its 500-row paginated full scan** — it live-joined `stores!inner(is_active, is_approved)` on every single page, so a store's approval flipping mid-scan could produce an inconsistent result set across pages of the same request. Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by restructuring `fetchProductRows` to resolve the eligible store-ID set as a single up-front snapshot — a new `getApprovedActiveStoreIds()` (one query against `stores`, no join) — then reusing the exact same chunked (`IN_FILTER_CHUNK_SIZE`) + paginated (`.range()`, 500/page) query path the location-filtered branch already used, now driven by `.in('store_id', ids)` instead of a live `stores` join on `products`. Both branches (location-filtered and no-location) now share one code path; the no-location branch is just the case where the store-ID set comes from a fresh snapshot query instead of the caller. As a side effect this also gives the no-location branch real per-chunk pagination it never had a join-based path for before, and removes the now-unnecessary `stores!inner` join and its two `.eq('stores.*')` filters entirely (nothing read the joined `stores` fields — confirmed via grep — the join existed purely to filter). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, the full test suite (26/26), and a live anon-key REST call confirming the new `stores`-then-`products` query shape returns correctly (the one approved+active store currently in this environment has zero products, unrelated to this change — confirmed via a raw unfiltered count).
- **60+ files contained unstripped `console.log`/`console.error` calls reaching production, including full order payloads and user IDs/phones.** Given the scale (35 files still had at least one call site at the time of this fix, likely more before earlier cleanups in this doc), gating each call site individually the way the mobile apps did wasn't proportionate — this app already has a Vite build, which can strip console statements at the bundler level in one place. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by adding `esbuild: { drop: mode === 'production' ? ['console', 'debugger'] : [] }` to `vite.config.ts` (dev/test builds keep every call for debugging; only `vite build`'s output is affected). This has a known blind spot: esbuild's `drop` only strips literal `console.x(...)` call expressions, not a bare reference to the method used as a callback value (e.g. `.catch(console.error)`, or `console.warn` passed directly as a geolocation error handler) — confirmed via an actual production build that 3 such point-free references survived in the output. Found and rewrote all 3 real call sites into explicit arrow functions (`.catch((err) => console.error(err))`, etc.) across `DriverApp.tsx` (3 sites) and `OrderTrackingPage.tsx` (1 site) so `drop` can see and remove them too; re-verified via a second production build that zero app-code `console.*` calls remain in the output (the one remaining hit found was inside the third-party Supabase SDK's own bundled code, not this app's source). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full test suite (26/26).

## Admin panel (`admin/src`)

### Critical

- **`getAdminClient()` silently downgraded to the anonymous client on any missing/expired token, for every call site** — not just login, but every subsequent data fetch mid-session. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by having `getAdminClient()` itself redirect to `/login` the moment no token is available, instead of silently returning an anon client.
- **Admin creation had no server-side role check** — any authenticated admin, including `viewer`, could assign themselves `super_admin` via a direct client-side insert. **Fixed 2026-07-24** — new `POST /api/admin/create` (`requireAdmin`-gated) checks the *caller's* own role server-side and requires `super_admin`; password hashing also moved server-side.
- **New dashboard stat tiles silently showed `0` instead of an error on query failure.** **Fixed 2026-07-18** by making the 4 new count queries participate in the existing error-throwing pattern.
- **Store approval was "blind"** — admin could approve without ever seeing submitted ID documents. **Fixed 2026-07-17** as part of the per-document verification rebuild (see Backend/general verification-documents work) — a "Review Documents" modal with per-document approve/reject.
- **Client-side admin login read `admins.password_hash` via anon-readable RLS, and there was no RLS on `admins`/`admin_sessions` at all** — meaning anyone with the anon key could read the admins table directly, or even forge a valid admin session by inserting an arbitrary session row. **Fixed 2026-07-16** (Jira SCRUM-69) — new server-side `POST /api/admin/login` does the lookup + `bcrypt.compare()` with the service-role client; new migration enables RLS on both tables. **Migration was not applied to the live database at the time of this fix** — code-side complete, DB-side needed manual application via the dashboard/CLI (status of that application not re-confirmed in this doc).
- **Document preview and Approve/Reject buttons vanished after any admin review action**, because the PATCH endpoint's raw-row response has no signed `url` and `review()` replaced the local document object with it outright. **Fixed 2026-07-17** by merging the response into the existing document object instead of replacing it. Confirmed by static code review, not live-device/browser QA.
- **Store/rider approval had no gate requiring verification documents to actually be reviewed or approved** — found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit; `toggleApproval` in both `StoresPage.tsx` and `DeliveryPage.tsx` was only ever `disabled={approvingId === ...}`, with no check on document status at all, so an admin could approve a store/rider that had never uploaded a document or had one explicitly rejected. **Fixed 2026-07-28** client-side on both pages: `refreshDocsUpdatedAt()` on each page now also builds a `docStatusByStore`/`docStatusByPartner` map (`store_id`/`partner_id` → `{doc_type, status}[]`) from the same bulk query it already ran for timestamps; a new `approvalReadiness()` helper on each page compares that against the required doc-type list (`DOC_LABELS`'s keys for stores; `DOC_LABELS` minus `vehicle_registration` for cycle/e-bike riders, mirroring the backend's `isVehicleRegistrationRequired()` in `deliveryPartnerVerificationDocuments.ts` — reimplemented client-side in `DeliveryPage.tsx` since the admin panel doesn't import backend code) and returns `{ready, reason}`. `toggleApproval` now short-circuits with a clear error (`setError`) before making the Supabase write if approving (not revoking — revoke is never gated) while `readiness.ready` is false; the Approve button is also `disabled` and shows the reason in a `title` tooltip in that state, so it's not just a post-click error. Revoke is intentionally left ungated in every respect (an admin must always be able to pull a store/rider offline regardless of document state). This is a client-side-only fix — the write still goes directly to Supabase via `getAdminClient()` with no server-side/RLS enforcement of the same rule (a raw authenticated Supabase call could still bypass it); that gap is intentionally left as a smaller follow-up, not folded into this fix. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (admin panel, clean) and a full `vite build` (clean); not click-tested live in a browser.
- **Store/rider approval RLS had no permission check — a `viewer`- or `manager`-role admin could approve or revoke any store or delivery partner, bypassing the intended read-only role.** Same finding as documented above (`toggleApproval` writes bypassing role checks via the session-only `admin_full_access` policy). **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new migration (`20260919000000_stores_delivery_partners_permission_rls.sql`), extending the `20260821000000` migration's `admin_has_permission()` RLS pattern from `master_products`/`categories` to `stores`/`delivery_partners`: dropped each table's `admin_full_access` policy and replaced its write half with `store_verification.edit`/`delivery_partners.edit`-gated INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies (matching the backend's own permission names for the equivalent routes in `adminStores.routes.ts`/`delivery.routes.ts`); `stores` kept its existing `public_read` SELECT policy untouched, `delivery_partners` got a new unrestricted `admin_read` SELECT policy replacing the SELECT half of `admin_full_access` (no role gates reads in the current permission model, matching the master_products/categories precedent). Applied to the live DB via `supabase db query --file ... --linked` and verified live via `pg_policies`/`has_table_privilege`. **Caveat found during that same live verification, not closed by this fix:** `delivery_partners` is confirmed fully effective (no other write-capable policy on that table could bypass it), but `stores` turned out to have a separate, previously-undocumented `"Allow all for service role"` policy (`roles: {public}`, `qual: true`) that grants any anon-key caller full write access regardless of the new gate — see Admin panel → Critical (open) for the full writeup. So this fix is live and correct on its own terms for both tables, but only *practically effective* for `delivery_partners` today; `stores` needs the separate Critical item fixed first before this one actually closes anything there.
- **Regression from the fix above, found the same day during a full regression review: gating all `delivery_partners` UPDATEs behind `delivery_partners.edit` broke `DeliveryPage.tsx`'s rider online/offline toggle for `manager`-role admins.** `DeliveryPage.tsx` has two distinct writes to this table — the approval toggle (correctly meant to require `.edit`) and a separate, routine ops action, `toggleOnline`, which previously only required *any* admin session. `manager`-role admins only ever get `.view`, never `.edit`, so their online/offline toggle silently started failing RLS with a misleading "insufficient permissions" message, while the button itself still rendered fully clickable. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new migration (`20260920000000_delivery_partners_online_toggle_permission_fix.sql`): loosened the RLS UPDATE policy itself back to `delivery_partners.view` (which every role has), and added a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`delivery_partners_enforce_edit_permission()`) that re-enforces `.edit` specifically when any approval-affecting column (`is_approved`/`status`/`approved_at`/`approved_by`) is actually changing — comparing `OLD` vs `NEW` directly, since a plain RLS policy's `WITH CHECK` can't see `OLD`. The trigger exempts `current_user = 'service_role'` so backend writes (`demoteRiderIfDocsIncomplete`, etc.) are unaffected — those already bypass RLS entirely for this exact reason, and shouldn't be gated a second time by a permission model that only exists for the admin panel's own session-header mechanism. One deliberate tightening, not a new regression: `toggleOnline`'s own code also flips `status` to `'active'` when a non-active rider goes online, and a `manager` attempting that specific sub-case now correctly still needs `.edit` — arguably closing a real pre-existing gap, since before *any* admin session (including manager) could flip a rider's status to `'active'` this way, bypassing the approval workflow entirely. Applied to the live DB via `supabase db query --linked` and verified live via `information_schema.triggers`/`pg_policies`.
- **`stores` had a live, currently-exploitable RLS hole identical in shape to the `app_users` instance (still open — see Supabase schema & migrations → Critical): a policy named `"Allow all for service role"` (`cmd: ALL`, `qual: true`) was scoped to `roles: {public}` instead of `{service_role}`, letting anyone holding the public anon key write any row in `stores` directly with no admin session at all.** Held on discovery (2026-07-28), per instruction not to touch production RLS without explicit authorization. **Fixed 2026-07-28**, on explicit request, after a full repo grep confirmed it was safe: the only anon-key writer of `stores` is the admin panel's approval toggle (already correctly gated by the `store_verification.edit`-requiring policies from the fix above); the backend only ever writes `stores` via `supabaseAdmin` (service_role, bypasses RLS regardless of policy roles); no other app (store-owner, rider, customer app, customer website) writes to `stores` via an anon-key client at all; and the two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions touching `stores` (`mark_verification_submitted_if_ready`, `review_store_profile_change_request`) both run as their owning role and already bypass RLS internally, independent of this policy. Fixed with a minimal `ALTER POLICY "Allow all for service role" ON public.stores TO service_role;` (migration `20260921000000_stores_fix_service_role_policy_scope.sql`) — changes only the policy's role scope, leaves its `USING`/`WITH CHECK` untouched. Applied to the live DB via `supabase db query --linked` and verified two ways: (1) `pg_policies` confirms `roles` is now `{service_role}`; (2) a direct anon-key-only `curl PATCH` against a real store (no admin token, setting `is_active` to its own existing value as a deliberately idempotent no-op) now returns `42501 permission denied for table stores` / HTTP 401, where it would previously have silently succeeded — and a follow-up anon-key `GET` on the same row confirms no data was altered by the test. This also retroactively makes the `store_verification.edit` RLS gate from the fix above actually effective for `stores` (previously moot in practice because of this hole), closing two documented issues with one migration.

### High

- **No per-endpoint/server-side permission enforcement for admin roles** — `hasPermission()` was only ever checked in React; a `viewer` could perform any write a `super_admin` could, and 6 direct-Supabase writes bypassed the backend entirely. **Fixed 2026-07-26** — new `requirePermission()` middleware across 20 write routes, plus RLS-level `admin_has_permission()` for the 2 direct-Supabase tables (`master_products`/`categories`). Also fixed a related drift bug: `hasPermission()` now computes from live `admin.role` instead of a stale stored snapshot.
- **Anon-writable RLS holes on 5 tables** (`master_products`, `categories`, `products`, `order_items`, `store_orders`) found while scoping the fix above — no admin session needed at all, not just a low-privilege bypass. **Fixed 2026-07-26** — dropped the 2 unused holes outright; the 3 load-bearing ones (needed by the shopkeeper app's anon-key "add custom product" feature) now go through new `shopkeeper_authenticated()`/`shopkeeper_owns_store()` SQL functions gated on a shopkeeper session header.
- **`updateAdmin`/`deleteAdmin` had zero role check** — same privilege-escalation class already closed on `createAdmin`, just missed on edit/delete. **Fixed 2026-07-25** — routed through new backend endpoints requiring `super_admin`, with a carve-out allowing any admin to change only their own password. Also moved edit-path password hashing server-side.
- **`PushNotificationPanel.handleSend` never actually sent a push** — only logged a fake-success row, with no real Expo API call. **Fixed 2026-07-25**; also fixed "All Apps" broadcasts only ever reaching drivers, never shopkeepers.
- **Unchecked mutations in `NotificationsPage.tsx`/`AdminHeader.tsx`** (same bug class as the earlier `StoresPage.toggleApproval` fix) — a stale session made writes silently no-op while the UI reported success. **Fixed 2026-07-25** across all 5 call sites using the same `.select()` + error/empty-result check.
- **Password-strength schemas were defined but never actually enforced** — `aaaaaaaa` was accepted for an admin account. **Fixed 2026-07-25** on both the frontend (real schema validation) and backend (`createAdmin`/`updateAdmin` shared a new `passwordStrengthError()` helper).
- **`admin_notifications.is_read` was a single global boolean shared by every admin** — reported directly by the user ("bell not catching updates," "super admins should also see notifications for other admins"). Confirmed live: TOTAL 61 / UNREAD 0, including a notification from 36 minutes earlier — once *any* admin's session (including via their own "mark all read") flipped a row to read, it silently went read for every other admin/super admin too, with no way to tell it apart from "genuinely already seen." **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new `read_by UUID[]` column (migration `20260827000001`, applied to the live DB via `supabase db query` after `supabase db push` proved unsafe — the CLI's remote migration ledger has zero recorded history despite the live schema being fully current, so a blind `push` tries to replay the *entire* migration history from scratch and hits old conflicts; every fix in this doc noted as "applied to the live DB" was done the same targeted way, not via `push`) plus `current_admin_id()`/`mark_admin_notification_read()`/`mark_all_admin_notifications_read()` SQL functions (mirroring `admin_has_permission()`'s existing x-admin-token header-read pattern). `AdminHeader.tsx` and `NotificationsPage.tsx` both now compute unread per-viewing-admin. `is_read` left in place, unused, rather than dropped (irreversible, harmless to leave).
- **Clicking a notification did nothing** — no navigation to whatever it was actually about, in both `NotificationsPage.tsx` and the header bell dropdown (confirmed live via click-test: URL never changed). **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new shared `admin/src/utils/notificationLink.ts` maps each notification type to its real detail route (`new_order`/`refund_required` → `/orders/:id`, `new_user` → `/customers/:id`, `product_updated` → `/products/edit/:id`; store/rider verification types have no per-record detail page today, so they route to the relevant list page instead of nothing). Clicking a row now also marks it read via the new per-admin RPC.
- **Admin Dashboard's stats intermittently failed outright** ("Failed to fetch" on every load, confirmed live, not just slow) — `getDashboardStats()` and `fetchDashboardData()` each separately paginated through the *entire* 44k+-row `master_products` table client-side (45+ sequential requests each, ~90 total) just to derive `products.length`, a unique-category count, and per-product images for the "top 5" panel — unreliable enough to fail under real conditions. **Fixed 2026-07-27**: `totalProducts` now a plain `count`; `totalCategories` and `CategoriesPage`'s own `getProductCountsByCategory()` (same underlying cost) now call a single new `get_product_counts_by_category()` SQL aggregate (migration `20260827000000`, applied live) instead of a full scan; the dashboard's "top products" panel now builds directly from already-fetched order-item data (which already carries name/price/image) instead of a second separate full-catalog fetch, and the fabricated "X in stock" badge (never real data, per the code's own comment) was dropped rather than kept faking a number.
- **Three pages had product/category/customer name text styled like a link (color-shifts on row hover) that was not actually clickable** — `ProductsPage.tsx`, `CategoriesPage.tsx`, `CustomersPage.tsx`; only a separate, easy-to-miss hover-only icon in the Actions column actually navigated. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by making the name itself a real `<Link>` to the same edit/detail route, in addition to the existing icon.
- **Newly created categories vanished from the Categories list immediately, with no other UI path to edit or delete them.** Found 2026-07-28 during a frontend-focused deep audit. `CategoriesPage.tsx`'s `filteredCategories` hard-filtered out any category with zero linked products, and `stats.totalCategories` did the same — since a brand-new category has 0 products by definition, it disappeared from the list the instant it was created, and the only links to `/categories/edit/:id` were rendered from that same filtered list, so there was no way back to fix a typo or delete it (it still existed in the DB and was assignable via `AddProductPage`'s unfiltered dropdown). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing the 0-product filter from `filteredCategories` (search-only now) and changing `stats.totalCategories` to the real total category count instead of only categories-with-products — the "0 products" badge in the Products column already handled a zero count gracefully (gray badge, "0 products" text), so no other change was needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and, live against the real database, that 5 categories (`Body Creams & Lotions`, `Intimate Hygiene`, `Nail Care`, `Beauty & Makeup`, `Sexual Wellness`) currently have 0 products and were confirmed to be exactly the ones this bug had been hiding.
- **Stores had no online/offline admin toggle — only a read-only badge — unlike riders, which already had one; and neither toggle (nor store/rider approve/revoke) notified other admins.** Found 2026-08-04, user explicitly requested both a store online/offline toggle and "sufficient admin notifs for that." **Fixed 2026-08-04**: added `toggleStoreActive` to `StoresPage.tsx`, mirroring `DeliveryPage.tsx`'s existing rider `toggleOnline` exactly (loading state via `togglingActiveId`, disabled + tooltip when not yet approved, direct `getAdminClient()` write to `stores.is_active`, live local-state update). Generalized the existing `notifyAdminAction()` helper (`adminService.ts`, previously hardcoded to `type: 'product_updated'`, used by `ProductsPage.tsx`) to accept an optional `type` param, and wired it into 4 call sites: `StoresPage.tsx`'s new `toggleStoreActive` and existing `toggleApproval`, and `DeliveryPage.tsx`'s existing `toggleOnline` and `toggleApproval` — all four were direct browser writes to Supabase that never touched `admin_notifications`, so other admins had no way to see any of them without polling the same page themselves (approve/revoke was a related, previously-undocumented instance of the same gap, found during this fix and closed in the same pass). New `store_status_changed`/`rider_status_changed` notification types added (icon: `Wifi`, emerald) to `NotificationsPage.tsx`'s type union/`TYPE_META`, `AdminHeader.tsx`'s `notifStyle()`, and `notificationLink.ts` (routing to `/stores`/`/delivery` on click); approve/revoke reuses the existing `admin_review_action` type. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build`; `admin_notifications.type` confirmed to have no restrictive DB constraint via `pg_constraint`, so no migration was needed for the new type values.
- **`ReportsPage.tsx` showed fabricated/hardcoded fallback numbers instead of real zeros, and a static "Growing" badge that ignored actual data.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — removed the `|| 198`/`|| 12`/`|| 8`/`|| 87`/`|| 23` fallback pattern from all 5 `MetricCard`s (a real 0 now displays as 0); replaced the permanently-static "Conv. Rate: 4.2%" tile (this app has no visitor/analytics tracking, so a real conversion rate can't be computed) with a genuine, computable "Cancelled" count instead of leaving a fake metric in place — swapped its icon from `Percent` to `XCircle` to match; the Revenue Trend "Growing" badge now reads `stats.revenueGrowth` and correctly shows "Declining" (red, `TrendingDown`) when revenue is actually down, "Growing" (green, `TrendingUp`) otherwise. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build`.
- **"Current Password" field on the admin Settings → Account tab was decorative — never validated or even sent to the server.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** on both ends: backend `admin.controller.ts`'s `updateAdmin` now requires and verifies `oldPassword` via `bcrypt.compare()` against the real `password_hash` whenever `id === req.adminId` (a genuine self-service password change) before accepting a new one — a `super_admin` resetting a *different* admin's password (`id !== req.adminId`, already gated to `super_admin` by existing logic) is a deliberate reset flow with no old password to prove, so that path is intentionally left unchanged. `SettingsPage.tsx`'s `changePassword` now sends `oldPassword` in the request and requires it non-empty before submitting (previously only checked client-side then discarded). `adminAuthService.ts`'s `UpdateAdminData`/`updateAdmin()` extended to pass it through. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend`/`admin` and a full `vite build`.
- **Order list/detail never showed which store or which rider was handling an order.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `adminService.ts`'s `Order` interface gained `stores: {id, name}[]` and `delivery_partner: {id, name, phone} | null`; `getOrders()`/`getOrderById()` now additionally select `store_orders.delivery_partner_id`, batch-fetch the referenced `stores`/`delivery_partners` rows, and attach them per order. `OrdersPage.tsx`'s table gained a "Store / Rider" column (store name(s) + assigned rider or "Unassigned"); `OrderDetailPage.tsx` gained a new "Fulfillment" card (store name(s), rider name + phone or "Not yet assigned", with a link to the Delivery page). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build`.
- **Store "Delete" was declared impossible (Confirmed Not Bugs, below) due to `RESTRICT` foreign keys on any store with order history — and the rider "Delete" button, which already existed, turned out to have the exact same problem, silently.** Found/corrected 2026-08-04, on direct user pushback: real deletion doesn't have to mean a hard `DELETE` row — the actual requirement was "remove from the active roster, keep history," which soft-delete satisfies without touching any constraint. Investigating the *existing* rider delete (`database.service.ts`'s old `deleteDeliveryPartner`) to build the store equivalent surfaced that it was already broken the same way: it attempted a real `delivery_partners.delete()` (blocked by `delivery_partners_payouts` `RESTRICT` and `driver_order_offers`/`customer_orders.assigned_driver_id` `NO ACTION` for any rider with a completed delivery) **without even checking that call's error** — silently proceeding to delete `app_users`, which would then fail loudly via the `delivery_partners_user_id_fkey` cascade hitting the same block. **Fixed 2026-08-04** for both: new `stores.deleted_at`/`delivery_partners.deleted_at` columns (migration `20260930010000`, applied live). `deleteDeliveryPartner` rewritten as a soft delete — sets `status='offboarded'` (an enum value that already existed for exactly this, per admin-approval-flow design notes, but had never actually been wired to anything until now), `is_online=false`, `is_approved=false`, `deleted_at=now()` — no row is ever removed, so every order/payout/document referencing it stays intact. New `restoreDeliveryPartner` (`POST /delivery/partners/:id/restore`) undoes it, resetting to `pending_verification` (deliberately not straight back to active — a restored rider must be re-approved). Store side mirrors this as a direct `getAdminClient()` write (matching that page's existing convention, unlike riders' backend-routed pattern) — `handleDeleteStore`/`handleRestoreStore` on `StoresPage.tsx` set/clear `deleted_at` (+ force `is_active`/`is_approved` false on delete). Both pages gained a new "Deleted" stat-filter tab (excluded from all other tabs' counts) and a Delete/Restore icon-button column; both delete/restore actions broadcast to `admin_notifications` via `notifyAdminAction`. Soft-deleted stores/riders are automatically invisible to every customer-facing/rider-facing path with no additional filtering needed, since `is_active`/`is_approved` (stores) and `status`/`is_approved` (riders, via `isRiderVerified()`'s existing `status === 'active'` check) already gate all of those — confirmed live that `delivery_partners_payouts`/`stores`' FK-constrained tables are genuinely untouched by either flow. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend`/`admin`, a full `vite build`, backend vitest 17/17 still passing, and both new columns' presence confirmed live via `information_schema.columns`.
- **"Advanced Filters" button on `ReportsPage.tsx` had no `onClick` at all — a pure dead button.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by removing it — the page's real filter dimension (7/30/90/365-day period selector) already exists at the top of the page, and no other filter concept (store/category-level report filtering) exists anywhere in this feature, so faking an "advanced filters" panel for filters that don't exist would have been worse than removing the dead button. Also removed the now-unused `Filter` icon import. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Appearance settings ("Compact Mode", "Animations", language/timezone/currency selectors) were entirely inert no-ops.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by removing the whole Appearance tab — confirmed via repo-wide grep that zero infrastructure exists anywhere in this codebase for i18n, timezone conversion, or UI theming (this is a single-region INR/IST app with no dark-mode or density-toggle precedent either), so every control in this tab (not just the two flagged) was decorative, silently showing "Saved" while doing nothing. Building real backing for any of it is a much larger scope than a bug fix; removing the deceptive dead controls was the honest, proportionate fix. Removed the `AppearanceTab` component, its `TABS`/render-switch entries, and the now-unused `Palette`/`Globe` icon imports. **Not addressed in this pass, flagged as a follow-up:** the Notifications tab's preference toggles (`newOrders`, `orderStatus`, etc.) may have the identical unread pattern — not traced far enough to confirm. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Order status dropdown allowed arbitrary jumps (e.g. Placed → Delivered, or → Cancelled) with zero confirmation**, unlike every other destructive action in this codebase. Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — both `OrdersPage.tsx`'s `StatusDropdown` and `OrderDetailPage.tsx`'s `handleStatusUpdate` now show a `window.confirm()` specifically when the target status is `delivered` or `cancelled` (the two final, consequence-bearing states payout/refund logic keys off) — ordinary in-progress status corrections stay a single click, matching their low-stakes nature. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Push notification "All Apps" target never actually reached the customer app, despite the UI implying it did.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `PushNotificationPanel`'s "all"/"stores"/"customers" targets are now each independently selectable (previously only "all"/"drivers" existed, with "all" silently only querying `delivery_partners`); customer tokens are fetched directly from `app_users` (unlike stores, `app_users` has no public-facing SELECT policy needing an RPC carve-out — the admin session's existing `admin_full_access` RLS policy already covers it, confirmed via `pg_policies`/`information_schema.column_privileges`). Target-app button grid widened from `grid-cols-3` (2 items, one dangling cell) to `grid-cols-4` (4 items, exact fit). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Dashboard "Recent Orders" and "Top Products" rows weren't individually clickable — only the "View All" link worked.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — Recent Orders rows now `Link` to `/orders/:id`; Top Products' revenue aggregation (`productSales`, built from `order_items` which already carries `product_id` per line) now threads a `productId` through so each row links to `/products/edit/:id` (falling back to the `/products` list for older aggregated entries with no captured id). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **No suspend/ban action existed anywhere for customers — the only entity type without one (Stores/Riders/Admins all have an explicit suspend-equivalent).** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). Unlike the other 5 items in this batch, `app_users` had no status/suspension column or any enforcement infrastructure at all — asked the user whether to build it for real or leave documented; **confirmed: build it.** **Fixed 2026-08-04**: new `app_users.is_suspended BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (migration `20260930000000`, applied live). Enforcement in two places: `customerAuth.middleware.ts`'s `requireCustomer` now 403s on every request once suspended (blocking all API access for an already-logged-in session), and `auth.controller.ts`'s `verifyOTP` login path 403s before even issuing a new session token. Admin UI: `Customer.status`'s existing 'Active'/'Inactive' badge now derives from `is_suspended` (was hardcoded 'Active' always) and is a real toggle button on both `CustomersPage.tsx` (list) and `CustomerDetailPage.tsx` (detail), via a new `setCustomerSuspended()` direct-write helper — mirrors the store/rider online-offline toggle pattern exactly, including a `confirm()` gate on the suspend direction (not reactivate) and an `admin_review_action`-typed `admin_notifications` broadcast so other admins see it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend`/`admin`, a full `vite build`, backend vitest 17/17 still passing, and the new column's presence/default confirmed live via `information_schema.columns`.

### Medium

- **Order-status dropdown bypassed the entire dispatch state machine and skipped customer notification** — wrote directly to `customer_orders.status`, never touched `store_orders` or fired a notification. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by rerouting through the backend's `PATCH /api/orders/:orderId/status` endpoint (which now also has state-machine validation). The separate status-collapse UI limitation (5 exposed states vs. 10 real ones) remains open.
- **No admin panel UI existed to view or set `master_products.gst_rate`** (and, once actually investigated, also `hsn_code`, `hsn_description`, `cgst`, `sgst`, `rating_count`, `brand`) — `ProductsPage`/`AddProductPage`/`EditProductPage` already wrote directly to `master_products` via `adminService.ts`, but `toMasterProduct()`/`updateProduct()` and the forms silently dropped these fields (plus `min_quantity`/`max_quantity` on update specifically), so they could only ever be set by hand in the Supabase table editor. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — all six fields added to the create/update payloads and to both forms, gated to `super_admin`/`admin` roles only (via the existing `hasRole()` helper) even though the underlying RLS `products.edit` permission is looser (also covers `manager`). Two related asks from the same request: (1) a brand-new admin screen for the separate per-store `products` table (which catalog items a given store carries + its own `is_active` toggle) — built via a new backend route (`backend/src/controllers/adminStoreProducts.controller.ts`, `requireAdmin` + a new `store_products.*` permission) rather than a third direct-Supabase-write RLS policy, plus a new `StoreProductsPage.tsx` (store picker → product list → add-from-catalog), reachable via `/stores/products`; this mutates the same rows the shopkeeper app's own inventory flow does, with no coordination between the two actors (last-write-wins, acceptable for v1, noted in code comments). (2) every product create/update/delete/stock-toggle, plus every per-store add/toggle/remove, now logs a row to `admin_notifications` (new `product_updated` type) via a shared `notifyAdminAction()` helper (frontend + backend versions), so these actions now show up on the admin's own notification bell (`AdminHeader.tsx`'s existing 15s poll), which previously never happened for any admin self-action. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build` on the admin panel, plus backend `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing 17/17 vitest suite. **Click-tested live 2026-07-27** against real production data (view-only) — confirmed the new GST/HSN fields render and the catalog/store-inventory pages work as built.
- **Local admin-panel dev couldn't reach any backend at all** (found 2026-07-27, while setting up a live click-test). `admin/vite.config.ts` had no `server.proxy` entry despite `utils/apiBase.ts`'s own comment describing one ("Vite proxies those to the local backend... not to `VITE_API_URL`") — in dev mode with an `https://` `VITE_API_URL` (the deployed Vercel backend, the normal case for local dev so requests don't accidentally hit production), `apiUrl()` builds a same-origin `/api/...` path expecting a proxy that never existed, so every backend call 404'd, including login. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding the missing `/api` → `http://localhost:3000` (or `VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET`) proxy to `vite.config.ts`, matching the already-written comment's intent.
- **ID display was inconsistent and, on `StoresPage`, actually broken.** `ProductsPage`/`CategoriesPage`/`OrdersPage`/`CustomersPage`/`CustomerDetailPage` all showed only an 8-char truncated ID prefix with no way to see or copy the real one; `StoresPage` showed the *full* raw UUID with no `whitespace-nowrap`, so it wrapped across 4-5 lines and inflated every row's height — confirmed via live screenshots against production data. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new shared `admin/src/components/admin/IdCell.tsx` (full ID, single line, small monospace text, click-to-copy icon) used consistently across all five pages; removed the now-dead per-page `truncateId` helpers. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vite build`, and live screenshots before/after on all five pages.
- **`getCustomers`/`getCustomerById` returned all `app_users` rows with no role filter** — `app_users` also holds shopkeepers and delivery partners, and this wasn't just theoretical: confirmed live against the current database that 10 shopkeepers and 2 delivery partners were sitting alongside the 8 real customers with no `.eq('role', 'customer')` anywhere, meaning the admin Customers list/count and the individual customer-detail lookup were both silently including non-customer accounts. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding the role filter to both queries in `adminService.ts`. The other `app_users` queries in the same file (`fetchDashboardData`'s order-customer lookups) are already implicitly scoped — they resolve IDs sourced from `customer_orders.customer_id`, which can only ever reference a genuine customer — so no change needed there. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a direct query against the live database confirming the role split (10 shopkeeper / 2 delivery_partner / 8 customer) that the unfiltered query was including.
- **"Session-creation writes in `authenticateAdmin` are unchecked"** — **found mostly already resolved 2026-07-28.** The finding cited `adminAuthService.ts`'s old direct-Supabase login flow, which has since been fully replaced by the backend's `POST /api/admin/login` (`admin.controller.ts`) — that endpoint already properly checks the `admin_sessions` insert result and 500s if it fails (the frontend now just calls this endpoint and trusts its JSON response). The one write still genuinely unchecked was the trailing `last_login_at` update — fixed by logging its error instead of discarding it silently; left non-blocking since a failed session-row insert already fails the whole login earlier in the same function, so `last_login_at` failing on its own is just a missed audit timestamp, not a broken session.

- **`updateOrderStatus` could never explicitly set `preparing_order`** — the admin status dropdown only exposed 5 coarse frontend states (`placed`/`confirmed`/`shipped`/`delivered`/`cancelled`), and `confirmed` always mapped to `store_accepted`, so an admin overriding order status skipped the real `preparing_order` DB state entirely. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by giving it its own frontend state, `'preparing'`: `adminService.ts`'s `Order['order_status']` type, `mapDbStatusToFrontend`, and the `updateOrderStatus` reverse map all updated; the dropdown option, status badge color/icon, and stat card added to both `OrderDetailPage.tsx` and `OrdersPage.tsx` (which also gained a 7th "Preparing" stat tile), plus the read-only badge switch in `AdminDashboardPage.tsx`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`ready_for_pickup` also collapsed into `'confirmed'`, same root cause as the `preparing_order` bug above.** **Fixed 2026-07-26** in the same pass — added a further `'ready'` frontend state (`adminService.ts` type/mapping/reverse-map, dropdown option "Ready for Pickup", badge color/icon and an 8th "Ready" stat tile in `OrdersPage.tsx`/`OrderDetailPage.tsx`, read-only badge switch in `AdminDashboardPage.tsx`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`delivery_partner_assigned`/`picking_up`/`order_picked_up`/`in_transit` all collapsed into a single `'shipped'` frontend state — and worse, `mapDbStatusToFrontend` never actually handled `picking_up` at all**, so any order genuinely in that DB status silently fell through to the function's `'placed'` default, displaying as freshly-placed instead of in-progress-delivery. **Fixed 2026-07-26**, same pass as the two fixes above — added distinct `'assigned'`/`picking_up`/`picked_up'` frontend states (mirroring the rider app's own `rider_assigned`/`picking_up`/`picked_up` vocabulary in `deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `mapDbStatusToRider`), each with its own dropdown option, badge color, and the icon switch in all three files; `getDashboardStats()`'s separate raw-status `shippedOrders` filter (line ~925) had the identical missing-`picking_up` undercount bug, fixed the same way. To avoid an 11-tile stat row, the "Shipped" stat card stays an aggregate of all four in-delivery states rather than splitting further. Status-badge label rendering (which did a bare `charAt(0).toUpperCase()` on the whole string) was also fixed in all three files so `picking_up`/`picked_up` render as "Picking Up"/"Picked Up" instead of "Picking_up"/"Picked_up". The admin order-status dropdown now round-trips all 10 non-terminal backend states distinctly. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`StoreProfileChangeRequestsPage.tsx`'s own `<h1>` still said generic "Profile Change Requests"** even though the sidebar entry and the rider twin page were both already correctly labeled "Store/Rider Profile Change Requests" — missed during the original rename pass. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — header now reads "Store Profile Change Requests".
- **Neither admin profile-change-request page (store or rider) could show reviewed history** — both only ever fetched `?status=pending` and, on approve/reject, removed the row from view entirely; an admin had no way to see what was approved/rejected in the past, or by whom's decision, even though the backend already stored full history (`reviewed_at`, `rejection_reason`, and the row is never deleted — confirmed both `listProfileChangeRequests` and `listRiderProfileChangeRequests` already supported `?status=all`/`approved`/`rejected`, unused by the frontend). **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding Pending/Approved/Rejected/All tabs to both pages, backed by the same existing endpoint with a different `status` query value; approved/rejected rows now render read-only (status badge, reviewed timestamp, rejection reason if any) instead of the Approve/Reject buttons. No backend or migration changes needed — this was purely a dormant-capability gap on the frontend. Verified via a full admin `vite build` (clean).
- **A shopkeeper/rider whose profile-change request was reviewed had no positive confirmation of the outcome, and could keep using the rest of the app as if nothing happened** — flagged directly by the user as a bug, and correctly so: `app/profile.tsx` in both the store-owner and rider apps only ever showed an inline "pending review" banner that silently disappeared once resolved (quietly refreshing to the new values on approval, or just clearing on rejection with no visible reason shown) — nothing told the user their change was actually approved/rejected, and nothing blocked them from continuing on to other screens without seeing that outcome. **Why this wasn't caught by the earlier deep-dive reviews of this same feature:** those passes (5 bugs found for the store version, 2 for the rider version) were deliberately scoped to backend correctness — atomicity/race conditions in the review/apply path, stale-response guards, React purity — and never asked the product-completeness question of whether the *user* gets told the outcome at all; a screen-local banner silently clearing looks correct from a pure-backend-correctness lens even though it's a real UX gap. **Fixed 2026-07-27** in both apps, without any backend/migration changes — approving or rejecting a request already persists an unread `profile_change_reviewed` notification via `notificationService.notifyProfileChangeReviewed()`/`notifyRiderProfileChangeReviewed()` (pre-existing), so a new `useProfileChangeOutcomeGate()` hook (`lib/useProfileChangeOutcomeGate.ts`, near-identical in both apps) polls the existing `GET /store-owner/notifications` / `GET /delivery-partner/notifications` (`?unreadOnly=true`) endpoint every 20s for that specific notification type and surfaces it via a new blocking `ProfileChangeOutcomeModal` (approved/rejected + rejection reason, if any) mounted once at the app's root layout (`app/_layout.tsx`) — above the whole `Stack`, not inside a single screen — so it appears no matter which tab/screen the user is on when the review happens, not just if they're sitting on Profile. The modal has no backdrop-tap dismiss and a no-op `onRequestClose` (Android back button), so the only way past it is the "Got it" button, which calls `PUT .../notifications/:id/read` (also pre-existing) to acknowledge it — if that call fails, the same outcome simply reappears on the next poll rather than being lost. The existing screen-local banner on `app/profile.tsx` in both apps is untouched and still works as before; this is a separate, independent mechanism layered on top specifically for the "block the rest of the app until acknowledged" requirement. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both apps and `expo lint` on both (23 pre-existing problems on rider, 45 pre-existing on store-owner — both unchanged, no new errors/warnings introduced). Not click-tested live (would need a real admin review action to trigger the notification).
- **`updateCustomerStatus` (`adminService.ts`) was a dead no-op** — took a customer id and target status, logged `"Customers table does not exist. Operation not supported."`, and returned `null` without touching the database. Confirmed zero callers anywhere in the app (no button, no page references it) — a trap for whoever eventually wires up a "suspend customer" feature and gets silent, wordless failure instead of a working update. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting it outright rather than fixing it in place — there's no `customers` table to update in this schema (customer data lives on `app_users`, already correctly handled by `getCustomers`/`getCustomerById` in the same file), so a real implementation would need a different design, not a patch of this function. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full admin `vite build`.
- **`AdminSidebar.tsx`'s Dashboard nav item could never show as active.** `isActive('/')` special-cased the Dashboard's path to check `location.pathname === '/admin'` — but this admin SPA has no `/admin` path anywhere in its own routing; `AdminDashboardPage` is mounted at `/` directly (`AdminRoutes.tsx`, itself mounted at `/*` in `App.tsx`), so the actual `location.pathname` on the dashboard is always `/`, never `/admin`. Leftover from what was likely an earlier routing scheme. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by checking `location.pathname === '/'` instead, matching the app's real routing. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full admin `vite build`; not click-tested live.
- **Admin session lived only in `sessionStorage` — closing the tab silently logged the admin out.** **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a new "Remember me" checkbox on `AdminLoginPage.tsx`; a new shared `services/adminSession.ts` abstracts storage backend selection (localStorage when remembered, sessionStorage otherwise) and every one of the ~20 direct `sessionStorage.getItem/removeItem('adminToken'/'adminData'/'adminTokenExpiry')` call sites across the app (`secureAdminAuth.ts`, `adminAuthService.ts`, `services/supabase.ts`, `adminService.ts`, `AdminHeader.tsx`/`AdminSidebar.tsx` logout handlers, and 8 page-level token reads) now goes through it. Also folded in the related Low item below (raw `JSON.parse` on `adminData` in 3 pages) as the same root cause. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full admin `vite build`.
- **`SettingsPage`'s notification/appearance prefs were localStorage-only, didn't follow the admin across devices.** **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new `notification_preferences`/`display_preferences` jsonb columns on `admins` (migration `20260914000000`, applied live), read from/written to via the existing `PATCH /api/admin/:id` endpoint (`updateAdmin` on both frontend and backend) instead of `localStorage`.
