Backend's self-service carve-out (previously password-only) widened to also allow either preferences field for one's own record, any role — these are per-admin settings, not privileges. `NotificationsTab`/`AppearanceTab` now initialize from `getCurrentAdmin()?.notification_preferences`/`display_preferences` (merged over hardcoded defaults) and update the stored session object in place after a successful save via a new `updateStoredAdminData()` helper. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean (both admin and backend), full admin `vite build`, and backend's existing 17/17 vitest suite; confirmed live that the new columns exist via a direct schema query.
- **Only `refund_required` notifications showed inline detail in `NotificationsPage.tsx`'s list; the store/rider verification-document types and `product_updated` showed nothing extra.** **Fixed 2026-07-27** — added detail badges for `document_uploaded`/`document_removed`/`rider_document_uploaded`/`rider_document_removed` (humanized `doc_type`), `verification_submitted`/`rider_verification_submitted` ("ready for review"), and `product_updated` (`product_name` or `product_id`, since that payload's shape varies by which action fired it — some call sites send `product_id`, one sends `master_product_id` only). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full admin `vite build`.
- **`StoresPage.tsx`'s "Updated On" column and its default sort only ever reflected verification-document activity — a shopkeeper changing storefront photos or submitting a profile change request left the column frozen at whatever the last document edit was (or `—` if none had ever happened), even though real activity had just occurred.** Found 2026-07-29, flagged directly by the user ("the updated_on flag is not properly working"). `refreshDocsUpdatedAt()` (`StoresPage.tsx:397`, previously) built its `latest` map from `store_verification_documents.updated_at` only — no query against `store_images` or `store_profile_change_requests` at all, and the Realtime subscription (`StoresPage.tsx:501`) only listened for changes on `store_verification_documents`/`stores`. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by renaming the function to `refreshLastActivityAt()` and having it separately query `store_images` (`created_at`) and `store_profile_change_requests` (`created_at`/`reviewed_at`) in addition to the existing documents query, merging all three into the same per-store `latest` timestamp map (each query wrapped in its own try/catch so one source failing doesn't blank the others); the Realtime subscription now also listens for `*` events on both `store_images` and `store_profile_change_requests`. `DeliveryPage.tsx`'s equivalent rider-side column was **not** touched — confirmed its `docsUpdatedAt` is built entirely from `delivery_partner_verification_documents`, which already includes rider vehicle photos (`vehicle_photo_front/side/rear` are rows in that same table, not a separate gallery), so no analogous gap exists there. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the admin panel; not click-tested live.
- **Product grid-view stock-toggle button never got its color — a non-template-literal string contained a literal `${...}`.** `ProductsPage.tsx`'s `className` on the grid-view "Toggle" button was a plain double-quoted JSX string (not a template literal), so `${product.in_stock ? '...' : '...'}` was never evaluated and passed through as invalid, no-op CSS class text — the button rendered with no in-stock/out-of-stock styling, unlike the correctly-implemented list view (`ProductRow`) and the correctly-implemented badge on the same card (a real template literal). Confirmed the only occurrence of this exact pattern anywhere in `admin/src` via grep. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by wrapping the string in backticks — a one-line change, no other text needed to differ. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, a targeted `eslint` pass on the file (only a pre-existing, unrelated `prefer-const` finding at a different line), and a full `vite build`.
- **The document-review modal didn't refresh the data the Approve-button readiness gate reads from — could show a stale "not ready" reason for up to 20s after approving the last document in the same session.** `DocumentReviewModal`'s (`StoresPage.tsx`) and `DeliveryDocumentReviewModal`'s `onDocumentUpdated` callback only ever passed `(id, updatedAt)` back to the parent page, which used it to bump `docsUpdatedAt` — never `docStatusByStore`/`docStatusByPartner`, the map `approvalReadiness()` actually reads to decide whether Approve is enabled. **Failure scenario:** admin opens "Review Documents," approves the last outstanding document, closes the modal — the row's Approve button could stay disabled with a stale "Not yet approved: X" tooltip until the next 20s background poll (`refreshAll`) or a Realtime event (the code's own comment flagged as unconfirmed to actually fire for this client) caught up. Directly undermined the same-day approval-readiness gate fix (documented above, Critical), which never wired the modal's callback to keep its own gating data fresh. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by widening `onDocumentUpdated`'s signature to `(id, updatedAt, docType, status)` in both modals — both already had `docType` and the review outcome in scope at the exact call site inside `review()`, so this was purely additive, no new fetch needed (using the server-echoed `json.document.status` when present, falling back to the locally-known `status` param). Both parent pages' callbacks now also do a `setDocStatusByStore`/`setDocStatusByPartner` functional update that patches the matching `doc_type` entry for that store/partner (or appends it if it wasn't present yet, matching the shape the bulk-refresh path already builds) alongside the existing `docsUpdatedAt` bump. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, a targeted `eslint` pass on all 4 changed files (only pre-existing warnings, none new), and a full `vite build`.
- **Four image-upload paths never notified admins at all, unlike every other verification-related action in the app.** Found 2026-07-29, directly asked by the user to check ("check whether the images... when they are uploaded and saved they should be in the admin notifications as well"). Confirmed via code read: `updateStore` (store owner's own `owner_image_url`), `addStoreImage`/`deleteStoreImage` (storefront gallery), and the rider's `updateProfileImage`/`updatePhotoUrls` (`profile_image_url` and the separate single `vehicle_image_url` field — distinct from the 3 `vehicle_photo_front/side/rear` verification-document rows, which already notify via the existing `rider_document_uploaded` path) all silently wrote to the DB with zero `notifyAdmins`/`notifyAdminsOfRiderDocs` call. **Fixed 2026-07-29**, notification-only (deliberately not a suspend/change-request gate — matches the earlier product decision that owner/profile photos stay ungated, and keeps store/rider vehicle-gallery photos at "admin is informed" rather than the harsher document-edit treatment, pending the separate open decision on a full approval gate above): added a `getStoreName(storeId)`/`getRiderName(riderId)` helper to each controller (name-only, no suspend side effect, unlike `suspendStoreIfApprovedAndGetName`/`suspendRiderIfApprovedAndGetName`) and wired in 5 new notification types — `owner_photo_updated`, `store_image_added`, `store_image_removed` (`storeOwner.controller.ts`), `rider_profile_photo_updated`, `rider_vehicle_photo_updated` (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`). Admin panel updated to match: `utils/notificationLink.ts` routes all 5 to `/stores` or `/delivery`; `NotificationsPage.tsx` and `AdminHeader.tsx` both got matching icon-map entries (a new `Image` lucide icon, indigo for additions/updates, gray for removal — same color convention as the existing document types) instead of falling through to the generic default icon. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17) on the backend, and `tsc --noEmit` clean plus a full `vite build` on the admin panel; not click-tested live.
- **Store storefront photos had zero admin-facing gate on edit-after-approval, and admin couldn't even see them during the original review — the gap the notification-only fix above deliberately left open.** `store_images` had no `status` column and no endpoint anywhere returned it to the admin panel (confirmed via grep — the only admin-side reference was a direct, silently-failing `getAdminClient()` query in `StoresPage.tsx`'s "Updated On" column, since the table had zero RLS policy or anon grant since the day it was created — a second, adjacent bug found and fixed in the same pass). **Fixed 2026-07-30**, going with the lighter of the two directions the audit itself proposed (a `status` column on `store_images`, not a separate `store_image_change_requests` queue table) — confirmed first via repo-wide grep that `store_images`/`stores.image_url` has zero customer-facing reader anywhere in the monorepo today, so this is an **admin-oversight gate, not a customer-visibility gate**: new uploads are reviewable, but nothing is hidden from a customer view that doesn't exist yet. Migration `20260926000000` adds `status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'approved'` (existing photos aren't retroactively flagged — only new uploads from now on start `'pending'`) plus `rejection_reason`/`reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`, mirroring `store_verification_documents`'s exact shape; also adds the same `admin_full_access` RLS policy + `anon` SELECT grant `store_verification_documents` already has, fixing the adjacent silent-failure bug for free. `storeOwner.controller.ts`'s `addStoreImage` now inserts new rows with `status: 'pending'` (deliberately **not** the harsh `suspendStoreIfApprovedAndGetName()` pattern — matches the audit's own "no need to fully suspend a store over a photo swap" framing); `deleteStoreImage` is untouched, since removing content isn't a review-worthy risk. New backend endpoints `GET/PATCH /api/admin/stores/:id/images` (`adminStores.controller.ts`'s `getStoreImages`/`reviewStoreImage`, mirroring `getStoreVerificationDocuments`/`reviewStoreVerificationDocument` exactly, reusing the existing `store_verification.view`/`.edit` permissions rather than inventing new ones) fire the same `notifyAdminsOfReviewAction()` used by every other review category (widened its `category` union type to add `'store_image'`) — which the admin panel's `admin_review_action` notification type/icon/routing already handles generically, no admin-panel notification-map changes needed. Admin UI: extended the existing `DocumentReviewModal` (`StoresPage.tsx`) with a "Storefront Photos" section below the documents list — same card/Approve/Reject/rejection-reason-textarea pattern as documents, no new page or tab needed since the modal's shell was already generic. `onDocumentUpdated`'s signature was widened (in this same modal, for both the document and image review flows) from `(id, updatedAt)` to `(id, updatedAt, docType, status)`, closing the "document-review modal doesn't refresh readiness data" bug (documented above, fixed same day) for images too — `docStatusByStore` gets a harmless `doc_type: 'store_image'` entry that `approvalReadiness()`'s `DOC_LABELS`-keyed check simply ignores, since image review was deliberately kept independent of the store-approval gate. Migration applied to the live database and verified: all 4 new columns, the RLS policy, and both grants confirmed present via direct queries. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing vitest suite (17/17) on the backend, `tsc --noEmit` clean, a targeted `eslint` pass (only pre-existing warnings), and a full `vite build` on the admin panel. Not click-tested live. **Deliberately out of scope:** no shopkeeper-facing UI was built to show a rejected-photo reason or a "pending review" badge in the store-owner app's own gallery — the gate is admin-side oversight only for now, a natural follow-up once/if `store_images` ever gains a real customer-facing consumer.

### Low

- **Paired-number lookup for Aadhaar/PAN back-side documents used a non-anchored `.replace('_back', '_front')`.** **Fixed 2026-07-18**, switched to an anchored regex.
- **Stale "5 verification documents" doc-comment.** **Fixed 2026-07-18** (`StoresPage.tsx:388`).
- **`navigator.userAgent` called unguarded in `auditLog.ts`** — `services/auditLog.ts:37,62,78` all called it directly with no environment guard; if ever run outside a browser context, would throw and silently drop the audit-log write (caught by an outer try/catch, but the security event is lost). **Fixed 2026-07-26** with a small `getUserAgent()` helper (`typeof navigator !== 'undefined'` check) used at all 3 call sites. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Verbose console logging on every admin login attempt, unconditional in production** — `AdminLoginPage.tsx` logged the plaintext email at every step (🔐/📡/📥/✅ emoji-prefixed step traces) plus the full error object (`message`/`stack`/`name`) on any failure; `adminAuthService.ts`'s `authenticateAdmin` logged the email and role on every successful login too. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by removing the step-tracing logs entirely (they added nothing beyond what the existing `logAdminAction`/`logSecurityEvent` audit-trail writes already persist to the DB) and gating the remaining `console.error` calls behind `import.meta.env.DEV`, the established convention already used elsewhere in this app (`utils/apiBase.ts`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`services/supabase.ts` inside the admin app contained ~1250 lines of dead customer-facing logic** — `getAllProducts`/`getProductsByCategory`/`searchProducts`, `loginWithOTP`/`verifyOTP`/`getCurrentUser`/`logout`, `createOrder`/`getUserOrders`, `subscribeToNewsletter`, and 5 address-CRUD functions, plus all their private helpers — a stale copy-paste from the customer-facing website's own `supabase.ts`, none of it relevant to an admin panel. Confirmed via repo-wide grep that every one of these exports has zero real importers anywhere in the admin app (one apparent `logout` hit turned out to be an unrelated comment/string in `secureAdminAuth.ts`, not an import). The one export that *is* genuinely used, `Product` (imported by `ReportsPage.tsx` as a convenient type shape), was kept. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by trimming the file from 1345 lines to 90 — keeping only the Supabase client setup, `getAdminClient()`, and the `Product` interface; also dropped the 4 now-fully-unused imports (`geocodeAddress`, `parseGstRatePercent`/`priceWithGst`, `shouldUseBackendApi`) and the `readApiErrorMessage` helper (only ever called from the deleted code). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build` (bundle size roughly unchanged — Rollup had likely already tree-shaken the unreferenced exports — but the source file itself is no longer a maintenance/confusion trap).
- **Client-side-only rate limiting on admin login provided no real protection** — `utils/rateLimit.ts`'s in-memory `Map` was trivially bypassed by a page refresh. Investigating turned up that real, working protection already exists server-side (`admin.routes.ts`'s `express-rate-limit` instance, 10 attempts/15min keyed by email) — so the actual fix wasn't building new infrastructure, it was removing the misleading client-side layer sitting on top of it and a companion bug that layer was masking: `authenticateAdmin` collapsed every non-2xx response (wrong password *and* an actual 429 rate-limit block) into a generic `null` → hardcoded "Invalid email or password," meaning a real rate-limited admin would be told they had the wrong password. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by having `authenticateAdmin` throw with the server's real error message on any non-2xx response (network-level failures still return `null`, now specifically meaning "couldn't reach the server," with `AdminLoginPage.tsx`'s message updated to match) and deleting the client-side `checkRateLimit('ADMIN_LOGIN', ...)` gate entirely from the login page. `utils/rateLimit.ts` itself had zero other importers once that call site was removed, so deleted the whole file rather than leaving fully-dead code behind. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build`; confirmed via grep zero remaining references to `rateLimit`/`checkRateLimit`/`RATE_LIMITS` anywhere in the admin app.
- **No catch-all/404 route.** `App.tsx` mounts `<Route path="/*" element={<AdminRoutes />} />`, and `AdminRoutes.tsx` had no `<Route path="*">` fallback inside its own `<Routes>` — an unmatched path (typo, stale bookmark, removed route) rendered nothing at all, not even the `AdminLayout` shell. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a trailing `<Route path="*" element={<Navigate to="/" replace />} />` — redirects to the dashboard, which `AdminAuthGuard` already correctly bounces to `/login` if the admin isn't authenticated, so an unmatched path for a logged-out visitor still ends up at login, not a dead end either way. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and a full `vite build`.
- **Notification-bell mark-as-read (header dropdown) was fire-and-forget, unlike the already-fixed `NotificationsPage.tsx` equivalent.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `AdminHeader.tsx`'s `handleNotifClick` now awaits the mark-read RPC and only updates local "read" state on success (matching `NotificationsPage.tsx`'s `markOneRead`), instead of a `void`'d call with unconditional local-state update. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Login page didn't redirect an already-authenticated admin away from `/login`.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `AdminLoginPage.tsx` now checks `isAdminAuthenticated()` on mount and redirects to `/` if already logged in, in addition to the existing post-submit redirect. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Quick Add Product modal kept stale typed data after Cancel.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `QuickAddModal`'s `formData` now resets on every `isOpen` transition to `true`, not just after a successful submit, so it doesn't matter whether the previous attempt ended via Cancel, the X button, or a backdrop click. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.

## Supabase schema & migrations

### Critical

- **`supabase/migrations/` couldn't bootstrap the schema from an empty database — systemic, not isolated.** Live-DB access confirmed 39 of 48 tables, 6 of 9 enum types, and 3 trigger functions had no tracked `CREATE` anywhere. **Fixed 2026-07-24** with a new idempotent baseline migration (`20260813000000`) sourced directly from a live `pg_dump`, verified by replaying it into a disposable schema. **Caveat:** the ~106 `ADD CONSTRAINT` statements inside it are *not* idempotent — safe against a genuinely empty DB, but will error if re-run against a DB that already has these tables.
- **`payment_method_type` and `order_status` enums had no `CREATE TYPE` anywhere in migration history.** **Fixed 2026-07-24** as part of the same baseline migration above.
- **`app_users` had a live, currently-exploitable RLS hole: full anonymous read/write access, including `session_token`.** Found 2026-07-27 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup — held at the time pending explicit authorization to touch production RLS). **Fixed 2026-07-29**, on explicit request, mirroring the identical `stores` fix from 2026-07-28: `ALTER POLICY "Allow all for service role" ON public.app_users TO service_role;` (migration `20260925000000`). A full repo grep found only two direct `.from('app_users')` call sites outside the backend/admin panel — `authService.ts`'s `updateCustomerProfile` (a write, fixed in the same pass below) and `supabase.ts`'s phone-lookup fallback in `getUserAddresses` (a read, but dead code in this deployment since `VITE_API_URL` is always set, making `shouldUseBackendApi()` true) — and confirmed none of the three mobile apps touch `app_users` directly at all. One real regression found and fixed in the same migration before applying: `fill_product_store_info` (a trigger on `products`, populating denormalized `name`/`phone` via a `stores`/`app_users` join, fired by the shopkeeper app's anon-key "add product to store" flow) was not `SECURITY DEFINER` — it currently only worked because of this same hole, and would have started silently returning `NULL` phone on every new row once closed. Fixed by making the trigger function `SECURITY DEFINER` (matching this codebase's established pattern for this exact class of cross-table read). Verified live: anon-key `curl` with no auth header at all now returns `[]` for a real customer read (previously returned name/email/session_token), and a `PATCH` attempt with `Prefer: return=representation` returns `[]`/0 rows affected (previously silently succeeded) — confirmed the target row's `name` was genuinely unchanged in the DB. Also verified the two things this fix could plausibly have broken still work: a real customer profile update via the new `PATCH /api/customers/me` path (below) round-tripped correctly, and a real shopkeeper anon-key insert into `products` still correctly populated `name`/`phone` via the now-`SECURITY DEFINER` trigger. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `frontend` and `backend`; existing test suites 26/26 (frontend) and 17/17 (backend) still passing.
- **Direct consequence of the same hole: the website's Profile "Save Changes" write path (`services/authService.ts`'s `updateCustomerProfile`) wrote directly to `app_users`/`customers` from the browser via the anon-key client, silently broken for address fields and only "succeeding" for name/email because of the RLS hole above, not by design** (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-07-29**, in the same pass as the RLS fix above (the two were entangled — fixing the RLS hole first would have made the name/email half start silently failing too, exactly like the address half already did): rewrote `updateCustomerProfile` to call the backend's existing `PATCH /api/customers/me` (`requireCustomer`-gated) via the shared `authedFetch`/`getAuthHeaders()` helpers, instead of writing to either table directly. Dropped `email` from the accepted fields — confirmed dead in practice via a full call-site trace (the only real caller, `ProfilePage.tsx`, only ever passes `{name}`; email changes already go through a separate, already-correct OTP-verified `changeCustomerEmail`/`verifyCustomerEmailCode` flow) rather than extending the backend endpoint to support a field nothing actually sends. Removed the now-unused `supabase`/`supabaseAdmin` imports from `authService.ts` (the latter is a same-file-misnamed anon-key client, not an actual service-role client — confirmed no real service-role key is exposed client-side anywhere in this file). Verified live with a real customer session: the new endpoint round-trips a real name change correctly (reverted after verifying). `tsc --noEmit` clean; frontend test suite 26/26 still passing.

### High

- **Most `SECURITY DEFINER` functions were still missing `SET search_path = public`**, including `is_admin_authenticated()` (the sole gate for 9 tables' RLS). **Fixed 2026-07-24**, applied to the live DB the same day (migration `20260815000000`).
- **`admin_role` enum only had 2 real values (`super_admin`, `admin`) in the live DB**, though the app's own logic already treated `manager`/`viewer` as real roles. **Fixed 2026-07-24** by widening the enum to match the already-built app-side permission logic; applied to the live DB.
- **`debug_admin_headers()`, a temporary diagnostic function, was never dropped** — `SECURITY DEFINER`, no `search_path` pin, granted to `anon`/`authenticated`, returning full `admin_sessions` rows including the session token. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by dropping it (confirmed zero callers), applied to the live DB.
- **`ensure_stores_near_location(lat, lng)` — a live, untracked, anon-callable `SECURITY DEFINER` function — auto-generates 6 fake "Near & Now Store #N" rows (plus a synthetic shopkeeper `app_users` row) stocked with 100 units of every active `master_product`, whenever fewer than 5 real stores exist within 3km of a given point.** Originally logged as a Low "untracked function, unknown behavior" item (found 2026-08-04, see Open Issues history above); upgraded to High once `pg_get_functiondef` revealed the actual body — clearly leftover demo/seed-data logic (synthetic owner email `storeowner@nearandnow.local`), confirmed via repo-wide grep to have zero call sites in `backend/src` or `frontend/src`, yet still live and callable by anyone holding the public anon key with no auth at all. Contained today only by `stores.is_approved` defaulting to `false` (the function never sets it, so its fake stores fail the approval filter added in `20260927000000`) — but it could still be used to spam production `stores`/`app_users`/`products` with garbage rows for free. **Fixed 2026-08-04**, per explicit user confirmation to revoke rather than drop, with migration `20260929000000_revoke_anon_ensure_stores_near_location.sql`. Hit the same Postgres gotcha this doc has seen before with column-privileges (`stores.expo_push_token`, 2026-07-29): `REVOKE ... FROM anon, authenticated` alone was a no-op, because Postgres grants `EXECUTE` to `PUBLIC` by default on function creation and every role inherits `PUBLIC`'s privileges regardless of a per-role revoke — confirmed live via `pg_proc.proacl` that this function (and, as a residual gap from the *2026-07-23 High* `finalize_order_if_ready`/`debug_admin_headers` fix above, those functions too) still carried an explicit `PUBLIC` grant. Added an explicit `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC` before the `anon, authenticated` revoke to actually close it. Verified live with a real anon-key `curl` RPC call: `42501 permission denied for function ensure_stores_near_location` (previously would have executed). `service_role`/`postgres` grants untouched, confirmed via `proacl`. **Not addressed in this pass:** the same latent `PUBLIC`-grant gap likely still applies to `finalize_order_if_ready`/`mark_verification_submitted_if_ready`/`mark_rider_verification_submitted_if_ready` from the 2026-07-23 fix and `debug_admin_headers` (already dropped, moot) — flagging for a follow-up pass rather than expanding scope here.

### Medium

- **No DB-level enforcement forced `stores.is_active` back to `false` when admin revoked `stores.is_approved` on an already-online store** — the rider side already had the equivalent trigger for `delivery_partners.is_online`/`status` (`20260428000001_decouple_is_online_from_status.sql`); the store side had no matching one, so a revoked store stayed online until the shopkeeper (or some future toggle) happened to touch the row. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (migration `20260828000001`) forcing `is_active := false` whenever `is_approved` is not true, mirroring the rider trigger's semantics exactly (does not force `is_active` true on approval, so the shopkeeper's own online/offline toggle still applies once re-approved). Applied live and verified end-to-end against a real store — revoked approval, confirmed `is_active` flipped in the same operation, then restored the store's original state.

### Low / informational

- **Same anon-RPC-no-caller-identity-check pattern also applied to `mark_verification_submitted_if_ready`/`mark_rider_verification_submitted_if_ready`.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** by the same revoke-execute migration as `debug_admin_headers()`/`finalize_order_if_ready`, applied to the live DB.
- **`stores.public_read` (`USING (true)`) exposed every column on the row, including `expo_push_token`** — anyone holding the app's anon key could read any store's push token and send arbitrary spoofed push notifications to that shopkeeper's device, since Expo's push-send API doesn't authenticate the sender. **Fixed 2026-07-29** (migration `20260830000000`). RLS can't do column-level restriction (it's row-level only), so the real fix is Postgres column privileges: `REVOKE SELECT ON stores FROM anon, authenticated` + `GRANT SELECT` back on every column *except* `expo_push_token`. Hit a genuine Postgres gotcha getting there — a naive `REVOKE SELECT (expo_push_token) ... FROM anon` ran with no error but had **zero effect**, because column-level and table-level privileges are tracked separately and a pre-existing table-level `GRANT SELECT` overrides any column-level revoke; only caught this by testing against the live anon-key REST API directly rather than trusting the migration's clean exit. New `admin_get_store_push_tokens()` `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC (gated by `is_admin_authenticated()`, mirroring the existing pattern) replaces the admin panel's direct column read for its broadcast-push feature (`NotificationsPage.tsx`). Two other call sites needed a `select('*')` → explicit-column-list change to keep working now that the column isn't blanket-readable (`StoresPage.tsx`'s store list, backend's `getNearbyStores`) — neither used `expo_push_token`, confirmed via search first. Verified live against the real anon-key REST API: direct `expo_push_token` select and `select=*` both now correctly 401 with "permission denied for table stores," the explicit safe-column list still returns real data, and the new RPC correctly rejects a call with no admin session header ("Not authorized").
- **`get_nearby_store_ids` Postgres function existed live with no corresponding migration file anywhere in the repo.** Found 2026-07-31 (see Open Issues history — was open as of 2026-07-30). **Fixed 2026-08-03** by commit `64cf747` ("Require store approval for customer-facing product and nearby-store visibility", author Rounak Jana): `supabase/migrations/20260927000000_get_nearby_store_ids_require_approved.sql` retroactively tracks the function and additionally hardens it to require `is_approved = true` (previously only `is_active`), closing the drift and a real visibility gap in the same migration. Reviewed 2026-08-04 and confirmed correct: matches the codebase's established `!inner` + dotted-`.eq()` PostgREST join pattern used elsewhere, `SECURITY DEFINER` with a safe `SET search_path = public`, and the fix is complete for the website (all customer-facing product read paths funnel through the single patched `fetchProductRows`). **Residual gap surfaced by this same review, now also closed:** the migration's own call to `haversine_km` was itself untracked. **Fixed 2026-08-04** with a new migration (`20260926000000_haversine_km.sql`, sequenced before `20260927000000` by timestamp), its `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` body pulled verbatim from the live DB via `pg_get_functiondef` to guarantee an exact match, applied live via `supabase db query --linked` and confirmed idempotent against the already-existing live function. The original performance concern (full scan + per-row haversine, no bounding-box/spatial-index pre-filter) is unchanged, deliberately out of scope for this fix. This also closed two related gaps the audited commit itself didn't cover: `backend/src/controllers/orders.controller.ts`'s legacy `createOrder` and the customer mobile app's `getProductById`/`getAllProducts` — see Backend → High and Customer app → High/Medium, below, both fixed the same day.

## Store-owner app (`near-now-store_owner`)

### Critical

- **Push notification service was never initialized at app startup** — notifications were effectively opt-in-and-hidden despite in-app copy implying they were on by default. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by adding a fire-and-forget caller to `home.tsx`'s session-bootstrap effect.
- **No global 401 handling** — an expired session left screens showing empty data indefinitely with no path back to login. **Fixed 2026-07-24**, mirroring the rider app's existing pattern (clear session, log via the previously-dead `handleAuthError()`, redirect to `/landing`).
- **Roughly half the app's network calls used raw `fetch()` directly instead of `apiClient`, bypassing the 401 handling fixed directly above.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by migrating every remaining raw `fetch()` call site to `apiClient` (`app/notification-inbox.tsx`'s three notification calls now use `apiClient.get`/`apiClient.put`; `lib/appCache.ts`'s `forceFetchStores` now uses `apiClient.request` directly, preserving its existing 15s timeout/no-retry behavior; `lib/storeApproval.ts`'s `refreshStoreApproval` now uses `apiClient.get`; `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s approval poll, active-order-count fetch, and both online/offline toggle calls now use `apiClient.get`/`apiClient.patch`) — removing the now-dead `API_BASE`/`config` import from `home.tsx` in the same pass. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same pre-existing baseline, nothing new) on the store-owner app.
- **Auth token stored unencrypted in AsyncStorage for up to 30 days.** **Fixed 2026-07-23** — the shopkeeper-app twin of the customer-app fix of the same shape; token moved to `expo-secure-store` with a one-time legacy migration so existing sessions survive the update.
- **"Send a message" in Help & Support was entirely fake — never actually sent anything.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). `app/help.tsx`'s `handleSendMessage` was a `setTimeout` with no network call at all, the code's own comment admitting it ("Simulate send — in production this would hit an API"). **Fixed 2026-08-04** with a real, persisted, admin-visible flow: new `support_messages` table (migration `20260930020000`, applied live, generic across `sender_role` so the rider/customer apps' equivalent forms can reuse it later without a schema change), a new backend endpoint (`POST /store-owner/support-messages`, `resolveShopkeeperFromToken`-gated) that inserts a row and broadcasts to `admin_notifications` — the same channel already used for approve/reject/status actions, so it shows up on admins' existing bell/`NotificationsPage` in real time with no new polling mechanism. New `support_message` type added (icon: `MessageCircle`, blue) to `NotificationsPage.tsx`'s type union/`TYPE_META` and `AdminHeader.tsx`'s `notifStyle()` (no click-through route yet — no dedicated support-message detail page exists). `help.tsx` now calls the real endpoint via `apiClient.post` with the session token, with the existing Call/WhatsApp/Email options surfaced as a fallback in the failure alert. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend`/store-owner app, a full `vite build` on `admin`, backend vitest 17/17 and store-owner Jest 21/21 both still passing, and the new table's presence confirmed live via `information_schema.tables`.

### High

- **`useStoreApprovalGate` was poll-only at 30s across every screen using it, no Realtime**, despite `home.tsx` having its own separate realtime subscription for its own local state only. **Fixed 2026-07-25** by adding a plain filtered `postgres_changes` subscription (no auth-bridge needed, since `stores.public_read` is intentionally public); 30s poll retained as fallback.
- **Both approval-gate hooks failed open on any transient network error, even on the very first, never-yet-confirmed check** — a shopkeeper who had never actually been verified could get past the gate simply because a request happened to fail. Separately, `app/inventory.tsx` used only the weaker reactive `useRequireStoreApproval()` (redirect fires after an async round-trip, content renders first) despite being deep-link-reachable outside the tab navigator's blocking gate. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — both `useStoreApprovalGate.ts` and `useRequireStoreApproval.ts` now track whether approval has ever actually been confirmed (`hasConfirmedApprovedRef`); an error before that first confirmation redirects to `/pending-verification` (fail closed), while an error *after* a real confirmation still fails open (so a flaky connection doesn't kick an already-verified shopkeeper out mid-session). `app/inventory.tsx` migrated from `useRequireStoreApproval()` to the same blocking `useStoreApprovalGate("require-approved")` pattern already used by the tabs layout, so it can no longer render its content before the approval check resolves. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean) and the existing Jest suite (21/21, unaffected).
- **Home tab locking, resolved by product decision.** Found 2026-08-04: `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`'s `useStoreApprovalGate("require-approved")` wraps the whole `<Tabs>` including Home, and `home.tsx` independently redirects to `/pending-verification` too — both agree today, but this contradicts an earlier documented model ("only Home is accessible while pending"). **Resolved 2026-08-04, no code change** — user explicitly confirmed the current behavior (Home locked pre-approval, same as every other tab) is what's actually wanted now; the older "Home stays open" note is superseded. The two-mechanism redundancy is harmless (both agree) and was left as-is.
- **Push token was never cleared on logout or re-associated on a different user's login** — on a shared device, a previous shopkeeper could keep receiving order pushes after logging out. **Fixed 2026-07-24** on both backend (`registerPushToken` now accepts an explicit `null` to clear) and client (`unregister()` called from all 3 real logout call sites).
- **`clearSession()` didn't clear the store-list cache** — a real cross-account data-leak window on shared devices (shopkeeper B could briefly see shopkeeper A's store data). **Fixed 2026-07-24** with a one-line `clearStoreCache()` addition to `clearSession()`.
- **The "Store Verified → Continue" alert fix was incomplete** — silently bypassed on the ordinary cold-launch-after-approval path because `useStoreApprovalGate("require-pending")` redirected on its own instead of letting the alert-driven screen handle it. **Fixed 2026-07-18** by making `checkApprovalNow(true)` fire immediately on mount, not just on the poll interval.
- **OTP autofill silently truncated the code to 1 digit if focus wasn't on the first box when the code arrived**, because only box 0 had `maxLength={6}`. **Fixed 2026-07-17** by removing the `maxLength` restriction (matching the rider app's already-correct setup) and adding `selectTextOnFocus`.
- **Android hardware back button unresponsive with a modal open, and auto-rotate not actually disabled** despite config saying portrait-only. **Fixed 2026-07-16** (Jira SCRUM-3) — added `onRequestClose` to both modals, and corrected a stale `AndroidManifest.xml` override that was winning over the JS-level config at runtime. **Not yet built into an APK for on-device QA** at the time of this fix — Jira ticket left in In Review, not Done.
- **Document preview silently vanished immediately after a successful save** (Upload Documents screen) — `saveOne` replaced the local document object with the raw save-endpoint response, which has no signed `url`. **Fixed 2026-07-17** by merging the response into the existing object instead of replacing it; also fixed a related corrupted "remaining documents" completion message caused by the same bug.
- **The store-suspension redirect (on editing an already-approved document) was silently undone by client-side caching** — `appCache.ts`'s 10-minute store-list cache wasn't invalidated, so the approval-gate screen re-read the stale, still-approved cached record and bounced the shopkeeper straight back in. **Fixed 2026-07-17** by calling `clearStoreCache()` before navigating. Not yet verified on-device at the time of this fix.
- **The "one-time locked" owner verification photo could be freely re-uploaded from the Profile screen — no lock check at all.** Found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit. `app/store-owner-signup.tsx`/`app/billing-info.tsx` both deliberately treat the owner's KYC photo as permanently read-only once set, but `app/profile.tsx`'s `pickOwnerImage` had no equivalent guard — gated only by the screen's generic `editing` toggle. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — added the same `if (ownerImageUri) return` guard to `profile.tsx`'s `pickOwnerImage`, and updated the avatar `TouchableOpacity` (`disabled`/`activeOpacity`) plus hid the camera-badge edit affordance once a photo is already set, matching `billing-info.tsx`'s locked-state UI. Also fixed `profile.tsx` redeclaring its own local `OWNER_IMAGE_KEY` constant instead of importing the shared one from `lib/storage.ts` (same string value today, so harmless in practice, but a drift risk the shared-key comment in `lib/storage.ts` specifically warns about) — now imports it like the other two screens. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Owner-photo cache was never cleared on logout and was trusted over the server record — leaked across accounts on a shared device.** Found 2026-07-28, same audit. `OWNER_IMAGE_KEY` was written on every upload but `clearSession()` only cleared session/inventory/store-list caches, never this key (`guardFreshInstall()` had the same gap); separately, neither `profile.tsx`'s `hydrate()` nor `store-owner-signup.tsx`'s `loadStore()` ever reconciled the displayed photo against the server's own `stores.owner_image_url`, trusting the AsyncStorage cache indefinitely instead. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — `OWNER_IMAGE_KEY` added to both `clearSession()`'s and `guardFreshInstall()`'s `AsyncStorage.multiRemove` lists (`session.ts`); `profile.tsx`'s `hydrate()` and `store-owner-signup.tsx`'s `loadStore()` now both set `ownerImageUri` from the freshly-fetched store record on every call (writing it back to `OWNER_IMAGE_KEY` to keep the two screens in sync, or clearing the key if the store has no photo), rather than relying solely on the one-time AsyncStorage read at mount. `CachedStore` (`lib/appCache.ts`) gained an `owner_image_url?: string | null` field so `store-owner-signup.tsx` could read it with proper typing instead of an `any` cast. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Regression from the fix above, found the same day during a full regression review: `hydrate()`'s new owner-image reconciliation could revert a freshly-uploaded photo if a background store refresh resolved after the upload.** `profile.tsx`'s mount effect fires `forceFetchStores(...).then(hydrate)` without awaiting it — a fire-and-forget background refresh (up to 15s). If a shopkeeper picked and uploaded an owner photo while that refresh was still in flight, and the refresh's response (captured before the upload/patch landed) resolved after the upload finished, `hydrate()` would see a still-empty `owner_image_url` and revert `ownerImageUri` back to `null` — visibly undoing the just-uploaded photo and silently re-opening the one-time lock (both key off the same state). **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new `ownerImageUploadedAtRef` (set the instant a photo is picked, before the upload even starts) and an optional `fetchIssuedAt` parameter on `hydrate()` (captured right before each network call that feeds it — the background `forceFetchStores` and the profile-change-request re-fetch): the owner-image reconciliation branch is now skipped whenever a local upload has happened more recently than the fetch behind the current `hydrate()` call was issued, so a stale response can never stomp a newer local upload. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **App-wide crash: `"cannot add postgres_changes callbacks ... after subscribe()"`, thrown from `useStoreApprovalGate.ts` and caught by the root `ErrorBoundary`.** Found 2026-07-29, reported directly by the user via a live crash log. Root cause: `supabase.channel(topic)` returns the *same* channel object if one with that topic already exists (confirmed in `@supabase/realtime-js`'s `RealtimeClient.channel()` — it looks up `this.channels` by topic string before creating a new one). `useStoreApprovalGate`'s realtime effect built its topic purely from the store id (`store-approval-gate:${store.id}`), and this hook is mounted by every gated screen (home, settings, orders, pending-verification, etc.); Expo Router keeps prior stack screens mounted underneath a pushed screen, so two screens gating the same store were routinely mounted at once. The second mount's effect called `supabase.channel(...)`, got back the *first* mount's already-subscribed channel instance, and calling `.on()` on an already-subscribed channel throws exactly this error — crashing the whole app via the error boundary regardless of which screen the shopkeeper was actually on. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by giving each hook instance a unique topic (`store-approval-gate:${store.id}:${instanceIdRef.current}`, a per-mount random suffix generated once via `useRef`), so concurrently-mounted screens for the same store never collide on the same channel object. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not reproduced live (would need two gated screens simultaneously mounted to force the collision).

### Medium

- **`CachedStore` type didn't declare `is_approved`, relying on untyped `as any` casts.** **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — the type now declares the field directly, zero untyped casts remain.
- **Home screen `activeOrderCount` was hardcoded to 0**, always showing "Waiting for orders..." even with real orders in progress. **Fixed 2026-07-23** by wiring it to the real `/shopkeeper/orders?active=true` endpoint, polled via the existing `useSmartPoll` hook.
- **`computeGroupStatus` mislabeled "one side approved, other side never uploaded" as "Pending review."** **Fixed 2026-07-18** by adding a `missingAny` check before the "Pending review" fallback.
- **`pickStoreImage`/`pickOwnerImage` fell back to a literal `"unknown"` path segment if the store/session id was momentarily unavailable** — real cross-user photo-collision risk on a public bucket. **Fixed 2026-07-18** by bailing out with an alert instead.
- **Realtime channel names included a `Date.now()` suffix**, so every legitimate resubscription (e.g. a shopkeeper switching between stores) churned out a brand-new, never-reused channel identity instead of a stable one. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — both `home.tsx` channels (`products-<id>`, `store-<id>`) now use stable, deterministic names; effect cleanup already unsubscribes the old channel before the next one is created, and confirmed no other subscriber in the app uses the same names. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean.
- **Generic error message on accept-conflict, finished** — a prior pass fixed `previous-orders.tsx` to surface `json?.error || response.error`, but the root cause remained: `lib/api-client.ts`'s `getErrorMessage()` only read `error.data.message`, while the backend's 409 responses (`shopkeeper.controller.ts`, `"Already responded: ..."`) use the field name `error`, not `message` — so `response.error` was always the generic HTTP status text, not the real reason. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by checking `error.data.error` first in `getErrorMessage()`. Also fixed the other half of the same bug in `hooks/useOrders.ts`'s `acceptOrder()`: its `catch` block discarded the thrown error entirely and always alerted a hardcoded generic string, unlike `previous-orders.tsx`'s equivalent path — now uses `err?.message`. A shopkeeper who loses an accept-race now sees the real "Already responded: ..." message on both screens. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`toggleStoreStatus` was implemented three separate times, only one actually wired to the UI** — `lib/store-service.ts`'s `storeService.toggleStoreStatus()` and `hooks/useStore.ts`'s hook-returned `toggleStoreStatus` both had zero callers anywhere in the app; the real, only-ever-used implementation is the inline `toggleOnline`/`handleStatusToggle` in `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`, which also does substantially more (confirm-dialog UX, `setAllProductsOffline`/`restoreActiveProductsOnline`, cache invalidation via `appCache.ts`) than either dead copy's plain PATCH call. **Fixed 2026-07-26**, not by merging behavior but by deleting the two dead copies: confirmed `hooks/useStore.ts` has zero importers anywhere in the app (its entire fetch/select/toggle trio was superseded by `lib/appCache.ts`'s `fetchStoresCached`/`patchStoreActive`, not just its `toggleStoreStatus`), so the whole file was removed; `lib/store-service.ts`'s `toggleStoreStatus` method was removed on its own since the rest of that file (`updateStore`, `getCachedStore`, etc.) is still actively imported by `app/settings.tsx`/`components/StoreSettingsModal.tsx`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean.
- **Two entirely dead-code order-management implementations existed in parallel with the real one** — `hooks/useOrders.ts` + `components/IncomingOrderPopup.tsx` (a more complete 60s-auto-reject flow, never wired in) and `lib/order-service.ts` + `components/OrderFilters.tsx` (targeted a `/store-owner/orders/*` API contract that doesn't match the real `/shopkeeper/allocations/*` endpoints in use — would have silently failed if ever revived). **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting all four files: confirmed zero importers of either pair anywhere in the app (no dynamic imports, no barrel re-exports) — the real, live order-accept flow is the inline `acceptAllocation` in `app/(tabs)/previous-orders.tsx`, calling `apiClient.post('/shopkeeper/allocations/...')` directly, entirely independent of either dead pair. **Side note found during verification:** the 2026-07-26 "generic error message on accept-conflict" fix's edit to `hooks/useOrders.ts`'s `acceptOrder()` catch block was applied to this same dead code — harmless (the file is now deleted anyway) but had zero real-world effect at the time, since that hook was never actually reachable from any live screen. The `lib/api-client.ts` `getErrorMessage()` half of that same fix was real and impactful (confirmed live via `previous-orders.tsx`'s use of `apiClient.post`), so the fix's actual customer-facing value stands — just narrower than described at the time. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean.
- **Store images supported only a single photo, no carousel/array** — `lib/storage.ts`'s `uploadStoreImage()` used a fixed path (`${storeId}/cover.${ext}`) that silently overwrote any prior upload, and `app/profile.tsx` held only one `storeImageUri` value. **Reframed during this fix (2026-07-27):** the original framing ("real product-completeness gap for customer-facing store listings") doesn't hold up — a repo-wide grep found `stores.image_url` has **zero customer-facing consumers**: no store-listing page exists anywhere on the customer website, the customer mobile app's own `storeService.ts` queries `stores` but never selects `image_url`, and the admin panel's `StoresPage.tsx` doesn't render it either. The only place this image was ever displayed was the shopkeeper's own profile screen, showing their own upload back to themselves. Built anyway per explicit request, as groundwork for a future store-listing feature. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a full multi-image gallery: new `store_images` table (migration `20260903000000`, service-role-only, one row per image with `sort_order`; backfills any existing `stores.image_url` as image 0 so nothing already uploaded is lost) plus new backend endpoints `GET/POST /store-owner/stores/:id/images` and `DELETE /store-owner/stores/:id/images/:imageId` (`storeOwner.controller.ts`, capped at 5 images/store, 400 once full). `stores.image_url` is kept (not dropped) as a denormalized "cover" convenience field, re-derived from the lowest-`sort_order` image on every add/delete via a `syncStoreCoverImage()` helper — cheap to maintain and covers any future single-field reader. `lib/storage.ts`'s `uploadStoreImage()` now generates a unique path per upload instead of the fixed `cover.${ext}` path that used to silently clobber prior photos. `app/profile.tsx` replaced its single `storeImageUri` with a `storeImages` array, added a horizontal gallery strip (shown once ≥2 photos exist) with a "Cover" badge on the first image and a remove (×) button per photo in edit mode, and the hero banner tap now adds a new photo rather than replacing the existing one, disabled once the 5-image cap is hit. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and store-owner, the existing Jest suite (21/21) and backend vitest (17/17), all clean; not click-tested live and the new migration is not yet applied to the live database.
- **`lib/orders-db.ts`'s 4-strategy order-fetch fallback chain swallowed every real error into the same empty-array result as "genuinely zero orders"** — RPC → direct-table → `customer_orders`-join → 90-day/100-row JS-side scan, each stage just `console.warn`'d and returned `[]` on failure, so an RLS misconfiguration or broken RPC looked identical to "no orders yet" on both `previous-orders.tsx` and `payments.tsx`. **Fixed 2026-07-26** — each stage now returns `{ orders, failed }` instead of a bare array (the `42883`/"RPC doesn't exist" case and a stage legitimately finding zero rows both still resolve `failed: false`, since those aren't errors); the exported `getOrdersFromDb()` throws a new `OrdersFetchFailedError` only when every stage came back empty *and* at least one actually failed. Both callers now catch that specific error type and render a real "Couldn't load orders/payouts" state with a Try Again button, instead of silently rendering the ordinary empty state. `invoice/[orderId].tsx` doesn't call this fallback chain (it uses the separate single-order `getOrderByIdFromDb`, out of scope here). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean; not click-tested live (would need to force a real Supabase error to trigger the new banner).
- **No admin notification or review trail on store profile edits (name/address/phone)** — `app/profile.tsx`'s `handleSave` PATCHed `stores` directly and took effect immediately, with zero `profile_change_request` concept or admin-notify call anywhere, unlike verification documents which already go through a review queue. **Fixed 2026-07-26** with a full admin-approval workflow, not just a notification: new `store_profile_change_requests` table (migration `20260831000000`, service-role-only — same pattern as the `notifications` table) holding a JSON diff of old/new values per field; new backend endpoints `GET`/`POST /store-owner/stores/:id/profile-change-request` (`storeOwner.controller.ts`) let the shopkeeper submit a request (diffed server-side against the current row, so only real changes are recorded) — resubmitting while already pending replaces the existing row rather than stacking duplicates. `updateStore`'s direct-PATCH path no longer accepts `name`/`address`/`phone` at all (only `image_url`/`owner_image_url` remain immediate); the admin-facing `GET/POST /admin/stores/profile-change-requests(/:id/review)` (`adminStores.controller.ts`, gated on the existing `store_verification.*` permission) list pending requests and apply an approval's diff to `stores`, or record a reject with a required reason. `notificationService.notifyProfileChangeReviewed()` persists+pushes the outcome to the store, mirroring `notifyStoreApproved`; `requestProfileChange()` fires an `admin_notifications` row on submit via the existing `notifyAdmins()` helper. Admin panel gained a new `StoreProfileChangeRequestsPage.tsx` (list + diff view + approve/reject, reject requires a typed reason) wired into `AdminRoutes.tsx` and the sidebar. Shopkeeper app: `app/profile.tsx` now submits to the request endpoint instead of patching directly, shows a "Changes pending admin review" banner listing the submitted diff, and polls (20s, via the existing `useSmartPoll`) while a request is pending so the banner clears and the approved values refresh in without needing to leave the screen. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend/admin/store-owner, the existing store-owner Jest suite (21/21), and a full admin `vite build`, all clean; not click-tested live and the new migration is not yet applied to the live database.
  - **Same-day deep-dive review found and fixed 5 more bugs in this workflow:** (1) `reviewProfileChangeRequest` did a check-then-act on `request.status` in Node instead of under a DB row lock — two concurrent approve/reject calls on the same request could both pass the "still pending" check and both apply the `stores` patch. (2) Approving did two separate, non-transactional writes (patch `stores`, then update the request row) — a failure between them left the store already changed while the request row still showed `'pending'`, a "phantom pending" request an admin could later "reject" even though it had already taken effect. Both fixed together with one row-locked Postgres function (`review_store_profile_change_request`, migration `20260901000000`) that re-checks status under the lock and does both writes in one transaction, raising a distinct `ALREADY_REVIEWED:<status>` exception the controller maps to a 409 instead of a false success or a duplicate notification. (3) `requestProfileChange`'s submit path had the identical check-then-act race on its own "is there already a pending request" check — two concurrent submissions could both see none and both `INSERT`, leaving duplicate pending rows for one store. Fixed with a partial unique index (`one pending request per store`, same migration) plus update-first-then-insert-with-retry-on-23505 logic. (4) `app/profile.tsx`'s `loadPendingChangeRequest` ran a real side effect (cache-clear + re-fetch) inside a `setPendingChangeRequest` state-updater callback — React requires updaters to be pure and may invoke them more than once per transition. (5) The same function had no stale-response guard, so an in-flight 20s poll issued just before a submission could resolve after it and clobber the just-shown "pending review" banner back to nothing. Both (4) and (5) fixed with a ref mirroring the state plus a monotonic fetch-sequence guard, matching the pattern this codebase already uses elsewhere (`useOrderTrackingRealtime.ts`). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and store-owner, and the existing store-owner Jest suite (21/21) plus backend vitest (17/17), both clean. New migration applied to the live database the same session (confirmed live via `supabase db query` — the new function and unique index both present with the expected `service_role`-only execute grant).
- **`context/AppContext.tsx` and `lib/testSupabase.ts` were both entirely dead/unreferenced code** — the latter's `testProductInsert()` performed a live insert followed by a delete against the real `products` table. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting both files: confirmed zero importers anywhere in the app (no dynamic imports, no barrel re-exports). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean.
- **The app's own `supabase/*.sql` scripts were tracked in git but weren't real Supabase-CLI migrations, unverifiable whether ever applied live.** **Investigated and fixed 2026-07-27** by checking every one of the 6 files directly against the live database (`supabase db query` against the linked project, not guesswork): 5 were confirmed dead/already-applied/redundant — including `custom-master-products-rls.sql`, whose blanket anon-write policies had already been superseded by the real `20260820000000_close_anon_write_rls_holes.sql` fix (re-running it would have silently reverted that fix), and `invoices.sql`, a fully abandoned invoice design (`store_invoice_sequences` doesn't exist live) that reused the table name `invoices` with a schema incompatible with the real, tracked invoice system. All 5 were deleted, along with the folder's README. The 6th, `orders-rpc-and-rls.sql`, turned out to be both genuinely live-depended-on (`get_orders_for_store()`, called directly by `lib/orders-db.ts`'s RPC-first order-loading path) **and** a live, currently-exploitable vulnerability: confirmed via direct query that `anon` had EXECUTE on this `SECURITY DEFINER` function with zero caller-identity check, meaning anyone holding the app's public anon key could call it with any store's UUID and read that store's full order/customer/item data — same class as the already-fixed `finalize_order_if_ready` hole. Converted into a real tracked migration (`20260904000000_get_orders_for_store_rpc_and_ownership_check.sql`) adding a `shopkeeper_owns_store()` ownership check (already existed from the 20260820000000 fix; the app already sends the `x-shopkeeper-token` header this check reads, so no app-side change was needed) — an unauthorized caller now gets `[]` instead of real data. **Applied to production the same day** via `supabase db query` (not `db push`, per this project's established safe-apply pattern) and verified live: a direct `curl` to the anon REST endpoint for a real store with 25 orders, with no shopkeeper session, returned `[]` after the fix (would have returned all 25 orders before). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21), both clean.
- **Settings screen's "Store Settings" modal silently overrode/conflicted with the real, admin-reviewed profile-edit flow, and every field it tried to save was already broken.** `StoreSettingsModal.tsx` (opened from Settings → Store Settings) PATCHed `name`/`address`/`phone`/`email`/`description`/`delivery_radius_km`/`delivery_fee`/`min_order_amount` directly via `updateStore`, entirely bypassing the admin-review workflow already built for `app/profile.tsx` (see the profile-change-request fix above) — a shopkeeper could open either screen and get two different, contradictory ideas of how editing their store's identity fields works. Investigating why the user noticed this at all turned up that it was already fully non-functional: `updateStore`'s whitelist only accepts `image_url`/`owner_image_url` since the profile-change-request fix landed, so every save from this modal already failed with "No valid fields to update," and the entire Delivery sub-section it exposed (radius/fee/min-order) was equally dead — not implemented anywhere by that endpoint either. **Fixed 2026-07-27** (explicit user decision, given the modal was both redundant and broken) by deleting `StoreSettingsModal.tsx` and the "Store" section/row entirely — the existing "Profile card" at the top of Settings (linking to `/profile`) is the one real, working place to edit these fields, with the admin-review workflow already in place. No delivery section anywhere on this screen anymore, per explicit instruction not to keep it.
- **Settings screen loaded slowly** — `loadData()` did its own raw, uncached `fetch('/store-owner/stores?userId=...')` on every visit, redundant with `lib/appCache.ts`'s shared 10-minute store cache that every other screen (`home.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, `low-stock-settings.tsx`) already uses specifically to eliminate exactly this kind of duplicate network round-trip. Compounding it, the screen's loading spinner blocked the *entire* page (header, profile card, all rows) on that fetch resolving, even though only one row ("Store ID") actually needed the store data at all. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by switching to `peekStores()`/`fetchStoresCached()` (same pattern as the other screens — instant synchronous cache hit when warm, e.g. right after visiting Home, falling back to one shared in-flight network request when cold) and decoupling the loading gate from the store fetch entirely: the page now renders as soon as the session is read (all it actually needs for the header/profile card), and the "Store ID" row fills in asynchronously once the store resolves, instead of blocking the whole screen behind it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Notification Preferences screen (`components/NotificationSettings.tsx`) had a mount-time race with its own service's preference load.** `NotificationService`'s constructor kicks off an async `loadPreferences()` from AsyncStorage but never exposed a way to know when it finished; `NotificationSettings` read `notificationService.getPreferences()` synchronously in its initial `useState`, so if this screen happened to mount before that load resolved (e.g. a fast tap into Settings right after launch), it would snapshot the hardcoded defaults instead of a real previously-saved preference — and toggling any *other* switch afterward would re-save that stale default state for every untouched field, silently reverting a legitimately-customized preference. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding `NotificationService.whenReady()` (resolves once the AsyncStorage load actually completes) and having the screen re-sync `prefs` from it after mount. Also fixed a smaller, adjacent issue found in the same file: the "On" status badge (shown once notifications are enabled) was a plain non-touchable `View`, so `handleEnable()`'s only-reachable-when-enabled branch — the "go to your device settings to disable" hint — was dead code with no way to trigger it; made the badge a `TouchableOpacity` calling `handleEnable`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (no new errors/warnings introduced in either changed file; pre-existing repo-wide lint problems elsewhere are unrelated and untouched by this fix). **Found but not fixed, flagged in Open Issues instead:** the per-type toggles on this same screen (New Orders/Low Stock/Daily Summary/Payments/System Alerts) are saved but never actually enforced by the backend when sending pushes, and the "Low Stock Alerts" settings screen reachable from the same Settings page turned out to be entirely dead end-to-end (columns don't exist live) — both are real product gaps, not mechanical bugs, so left for a scoping decision rather than fixed here.
- **"Low Stock Alerts" turned out to be dead by design, not by gap — removed entirely rather than built out.** Asked the user for a scope decision on the two flagged items above; clarified that this platform deliberately has no stock-count/threshold concept at all — stock is managed entirely by the shopkeeper toggling each product active/inactive themselves, on purpose, specifically to avoid the platform having to maintain any stock-threshold logic. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by removing the feature end-to-end rather than building the threshold-crossing detection a "Low Stock Alerts" label implies: deleted `app/low-stock-settings.tsx` and its Settings-page entry point row (store-owner app); removed the "Low Stock Alerts" per-type notification toggle from `NotificationSettings.tsx` and the now-meaningless `lowStock` field from `NotificationPreferences`/`DEFAULT_PREFERENCES` (`lib/notifications.ts`), plus a dead `low_stock`-type tap-handler branch in the same file (unreachable — no backend code has ever sent that notification type, confirmed via a full repo grep before touching anything). Also removed from the **admin panel**, per explicit instruction to clear it there too: the `low_stock` case from `utils/notificationLink.ts`'s click-routing, `AdminHeader.tsx`'s bell-icon mapping (and its now-unused `AlertTriangle` import), `NotificationsPage.tsx`'s type union/icon-map/filter-tab list, and `SettingsPage.tsx`'s own (separately-tracked, localStorage-only) admin notification-preferences toggle for the same concept. A pre-check grep across the entire codebase (all mobile apps, admin, backend) confirmed zero backend references to `low_stock`/`lowStock` anywhere before removing anything, and a post-removal grep + full rebuild confirmed the same, clean. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` on store-owner/admin/backend, `expo lint` on store-owner (43 problems now vs. 45 before — 2 fewer errors, from the deleted dead screen, no new issues), the store-owner Jest suite (21/21), a full admin `vite build`, and the backend vitest suite (17/17), all clean.

- **Whole verification nav feature (Details/Status/Documents) mirrored into the shopkeeper app, plus a new owner-photo upload and a Store Images section, per explicit request ("entirely copy that to shopkeeper app").** **Fixed 2026-07-29**:
  - New `components/VerificationNavBar.tsx` (Details/Status/Documents pills, `router.replace` between peer screens), added to `upload-documents.tsx` and `pending-verification.tsx`.
  - `store-owner-signup.tsx` gained a **read-only view mode**: reached via direct navigation (no `phone` param) with an existing session — this app never saves a session before signup completes (unlike the rider app, confirmed via `otp.tsx`: no `saveSession()` call on the `mode==="signup"` branch), so session-without-phone-param reliably means signup is done. Given the form's interactive map/address-autocomplete UI, the read-only view is a **simplified summary card** (owner name, store name, phone, email, address as plain text) rather than a frozen map, plus the nav bar and an owner-photo picker — not a pixel-identical freeze of the original form.
  - **New capability, per explicit scope decision (store owner's own photo only, not store photos):** an owner-photo picker added to the read-only details view, reusing `profile.tsx`'s existing `uploadOwnerImage`/`OWNER_IMAGE_KEY`/patch-store pattern verbatim. Not available during the very first, never-submitted signup form — there's no owner id to scope the upload to until signup actually completes (no session exists yet at that point either), so this is view-only-mode-only, which covers effectively the entire pre-verification window anyway.
  - **Store Images, added to `upload-documents.tsx` under a new "Store Images" section** (per explicit instruction, "photos of the stores will be in the upload docs page"), reusing the exact `store_images` table/endpoints and gallery logic `profile.tsx`'s hero banner already has — kept in **both** places rather than moved, per explicit follow-up ("they can ofc change the store images later on from the profile section as well").
  - Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (44/10/34 vs. the prior 49/11/38 baseline — net improvement, no new issues in any touched file), and the Jest suite (21/21).
- **Rider app: back-navigating from Documents to the Status page immediately bounced back to Documents** — `pending-verification.tsx`'s `checkApprovalNow` (mount + 10s poll + realtime) auto-redirected to `/documents` any time docs were incomplete, which is the normal state for a rider deliberately checking status mid-upload. Reported directly by the user. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing the auto-redirect — the screen's own "Upload Documents" button is now the only way it ever sends a rider to `/documents`.
- **Broader ask, same report: free back-and-forth navigation between signup details, documents, and verification status, for both apps, until verified** — previously each screen was reachable in only one direction, with no way back to signup at all once submitted. **Fixed 2026-07-29**: new `components/VerificationNavBar.tsx` (rider app) added to `documents.tsx`/`pending-verification.tsx`; `signup.tsx` gained the same **read-only view mode** later mirrored into the shopkeeper app (see above) — reached via direct navigation with `needsSignupCompletion: false`, renders the submitted Name/Email/Address/Vehicle Type read-only with the submit/session-clearing "Go back" buttons hidden, replaced by a note that changes are only possible post-verification via Profile.
- **Follow-up, same report: navigation between the 3 screens felt slow, clunky, and showed a white flash** — root-caused to two independent problems, present in **both** apps:
  1. **Duplicate fetching.** Rider's `pending-verification.tsx` ran its own independent `checkRiderVerification` call (mount + a *second*, separate 10s poll) on top of `useRiderVerificationGate`'s own mount/focus/30s-poll fetch — 2x the network round-trips on every visit. Shopkeeper's `pending-verification.tsx` had the identical duplication against `useStoreApprovalGate`. **Fixed** by consolidating both screens onto their respective hook's own `refresh`/`verified`(`approved`) state, removing the screens' separate poll/fetch entirely; the realtime subscriptions now also call the hook's `refresh` instead of a separate fetch path.
  2. **No caching — every visit re-fetched from a blank slate**, which is what produced the visible white-screen flash (especially once `animation: "none"` removed the slide transition that used to mask it). **Fixed** by seeding each screen's initial state from a synchronous cache when warm, so a second/third visit within the same session renders real content immediately while refreshing in the background: new `lib/riderVerificationCache.ts` (rider — shared by `useRiderVerificationGate` and `documents.tsx`, both populated by the same `checkRiderVerification` result); shopkeeper's existing `appCache.ts`/`peekStores()` now also seeds `useStoreApprovalGate` and `upload-documents.tsx`'s initial `storeId`/`loading` state, which previously ignored the cache it already computed and always started blank regardless.
  3. **Animation.** Initially set to `animation: "none"` (removed the slide-in transition entirely) as a first pass, then changed to `animation: "fade"` on the same `<Stack.Screen>` overrides per explicit follow-up request for smoother movement — a cross-fade is the standard Expo Router transition for switching between peer/sibling screens (vs. the default slide, which implies drilling deeper into a stack — the wrong metaphor for 3 equal tabs).
  - Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems) on the rider app; `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (no new issues), and Jest (21/21) on the shopkeeper app.
- **Rider app: keyboard didn't auto-dismiss after OTP auto-submit, and `KeyboardAvoidingView`'s Android behavior fought with the OS's own resize, making the keyboard look like it was swallowing the whole screen down to the buttons.** Reported directly by the user. **Fixed 2026-07-29**: added `Keyboard.dismiss()` alongside the existing auto-submit-on-6-digits effect in `otp.tsx`. Root-caused the sizing complaint to `behavior={Platform.OS === "ios" ? "padding" : "height"}` being set on top of an Android manifest that already declares `windowSoftInputMode="adjustResize"` (confirmed in both `app.json` and `AndroidManifest.xml`) — "height" behavior double-compensates on top of the OS's own resize, over-shrinking the content. Fixed across all 4 files using this exact pattern (`otp.tsx`, `phone.tsx`, `signup.tsx`, `profile.tsx`) by changing the Android branch to `undefined` (no RN-side compensation needed; the OS already handles it) — iOS keeps `"padding"` since it has no equivalent native resize. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Shopkeeper app: screen rotation didn't respect the device's rotation-lock setting** — reported directly by the user, suspecting the tablet-support work. Root-caused to `plugins/withTabletSupport.js` setting `android:screenOrientation="fullSensor"` on the main activity (added when tablet support was built) — `fullSensor` rotates purely off the physical sensor and **deliberately ignores the OS rotation-lock toggle**, a well-known Android gotcha (`fullSensor`/`sensor` vs. `user`/`fullUser`, where only the latter pair defers to the lock). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by changing both `screenOrientation` values in the plugin (main activity + the ML Kit barcode-scanner activity override) from `fullSensor` to `fullUser` — behaves exactly like `fullSensor` (phones stay portrait-first, tablets rotate freely) when rotation is unlocked, but respects the lock when it's on. Also updated the currently checked-in `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` directly (both occurrences), since a build might use that file as-is without re-running `expo prebuild`. **Requires a fresh native build to take effect** — this is a manifest-level change, not shippable via OTA update.
- **Follow-up: the Documents page's header ("Upload Documents" title + back button) visually collided with the nav bar directly beneath it, and per-mount entrance animations were adding real, avoidable delay to every nav-bar switch.** Reported directly by the user, who'd noticed Documents felt faster than Details despite having more content (images) — the actual difference was Documents already being cache-seeded while Details/Status still ran a fresh 300–500ms fade-in animation on every single mount (i.e. every tap of the nav bar), on top of any real fetch time. **Fixed 2026-07-29**:
  - Removed the header section (back button, title, doc-count subtitle) entirely from `documents.tsx`/`upload-documents.tsx` — the nav bar is now the only thing at the top of the screen. Cleaned up the resulting dead styles and now-unused `uploadedCount`/`totalRequired` variables.
  - Removed the `fadeAnim`/`slideAnim` entrance-animation pairs from `pending-verification.tsx` (both apps) and `upload-documents.tsx` (shopkeeper) — these fired on every mount with zero informational purpose, purely adding latency to a switch that should feel instant once data is cached. `documents.tsx` (rider) and `store-owner-signup.tsx` never had one.
- **Follow-up: Details page still loaded slowly relative to the now-fast Documents/Status pages, in both apps.** Root cause: `signup.tsx`/`store-owner-signup.tsx`'s view-only mode decided whether to show the read-only view (and whether to block on a spinner) **inside an async effect** — waiting on an `AsyncStorage`-backed session read before rendering anything — while Documents/Status already made that same decision synchronously from cache. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by using the route's `phone`/`signupTicket` param (already available synchronously, no async read needed) to decide `viewOnly` on the very first render instead of waiting for the session check, and seeding name/vehicle-type (rider, from `riderVerificationCache.ts`) or store name/address (shopkeeper, from `appCache.ts`'s `peekStores()`) synchronously from the same caches Documents/Status already populate — the async session check now only fills in the couple of fields not present in those caches (email/address for rider; owner name/phone/email for shopkeeper) in the background, without blocking the rest of the page behind a spinner.
- **Follow-up: added a Logout button to the Details page in both apps, restricting it to exactly Details + Status (not Documents), per explicit instruction.** Previously logout only existed on the Status page. Added the same `Alert.alert` confirm-then-`clearSession`-then-redirect pattern (shopkeeper's version also calls `notificationService.unregister()` first, matching its Status page) to `signup.tsx`'s and `store-owner-signup.tsx`'s view-only render, shown only when `viewOnly` is true (i.e. never on the original, one-time editable signup form). Confirmed via grep that neither Documents page has ever had one.
  - Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (0 problems on rider; same pre-existing baseline on shopkeeper, no new issues — 2 new exhaustive-deps warnings from the intentional one-time cache reads were suppressed with scoped `eslint-disable` comments and explanatory notes, matching this codebase's existing convention for that pattern), and the shopkeeper Jest suite (21/21).
- **Rider app: the "Need help? Contact an admin" section on the Status page was an unstyled heading + plain tappable text rows, requested to be improved.** **Fixed 2026-07-29** with a proper card (`helpCard`) — icon-badge header, then one row per admin with a circular avatar, name, formatted phone number, and a dedicated call-icon button, separated by dividers — replacing the old bare `linkBtn`/`linkBtnText` list (now-dead styles removed). Shopkeeper's own "Need help" link already points at a separate, already-well-designed `/help` screen (FAQ, WhatsApp, quick messages) with different (placeholder) contact data, so left untouched as an unrelated surface.
