- **Rider app: the document upload picker only offered "Choose Image"/"Upload PDF", missing the "Take Photo" (camera capture) option the shopkeeper app's equivalent picker already has.** Requested directly by the user. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding a `takePhoto()` function (`ImagePicker.requestCameraPermissionsAsync` + `launchCameraAsync`, same validation/shape as the existing `pickImage`) and a third "Take Photo" option at the top of the picker sheet in `documents.tsx`, applying to every document slot including the 3 vehicle photos. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Follow-up, same day: further polish on the rider Status page's admin-contact card** — per 2 rounds of direct feedback, removed the "?" from the "Contact an admin" heading (was "Need help? Contact an admin"), removed the per-admin avatar icon and the row dividers, and removed the green help-circle icon next to the heading entirely — now just plain text over unadorned name/phone/call-button rows, no icons or separators. Removed the resulting dead styles (`adminAvatar`, `adminRowDivider`, `helpHeader`) each time. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems) after each round.
- **Per-type notification toggles (Settings → Notification Preferences) were saved but never actually enforced — flagged as a known gap at the "Notification Preferences" fix above (line 314), now closed.** Found again 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit (same issue, not a new finding). `backend/src/services/notification.service.ts` sent every push (`new_order`, etc.) unconditionally to whatever `expo_push_token` was on file, ignoring the `app_users.notification_preferences` column entirely for shopkeeper-facing sends — mirroring the rider side's own `isRiderNotificationEnabled()`, which already did this correctly. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a shopkeeper-side twin, `isShopkeeperNotificationEnabled(ownerId, category)` (same table/column, keyed on the store owner's `app_users.id` instead of the rider's), wired into `notifyShopkeeperNewOrder` — the only shopkeeper-facing push with a corresponding mobile-app toggle (`newOrders`; the app's other three toggles — `dailySummary`/`payments`/`systemAlerts` — don't correspond to any push this service currently sends at all, so there's nothing yet to gate them against). `notifyStoreApproved`/`notifyProfileChangeReviewed` are deliberately left ungated, same precedent as the rider app's `rider_approved` — account-status notifications aren't optional. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean.
- **Shopkeeper app: owner-photo upload only worked on the *already-submitted* read-only Details view, not the original fresh signup form** — by design, since no account/user id exists yet at that point to scope a Storage upload to (confirmed via `otp.tsx`: this app never saves a session before signup completes). The user was checking the fresh form specifically and saw no change. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding the same photo picker to the fresh form too, deferring the actual upload: picking a photo there only sets a local preview (`pendingOwnerImageUri`), and `handleNext`, right after a successful signup response gives us a real `user.id`/token, fires a best-effort background upload (`uploadOwnerImage` → `AsyncStorage` → fetches the newly-created store via `fetchStoresCached` → `PATCH .../stores/:id` with `owner_image_url`) — non-blocking, so signup navigation isn't delayed by it, and non-fatal if it fails (the owner can still add the photo later from Details or Profile). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (same 42/10/32 baseline, no new issues), and the Jest suite (21/21).
- **The "required documents" progress count (`upload-documents.tsx`, `pending-verification.tsx`, `profile.tsx`) only ever tallied the 7 business documents, never the store's photo gallery — stuck reporting "7/7" even when store images were still missing, and the gallery itself had no "picked but unsaved" vs. "saved" distinction at all.** Reported directly by the user ("still saying 7/7"; "no ready to save state as well and no saved state"). Each of the three screens computed its own independent `uploadedCount`/total from `REQUIRED_DOC_KEYS`/`DOCUMENT_SECTIONS` only, with zero awareness of `storeImages`. Separately, `upload-documents.tsx`'s `pickStoreImage` uploaded to the `store-images` bucket and saved to `store_images` immediately on pick — unlike every document field (staged locally in `pendingFiles`, only persisted when "Save" is pressed) — so there was no equivalent "Ready to save" moment, and no visible "Saved" confirmation tied to the Save button either. **Fixed 2026-07-29**: all three screens now compute their total as `7 + MAX_STORE_IMAGES (5) = 12` and fold the store image count into the numerator (`pending-verification.tsx` fetches the count via the existing `.../images` endpoint; `profile.tsx` reuses its already-loaded `storeImages` state). In `upload-documents.tsx`, picking a photo now stages it in a new `pendingStoreImages` array (local URIs only) instead of uploading immediately — the thumbnail renders dimmed with a "Ready to save" chip, and the section header shows the same status text, mirroring the existing document-card status badges (`computeGroupStatus`). Pressing "Save Documents & Photos" (renamed from "Save Documents") now actually uploads every staged photo (`saveStoreImages()`) and refreshes the gallery, after which the header badge reads "Saved"; the completion alert also now reports remaining photo count when documents are done but the gallery isn't full yet. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the store-owner app for all three files; not click-tested live.
- **`app/inventory.tsx` reintroduced the cross-account cache-leak class already fixed elsewhere, via module-level JS variables `clearSession()` can't reach — was confirmed dead/unreachable code (nothing in the app navigates to `/inventory`; the real "Inventory" tab routes to `app/(tabs)/stock.tsx`'s `InventoryCatalogSection` instead).** Documented 2026-07-28 as a landmine to flag before deletion or reuse. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by deleting `app/inventory.tsx` outright (confirmed zero remaining imports/route references anywhere first) rather than patching module-level variables that could never execute, matching this doc's established convention for confirmed-dead code. **Correction caught during this same fix:** earlier the same day, a scroll-to-top button was added to this file in response to a direct user request about "the inventory" screen — since this file was never actually reachable, that fix never surfaced to a real shopkeeper. Ported the same fix to the real live screen instead: `app/(tabs)/stock.tsx`'s `StockTab` (the outer `ScrollView` wrapping `InventoryCatalogSection`, which itself renders a `scrollEnabled={false}` `FlatList` the same way the deleted file did) now tracks scroll offset and shows a floating scroll-to-top button past 400px, calling `scrollRef.current?.scrollTo({ y: 0, animated: true })`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`app/notification-inbox.tsx` had no caching at all — every visit blocked on `getSession()` (async storage read) plus a full network round-trip to `GET /store-owner/notifications` behind a blank spinner, no matter how many times the screen had already been viewed.** Reported directly by the user ("the stores notifications page is loading slowly"). Same root-cause shape as the "Settings screen loaded slowly" fix above and the verification-screens cache-seeding work — this screen just never got the same treatment. **Fixed 2026-07-29** with a new `lib/notificationsCache.ts`, mirroring `appCache.ts`'s store-cache shape (in-memory `_mem` + AsyncStorage backup, `peekNotifications`/`hydrateNotificationsCache`/`persistNotifications`/`clearNotificationsCache`) but with no TTL short-circuit — a background refetch always still happens on mount regardless of cache age, this only avoids rendering nothing while it's in flight. `app/index.tsx` (splash) now warms this cache alongside `hydrateStoreCache()`. `notification-inbox.tsx` seeds its initial state from `peekNotifications()` via a lazy `useState` initializer (read fresh on every mount, not module-scoped — a module-level `const` would only ever capture whatever was cached the very first time the route was imported and never reflect a later update on a subsequent visit) — when warm, the real list renders instantly with no spinner, while `fetchNotifications` runs silently in the background and re-persists the result; a genuinely cold, cache-less first load is unaffected (spinner shows as before). `markAllRead`/`markOneRead` now also call `persistNotifications` on their optimistic updates, so a quick back-and-forth doesn't flash stale unread state before the next background refetch lands. `session.ts`'s `clearSession()`/`guardFreshInstall()` both now call `clearNotificationsCache()` too — same cross-account leak class already fixed for the store list and owner-photo caches, avoided here from the start rather than found later. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.

### Low

- **`registration-success.tsx` always redirected to Home after a flat 2.5s regardless of outcome**, with no "you're pending" moment right after signup. **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — it now redirects to `/pending-verification` instead of Home (the 2.5s delay itself is unchanged).
- **`isActivated` fail-open default.** Both session-save call sites (`otp.tsx`'s login flow, `store-owner-signup.tsx`'s signup-complete flow) fell back to `?? true` if the field was ever missing from the server response — meaning a shopkeeper whose account status genuinely couldn't be determined would silently be shown "Active" on the profile page's Status row instead of "Pending." In practice `app_users.is_activated` is a `NOT NULL` column always set at signup, so this fallback never actually fires today — but it's the wrong direction for a status label to fail in if it ever does (a network hiccup or future response-shape change). **Fixed 2026-07-31** by changing both fallbacks to `?? false`, so a genuinely missing value now defaults to the more honest "Pending" rather than confidently claiming "Active." Only affects the profile page's label — this field was already confirmed to have zero access-control role elsewhere in the app. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing Jest suite (21/21, unchanged).
- **Heavy unconditional `console.log`/`warn`/`error` in `lib/notifications.ts`, including full notification payload contents, none of it gated behind `__DEV__`.** **Fixed 2026-07-31** by gating all 24 call sites (including the `Notifications.addNotificationReceivedListener`/`ResponseReceivedListener` handlers, which logged the full raw notification/response object) behind `if (__DEV__)`, matching the existing convention already used extensively elsewhere in this app (`otp.tsx`, `home.tsx`, `_layout.tsx`, `previous-orders.tsx`). No behavior change — `this.handleNotificationTap(...)`'s actual navigation logic inside the tap listener stays unconditional; only the logging around it is gated. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (same 43/10/33 baseline, no new issues), and the existing Jest suite (21/21).
- **`scheduleNotification()`'s `_triggerDate` parameter was entirely ignored — always fired immediately regardless of what was passed.** Confirmed via a full repo grep that this method has zero callers anywhere in the app (unlike its siblings `sendLocalNotification`/`cancelNotification`/`cancelAllNotifications`, which do exactly what their names promise and were left alone). **Fixed 2026-07-31** by deleting the method outright rather than implementing real scheduled-trigger support for a feature nothing currently calls — matches this session's established pattern of removing genuinely dead code instead of building out an unrequested feature underneath it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (same baseline), and the existing Jest suite (21/21).
- **Several silent `catch {}` blocks swallowed real failures with zero visibility, including the store-approval status poll.** `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s 30s approval-check poll, its cache-invalidation helper, `lib/appCache.ts`'s store-cache hydrate/persist functions, and `app/notification-inbox.tsx`'s mark-read pings all had bare `catch {}` blocks — a shopkeeper whose approval poll silently started failing (network blip, backend hiccup) would have no way to notice, and neither would a developer debugging a report of "my approval banner never showed up." **Fixed 2026-07-31** by adding `if (__DEV__) console.warn(...)` logging to all 6 sites, same convention as the `lib/notifications.ts` fix above — no behavior change, still fails open/continues exactly as before, just no longer silent in dev. Left two genuinely-intentional silent catches alone: `otp.tsx`'s `JSON.parse` fallback (legitimate non-JSON-response handling, not a failure) and `session.ts`'s `SecureStore.deleteItemAsync(...).catch(() => {})` calls inside `clearSession()`/`guardFreshInstall()` (best-effort deletion of a token that may not exist — not worth logging on every logout). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (same baseline), and the existing Jest suite (21/21).
- **`notification-inbox.tsx`'s optimistic mark-read state wasn't rolled back on failure** — the `console.warn` logging fix directly above only made the failure visible in dev, it didn't revert the local state. Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above — the rider/customer apps' equivalent screens got a full revert treatment on 2026-07-30, this store-owner screen only got the logging half). **Fixed 2026-08-04** with the same snapshot-and-revert-on-catch pattern used there: `markAllRead`/`markOneRead` now snapshot `notifications` before the optimistic update and restore it (persisting the reverted state to cache too) alongside an `Alert.alert(...)` on failure, instead of silently leaving stale "read" state showing. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same baseline, nothing new).
- **Closing the Home stock-search bar left the product list invisibly filtered by the stale query — looked like every product had vanished.** Found 2026-07-28 during a frontend-focused deep audit. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s search-close button only cleared `stockSearchQuery`, never the `debouncedSearchQuery` that `filteredStoreProducts` is actually keyed on, and didn't cancel any pending debounce timer either. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new `closeStockSearch()` handler that clears both state values, cancels any in-flight debounce timeout, and closes the search bar all in one place; also routed the in-bar "×" clear button through the existing `handleStockSearchChange("")` (which already keeps both values in sync) instead of only clearing `stockSearchQuery` directly, which had the same narrower version of the bug. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (no new issues beyond the pre-existing baseline).
- **One-tap product delete from Home's stock list with zero confirmation.** Found 2026-07-28, same audit. The trash icon next to each stock item called `deleteProduct` (soft-delete via `deleted_at`) directly on tap, unlike every other destructive action in this app (going online/offline uses a custom confirm modal; rejecting an order in `previous-orders.tsx` uses an "cannot be undone" `Alert.alert`) — a stray tap on the small hit target next to the active/off toggle silently removed a live product with no undo path. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new `confirmDeleteProduct()` that opens the same `confirmModal` UI already used for the online/offline toggle, naming the product and warning it can't be undone from there, before calling the real `deleteProduct`. The lower-severity twin in `app/(tabs)/stock.tsx:717` (clearing an in-progress, not-yet-saved custom-product photo) was deliberately left as-is at the time — since it's local, reversible picker state, not a live delete — but has since also gotten a lightweight confirm; see below.
- **Notification inbox had no error state distinct from "no notifications."** `app/notification-inbox.tsx`'s `fetchNotifications` caught all fetch failures (network error, non-2xx, bad JSON) into the same `setNotifications([])` as a genuinely empty inbox — worse, it didn't even check `res.ok`, so a non-2xx response with a non-array JSON body (e.g. `{error: "..."}`) fell through the *success* path into an empty list rather than the `catch` at all. No retry affordance either way, unlike `previous-orders.tsx`/`payments.tsx`, which both already distinguish a load failure (with a "Try Again" button) from a genuinely empty list via a separate boolean error state. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by adding a `loadError` state: `fetchNotifications` now throws on `!res.ok` or a non-array response body (instead of silently treating either as empty), clears `loadError` on success, and sets it in the `catch` — the existing "don't clobber real cached data on a background-refresh failure" behavior is preserved, `loadError` just also gets set alongside it so the UI can tell the two cases apart. `ListEmptyComponent` now branches on `loadError`, showing the same "Couldn't load X / Check your connection and try again" + red "Try Again" button pattern already established in `payments.tsx` (retry re-calls `fetchNotifications`), falling back to the original "No notifications yet" empty state otherwise. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **`app/(tabs)/stock.tsx:717`'s image "Remove" button (clearing an in-progress custom product photo before it's ever saved) had no confirmation** — same class as the Home stock-list delete above but originally judged lower-severity/not worth the friction, since it's local, reversible, pre-save picker state rather than a live product or a network delete. Revisited and fixed anyway per explicit user decision. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a plain two-button `Alert.alert("Remove photo?", ..., [Cancel, {text: "Remove", style: "destructive"}])` around the existing `setImageUri(null); setImageBase64(null)` call — deliberately the lighter native `Alert.alert` rather than replicating `home.tsx`'s full custom `confirmModal` component (icon/title/message/loading-state Modal), since that machinery is local to `home.tsx` and not shared, and a native two-button alert is proportionate to how low-stakes this particular action actually is. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.

## Rider app (`NAT_Near-Now_Rider-`)

### Critical

- **Supabase service-role key was plumbed into the client bundle.** **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — a prior, undated change had already removed all references.
- **Auth token stored unencrypted in AsyncStorage for up to 30 days.** **Fixed 2026-07-23**, same fix shape as the shopkeeper app — token moved to `expo-secure-store` with a legacy migration.
- **`markDelivered` had no server-side proof the delivery OTP was verified — a real delivery-fraud/payout-integrity gap.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup). `verifyDeliveryOTP` checked the OTP but persisted nothing on success; `markDelivered` never read any OTP state, trusting only the rider app's local React `otpVerified` flag — a replayed/direct call to the "delivered" endpoint could skip OTP verification entirely and still trigger payout. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by adding a `customer_orders.delivery_otp_verified_at` column (migration `20260928000000`, applied live via `supabase db query --linked` and confirmed present via `information_schema.columns`), having `verifyDeliveryOTP` set it on success, and having `markDelivered` reject (400) unless it's set — mirroring `verifyPickupCode`'s already-correct pattern of not trusting client state for a status transition that triggers payout. Also closed the related Low sub-issue (OTP-verified state lost on app relaunch, forcing spurious re-verification): `getPickupSequence` now returns `delivery_otp_verified` in its `order` payload, and the rider app's `loadSequence` (polled every 10s while on this screen) hydrates local `otpVerified` from it, so a rider who already verified before a relaunch isn't asked to repeat the OTP. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend` and the rider app.

### High

- **App requested "Always" location permission but never actually did background tracking** — foreground-only `watchPositionAsync` stopped the moment the app was backgrounded, leaving `driver_locations` stale mid-delivery. **Fixed 2026-07-25** with a real `expo-task-manager` background task, requesting background permission only after foreground is granted (per Apple's guidance) and handing off via `AppState` transitions so foreground/background tracking never double-send. **Requires a new native build** — not picked up by Expo Go or an existing dev-client build.
- **Reinstatement from suspended/offboarded was invisible until app restart** — the status-approval poll only ran while `pending_verification`. **Fixed 2026-07-25** by broadening the poll's gate to run whenever status exists and isn't already active.
- **No `ErrorBoundary` anywhere in the app** — an unhandled render exception crashed the whole app with no fallback UI, mid-delivery. **Fixed 2026-07-25**, mirroring the store-owner app's existing pattern (without its Sentry integration, out of scope here).
- **Approval detection during `pending_verification` was poll-only, no realtime subscription** — Realtime's RLS check requires a real Supabase Auth session, which this app's custom phone-OTP login never populated, and giving `delivery_partners` a public-read policy (the shortcut used for `stores`) would have exposed rider PII/GPS to anyone with the anon key. **Fixed 2026-07-25** by minting each rider a real, narrowly-scoped Supabase Auth bridge session at login (synthetic per-rider email, `delivery_partners.auth_user_id`, migration `20260819000000`) used solely to satisfy `auth.uid()` for Realtime — the app's own API auth is untouched. `partner_own_record` RLS policy re-created as SELECT-only. 10s poll retained as an unconditional fallback; minting is best-effort/non-fatal.
- **Unauthenticated fallback in `profile.tsx` appeared to leak every rider's PII** via `GET /api/delivery/partners` with no token. **Fixed 2026-07-24, but downgraded on investigation** — the actual leak had already been closed by an unrelated prior commit (the route already required `requireAdmin`), so the fallback was only ever getting a 401. What was real: the fallback was dead code masking a genuine profile-fetch failure behind a silent blank screen — fixed by removing the dead fallback and adding a real "Couldn't load your profile — Retry" error state.
- **Verification/suspension gate only blocked render on `checking`, never on `verified` — a since-suspended/unapproved rider could briefly still view and act on live orders.** Found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit; a direct side effect of the 2026-07-31 cache-seeding fix (Fixed Issues, Medium, "Duplicate fetching"/"No caching" items below) that wasn't caught at the time. **Fixed 2026-07-28 in two parts**, since the originally-proposed one-line fix (`|| !verified` on the render guard) turned out to only close half the gap on closer analysis — `verified` is seeded from the exact same cache as `checking`, so in the specific "was verified, got suspended while backgrounded" scenario both are stale-true together and a guard on `!verified` alone wouldn't have blocked anything. (1) `lib/useRiderVerificationGate.ts` gained an `AppState.addEventListener("change", ...)` listener (same pattern already used by `home.tsx`'s location-tracking handoff) that forces `checking` back to `true` and re-runs `evaluate()` on every foreground transition — this is the fix that actually closes the "suspended while backgrounded" window, since foregrounding doesn't fire `useFocusEffect` on its own (the screen was never blurred by React Navigation) and the existing 30s poll/focus-triggered checks don't run while backgrounded either. The ordinary in-session screen-to-screen bounce (Details → Status → Documents) never triggers an `AppState` transition, so the original cache-seeding fix's fast-paint behavior is untouched. (2) `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` and `app/delivery/[orderId].tsx` both added `|| !verified` to their render guards anyway — this closes a real, separate, narrower gap: the shared cache (`riderVerificationCache.ts`) is a single module-level object written by *any* screen's `evaluate()` call regardless of gate mode, so a `require-pending` screen (e.g. `pending-verification.tsx`) populating it with `verified: false` could leave a stale `false` sitting there for whatever `require-verified` screen reads it next, which would previously render for one frame with `checking` already false and no verified check at all. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` (0 problems, same clean baseline as before); not click-tested live (would need a real backgrounded app + a live admin suspension to observe the transition).
- **Regression from the fix above, found the same day during a full regression review: the new `AppState`-triggered re-check ran a pre-existing buggy branch far more often, kicking already-verified riders out on ordinary connectivity blips.** `evaluate()`'s network-error catch branch (pre-existing, not part of the fix above) redirected to `/pending-verification` on *any* thrown error from `checkRiderVerification` — including a transient network blip — without checking whether the rider was already confirmed verified. Before the AppState fix, this branch only ran on mount/focus/the 30s poll; adding a 4th trigger on every single app-foreground transition (one of the most connectivity-fragile moments — device waking up, Wi-Fi/cellular still reconnecting) meaningfully raised how often a legitimate rider got bounced by a mere blip instead of an actual verification failure. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with a new `verifiedRef` (mirrors the `verified` state via a `useEffect`, since `evaluate()`'s `useCallback` only has `mode` in its deps and would otherwise close over a stale `verified` value) — the network-error catch branch now only redirects if `!verifiedRef.current`, i.e. the rider was never actually confirmed verified this session; a rider already confirmed verified now rides out a transient blip instead of being bounced. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Same-day regression review, `home.tsx`: the new `canGoOnline` gate's comment inaccurately claimed the same precedence as `isRiderVerified()`.** `isRiderVerified()` is an OR (`is_approved === true` alone is sufficient, with `status === "active"` as a fallback); `canGoOnline = driverStatus === "active" && isApproved` is a stricter AND. Not a functional bug — the AND is the safer default for gating a live, order-accepting action — but the comment was misleading. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by correcting the comment to describe the actual (intentionally stricter) precedence instead of claiming parity with `isRiderVerified()`.
- **`billing-info.tsx`'s photo upload could fail silently after the file was already uploaded to storage, orphaning it with no alert.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup — the `try { ... } finally { ... }` around `pickProfileImage` had no `catch`, so a failure in the follow-up `PATCH /delivery-partner/photo-urls` call after `uploadRiderImage` already succeeded propagated unhandled: no alert, spinner just cleared, image orphaned in storage with nothing pointing at it). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by wrapping just that follow-up call in its own `try/catch`, mirroring `profile.tsx`'s already-correct `handlePickImage` pattern — on failure, shows `Alert.alert("Save failed", ...)` explicitly telling the rider the photo uploaded but wasn't saved, so they know to retry (the storage-upload failure branch just above it already had this treatment; only the DB-save step was missing it). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `expo lint` still 0 problems (same baseline, no new findings, none in this file).
- **Existing, correctly-registered riders (including approved ones) were rejected at login with "This phone number is not registered as a delivery partner."** Found and reported live 2026-07-31. Root-caused via direct production testing (live session tokens pulled from the DB, hitting `https://near-and-now-backend.vercel.app/delivery-partner/profile` directly): `getProfile`'s `app_users` lookup (`.select('id, name, email, phone, created_at').eq('id', req.riderId!).single()`) intermittently returned nothing in production — confirmed via repeated live curl tests showing the response missing `name`/`email`/`id`/`created_at` entirely while the parallel `delivery_partners` query (already `.maybeSingle()`) succeeded every time with the exact same `supabaseAdmin` client, for a rider account independently verified to be correctly linked and intact (`app_users.id` === `delivery_partners.user_id`, both fetched cleanly via a direct Supabase REST call using the same service-role key from `.env`) — apparently a transient production-only flakiness (cold start / connection-pool race), not a permanent misconfiguration, since a later identical request against a freshly-issued token for the same account returned the complete profile fine. The client's own `otp.tsx::hasRegisteredRiderProfile()` required *both* `profile.user_id` (always present, sourced directly from the authenticated session's own riderId) *and* `profile.name` (sourced from the flaky `app_users` join) — so any transient failure of that one query permanently rejected an otherwise-fully-valid login. **Fixed 2026-07-31** on both ends: (1) `hasRegisteredRiderProfile()` now only requires `profile.user_id`, which doesn't depend on the flaky join, and no longer swallows a genuine network/server error into the same misleading "not registered" message (removed the blanket `try/catch { return false }` so a real failure now surfaces as "Something went wrong. Try again." instead). (2) Backend `getProfile` switched the `app_users` query from `.single()` to `.maybeSingle()` (matching the already-more-forgiving `delivery_partners` query) and now logs the error via `console.error` instead of silently discarding it, so a recurrence is actually diagnosable in Vercel's function logs. **The underlying cause of the transient `app_users` read failure itself is still unexplained** — the fix makes login resilient to it, but if it recurs, the rider's name/email may still render blank on the Profile tab until the root cause is found (watch the new log line). Confirmed live end-to-end after the fix: login succeeded on retry (initial post-fix retry still failed with the same message, traced to Expo Go serving a stale Metro bundle — a full `expo start -c` restart resolved it, confirming the fix itself was correct). `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and rider app; backend vitest 17/17 passing.

### Medium

- **Approval checks for going-online (`updateStatus`) and offer-accept (`acceptOffer`) were hand-rolled separately rather than sharing one gate function.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by extracting a shared `getRiderApprovalState(riderId)` (returns `is_approved`/`is_online`/`status` from a single `delivery_partners` lookup) in `deliveryPartner.controller.ts` — `requireApprovedRider` (already used by `rejectOrder`/`markPickedUp`/`markDelivered`) is now a thin wrapper over it, `updateStatus` now calls `requireApprovedRider` instead of its own inline query, and `acceptOffer` now calls `getRiderApprovalState` directly instead of its own separate inline query. One query implementation instead of three. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17).
- **Rider "Earnings" screen hardcoded a 15% commission client-side with no backend-provided payout field.** Investigating this uncovered a much bigger gap: `delivery_partners_payouts`/`store_payouts`/`platform_ledger_entries` existed as real tables but nothing in the backend had ever written to them, and customer tips were silently dropped or folded into `notes`. **Fixed 2026-07-25** by building the first real payout write path: riders are paid a flat ₹17/order plus 100% of any tip (per confirmed business model), written by a new idempotent `payRiderForDeliveredOrder()` helper called from both `markDelivered` and the admin panel's manual status-override path (a same-session follow-up fix, since the manual override could otherwise silently skip payout entirely). New `tip_amount` column and migration `20260818000000`, applied to the live DB. **Deliberately out of scope, by explicit user decision:** `store_payouts`/`platform_ledger_entries` remain unimplemented — only the rider/tip slice was built.
- **`GET /delivery-partner/orders` was fully unpaginated — a long-tenured rider's "completed" tab pulled their entire order history on every screen focus.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04**, but deliberately opt-in only: `earnings.tsx` computes a lifetime "Total Earnings" figure by summing every returned order client-side, so defaulting to a capped page server-side would have silently under-reported it for any rider past the cap — a correctness regression worse than the original performance issue. The backend now accepts optional `?limit` (capped at 200, `.range()`-based) and `?offset` params, applied only when passed, preserving the original unbounded query as the default. `orders.tsx` (which has no such aggregate) was updated to pass `?limit=50`; `earnings.tsx` was deliberately left unbounded — a real fix for its case would need a server-side aggregate endpoint decoupled from the list payload, out of scope for this pass. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend` and the rider app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17).
- **No document image upload for rider verification.** **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — `app/documents.tsx` is now a full-featured upload screen; a vehicle-photo upload was also added to the profile screen separately.
- **Double-tap on "Accept offer" could fire two concurrent accept requests** — `handleAcceptOffer`'s disabled state relied only on React state (`accepting`), which doesn't take effect until the next re-render commits, so a fast double-tap could land both taps before the button actually disabled. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a synchronous `acceptingRef` guard checked and reset around the async request, independent of React's render timing. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean, after running `npm install` — `node_modules` didn't exist yet in this checkout) and `expo lint` (same 4 pre-existing errors as already documented in Build/Test Status, no new ones in the changed file).
- **A realtime-channel subscribe/teardown race could orphan a Supabase channel subscription** — the offers-channel effect started an async `getSession()` call before creating the channel, with no guard against `isOnline` flipping false (or the effect re-running for another dep) while that await was in flight. If it did, the `.then()` callback still ran afterward, creating and subscribing a channel that got assigned to the ref only *after* the effect's own cleanup had already run and found nothing to unsubscribe — an orphaned, live subscription causing duplicated haptic buzzes/fetches for new offers until the next toggle happened to clean it up. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with the same `cancelled` guard the sibling `rider-status` effect in the same file already used for the identical pattern — confirmed that precedent existed before writing the fix, so this exactly matches an already-proven approach in this codebase. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` (same pre-existing problems, no new ones).
- **Rider profile screen showed a hardcoded `4.8` star rating for every rider, with no backend field behind it.** Confirmed via a full schema search: no `rating` column exists anywhere on `delivery_partners`, and no separate rider-rating/review table exists at all — this isn't a "wire it to the real field" fix, since there is no real field, and building a full rate-your-rider feature (customer-facing UI, new column, aggregation logic) was out of scope for this fix. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing the fabricated stat entirely (mirroring the same-session precedent of dropping the admin dashboard's fabricated "X in stock" badge rather than keep faking a number) — the stats row now shows the two real, backend-derived values (`totalDeliveries`, `totalEarnings`) without a third slot standing in for data that doesn't exist. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems as already documented in Build/Test Status, none in `profile.tsx`).
- **No GPS accuracy or staleness check before sending a heartbeat/location update** — `sendLocation()` forwarded whatever `accuracy` the OS reported with no filtering (e.g. a 500m-accuracy cold-GPS-lock fix sent as-is), and `sendLastKnownLocation()` forwarded `Location.getLastKnownPositionAsync()`'s result unconditionally with no check against `loc.timestamp`, so an hours-old cached fix could be re-sent and shown on a customer's live tracking map as current — `driver_locations.updated_at` reflects server receipt time, not when the GPS fix was actually taken, so a stale fix looked perfectly "fresh" downstream with no rider-side warning either. **Fixed 2026-07-27**, enforced once server-side (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `updateLocation`) rather than duplicated across this app's two independent location-sending paths (`home.tsx`'s foreground tracking and `lib/backgroundLocationTask.ts`'s background task, both POST to the same endpoint): fixes with `accuracy > 100m` or a `timestamp` more than 2 minutes old now get skipped (the coordinates aren't written to `driver_locations`), while `last_seen` (the separate rider-connectivity heartbeat) still updates regardless, since that's an independent concern. Required adding a `timestamp` field (the GPS fix's own capture time) to both client call sites' POST bodies, since the backend previously had no way to know how old a fix actually was. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both the backend and rider app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean; not click-tested live (would need a real device with a controllably stale/inaccurate GPS fix).
- **Rider avatar upload bucket was capped at 2MB, not 5MB like every other image bucket — and turned out to not exist at all.** Requested as a quick fix (2026-07-27): a migration (`20260902000000`) bumped the `rider-avatars` bucket's `file_size_limit` to 5MB, matching `store-images`/`store-owner-images`/`delivery_partner_image`/etc. **Live-verified the same day (asked to double-check every recent migration against production) that this was a silent no-op** — `SELECT * FROM storage.buckets WHERE id='rider-avatars'` returned zero rows; that bucket had never existed live. Investigating why turned up a real bug: the backend's `PATCH /delivery-partner/profile-image` route (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `updateProfileImage`) still uploaded to `'rider-avatars'` on every call — a broken, guaranteed-to-500 endpoint, since the bucket it targets didn't exist. Confirmed via a full 4-repo grep that nothing in the current rider app calls this route anymore (it migrated to direct-to-Storage upload via `lib/storage.ts` → `delivery_partner_image`, plus `PATCH /delivery-partner/photo-urls`) — an initial pass deleted the dead route/controller method entirely on that basis. **Corrected same day per explicit instruction: keep the endpoint, create the bucket instead.** Restored `updateProfileImage` and its route, and added migration `20260907000000` to actually create the `rider-avatars` bucket (public, 5MB, `image/jpeg`/`png`/`webp`, service_role-only write policy — matching every other profile-photo bucket in this schema). Applied to production and verified live: the bucket now exists with the correct size limit, mime types, and policy. The route now works if ever called again, even though the current app's own upload path still goes through `delivery_partner_image` instead. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and rider app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean.
- **Orders screen status badges mismatched what the backend actually emits.** `STATUS_CONFIG` (`app/(tabs)/orders.tsx`) had an entry for `en_route_delivery`, a status `mapDbStatusToRider()` (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`) never actually returns, while `picking_up`/`picked_up` — which the backend does emit — had no config at all and fell through to the generic default: the raw status string as the label, with an unstyled help-circle icon. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by replacing `en_route_delivery` with real `picking_up`/`picked_up` entries ("Picking Up"/"moped" and "Delivering"/"truck-delivery", the latter reusing the dead entry's icon and warning color scheme), and added a comment pointing at `mapDbStatusToRider()` as the source of truth so the two don't drift apart again. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems already documented in Build/Test Status, none in `orders.tsx`).
- **No admin notification/audit trail on rider profile-edit requests.** `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`'s `handleSave` PATCHed `app_users`/`delivery_partners` directly and took effect immediately, with zero request-tracking or admin-facing notification — unlike verification documents, which already go through a review queue. **Fixed 2026-07-27** with the same full admin-approval workflow already built for the store-owner app (explicit choice, for consistency): new `rider_profile_change_requests` table (migration `20260908000000`, service-role-only, with the one-pending-per-rider partial unique index built in from the start this time, having had to add it as a follow-up fix for the store version) holding a JSON diff of old/new values — `name` lives on `app_users`, `email`/`address` on `delivery_partners`, so the diff/apply logic touches both. New rider-facing endpoints `GET`/`POST /delivery-partner/profile-change-request` (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`) let the rider submit a request (diffed server-side against both tables); resubmitting while pending replaces the existing row via update-first-then-insert-with-retry-on-23505, same pattern as the store version. The old `PATCH /delivery-partner/profile` route and its `updateProfile` controller method were removed entirely rather than reduced — unlike `updateStore` (which kept image fields), this endpoint had no other purpose once name/email/address moved behind review. Admin-facing `GET/POST /api/delivery/partners/profile-change-requests(/:id/review)` (`delivery.controller.ts`, gated on the existing `delivery_partners.*` permission) list pending requests and apply an approval's diff via the row-locked `review_rider_profile_change_request()` function (mirroring `review_store_profile_change_request` exactly, including its `ALREADY_REVIEWED:<status>` exception convention), or record a reject with a required reason. `notificationService.notifyRiderProfileChangeReviewed()` persists+pushes the outcome to the rider. Admin panel gained `RiderProfileChangeRequestsPage.tsx` (near-identical to the store version) wired into `AdminRoutes.tsx` and the sidebar (both profile-change-request sidebar entries renamed to disambiguate "Store" vs "Rider" now that both exist). Rider app: `profile.tsx` now submits to the request endpoint, shows a "Changes pending admin review" banner, and polls every 20s (a plain `setInterval`, since this app has no `useSmartPoll`-equivalent hook) while a request is pending. One route-ordering bug caught before it shipped: `GET /partners/profile-change-requests` was initially registered *after* `GET /partners/:partnerId` in `delivery.routes.ts` — same path depth, so Express would have matched the param route first and treated "profile-change-requests" as a `partnerId`; moved above it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend, admin, and rider app, `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems, none new), the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), and a full admin `vite build`, all clean. Migration applied to production and verified live (table, function, and unique index all present; function execute grant confirmed `service_role`-only). Not click-tested live.
  - **Same-day deep-dive review found and fixed 2 more bugs:** (1) **Severe** — `listRiderProfileChangeRequests` used a PostgREST embed (`select('*, app_users(name))`), but `rider_id`'s only real foreign key is to `delivery_partners(user_id)`, not `app_users(id)` — confirmed live via a direct `curl` to the REST API that this exact query returns `PGRST200`/"no relationship found", meaning the admin review page would have 500'd on every single load, unable to even show the pending list. Fixed by dropping the embed for a plain second query batch-keyed by the same `rider_id` UUIDs, re-verified working live afterward. (2) The `profile_change_request` `admin_notifications` type (used by both this feature and the earlier store version, sharing the same string) was never added to `notificationLink.ts`'s click-routing switch, falling into the `default: null` case — clicking either notification in the bell dropdown just marked it read with no navigation at all. This gap predates today and was missed in the store version's own deep-dive review too. Fixed by disambiguating on `data.storeId` vs `data.riderId` and routing to the correct review page. Neither fix needed a new migration. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and admin, the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), and a full admin `vite build`, all clean.
- **Home screen silently swallowed repeated polling failures** — empty `catch {}` blocks around both `fetchOffers()`/`available-orders` and `fetchActiveOrder()`/`orders?status=active` meant a rider online during a degraded-but-reachable backend saw the exact same "No orders right now" as someone genuinely idle, with no way to tell the difference. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by tracking consecutive failures per poll in refs (not state, so a single transient blip doesn't flicker a warning) and surfacing a distinct "Having trouble reaching the server" empty state once either poll fails twice in a row; a single success on either poll clears it immediately, since one working endpoint means the backend is reachable. `apiFetch` was confirmed to always throw on a non-2xx response (never resolves with an unthrown `success: false`), so tracking failures via `catch` alone is sufficient — no separate success-flag case to handle. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems as already documented in Build/Test Status — one round introduced 2 new unescaped-apostrophe errors in the new copy, caught by re-running lint and fixed with `&apos;` before finalizing).
- **The same gap existed on the Orders and Earnings tabs, missed by the Home-screen fix above.** `app/(tabs)/orders.tsx`'s `fetchOrders` and `app/(tabs)/earnings.tsx`'s `fetchCompleted` both swallowed fetch errors into an empty `catch {}`, then unconditionally rendered the same "No active orders"/"No past orders"/"No earnings yet" empty state regardless of whether the request actually failed — a rider with a genuine connectivity/backend problem had no way to tell it apart from having zero orders, with only an easy-to-miss pull-to-refresh as an implicit retry. **Fixed 2026-07-30.** Used a simpler pattern than Home's ref-based consecutive-failure counter, since neither screen polls on an interval (both only fetch on mount/tab-change and screen focus, per `useFocusEffect`) — a single boolean `loadError` state per screen, set `true` in the `catch`/cleared on every successful response, is sufficient without needing debounce-against-flicker logic. The empty state now branches on `loadError`: same `wifi-alert`/`Colors.warning` treatment as Home's degraded state, but with copy that doesn't overpromise a background retry ("Couldn't load orders/earnings — Check your connection and try again") plus an explicit "Try Again" button calling the same fetch function, since — unlike Home — there's no continuous poll quietly retrying in the background here; pull-to-refresh on an empty list is a valid but not always-discoverable affordance on its own. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both files.
- **Optimistic UI updates with no rollback on failure could silently diverge from server state — notifications and preferences.** `app/notifications.tsx`'s `markAllRead`/`markOneRead` and `app/notification-preferences.tsx`'s `toggle` all updated local state optimistically, then fired the actual API call with `.catch(() => {})` and no rollback. **Concrete effect:** if `markAllRead`'s `PUT /notifications/read-all` failed, the notifications screen showed everything read, but Home's bell badge (an independent, always-fresh server query, refetched via `useFocusEffect` on returning to Home) still showed the real, non-zero unread count — a visible mismatch with nothing explaining why. Same risk for a preference toggle silently not persisting. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a snapshot-and-revert-on-catch pattern, matching the closest existing precedent in this codebase (`billing-info.tsx`'s "don't commit the optimistic value until success is confirmed" idiom, generalized here to an explicit revert since these two screens need the immediate-feedback responsiveness a real optimistic update gives, unlike a single photo upload) — all three handlers now snapshot the prior state before applying the optimistic change, and restore it in the `catch` alongside an `Alert.alert(...)` (already the established in-app error-signal mechanism, no toast library exists in this repo) explaining the save failed, e.g. "Couldn't mark all as read — Please check your connection and try again." `notifications.tsx` needed a new `Alert` import; `notification-preferences.tsx` already had one from its permission-flow alerts. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both files; not click-tested live (would need a forced network failure mid-toggle to observe the revert).
- **`documents.tsx`: the document number that's format-validated wasn't the same string that got saved.** `saveOne` validated the trimmed input against `number.toUpperCase()` (so a lowercase PAN like `abcde1234f` passed the uppercase-only regex), but the value actually sent to `saveVerificationDocument` was the original, non-uppercased `number` — the string that was checked and the string that got persisted were two different values, so a rider could end up with a saved PAN/Aadhaar number in a case that never actually passed the format check as-stored. `autoCapitalize="characters"` on the input is only a keyboard display hint, not a guarantee — paste and some autofill/keyboard paths can still land lowercase characters in state regardless of it. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by normalizing once — `number = numbers[key]?.trim().toUpperCase()` — and using that single normalized value for both the format check and the save payload, instead of computing the uppercased form separately just for validation and discarding it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **Background poll and GPS watcher on the delivery screen kept running after the delivery was marked complete.** `app/delivery/[orderId].tsx`'s 10s `loadSequence` poll (lines 329-333) and the foreground `watchPositionAsync` GPS subscription (started in the mount effect, lines 303-327) were both scoped only to mount/`token`, torn down only on unmount — no dependency on `order.status` at all — so a rider lingering on the "Order Delivered!" screen kept polling the pickup-sequence endpoint and streaming GPS every 10s indefinitely for no functional reason (battery/network waste, not otherwise user-visible). This was the first terminal-state teardown of any kind in this file — the existing `isCompleted` check (`order.status === "order_delivered" || order.status === "completed"`) only ever existed in the render body, never referenced by either effect. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by lifting that check into a `isDeliveryComplete` value computed from state (available before the render body's own null-guard, since effects run regardless of what render later returns) and adding a new effect keyed on it that clears `pollRef`/removes `locationSubRef` once true — the existing poll-start and GPS-subscribe effects are otherwise untouched, so an in-progress delivery's behavior is unchanged. The render body's own `isCompleted` const was pointed at the same shared value instead of recomputing the identical check a second time. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live (would need a real completed delivery lingering on-screen to observe the poll/GPS actually stopping).
- **Active-order poll ran every 6s regardless of online/offline status** — gated only on `!token`, `fetchActiveOrder()` polled at the same 6s cadence whether the rider was actively online or deliberately offline, unlike the *offers* poll (gated on `isOnline`). A code comment already documented why it can't simply stop polling while offline (a simulation or manual admin dispatch can still assign an order to an offline driver), so the fix isn't to stop polling, just to slow it way down when there's no urgency. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by making the interval itself conditional — 6s while online (unchanged responsiveness for a genuinely active rider), 45s while offline (a manual offline-dispatch is a rare admin action, not something needing near-instant detection, so this still catches it within a reasonable window without draining battery/network at full cadence for a rider who's deliberately idle). The effect already re-runs on `isOnline` changes, so toggling online/offline naturally tears down and recreates the interval at the right cadence with no extra wiring needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems, none new).
- **"Ignore offer" was client-state-only, unpersisted** — `ignoredOfferIds` (`app/(tabs)/home.tsx`) lived purely in `useState`, so any app restart reset it to empty and a previously-ignored offer could reappear in the list. Considered and rejected a server-side fix (e.g. a `driver_order_offers` "rejected" status): ignoring is a soft, personal, this-device-only dismissal, not a real business-level rejection — other riders still see the offer, and it still re-expires/reassigns server-side on its own timeout — so persisting it in the offer's own state machine would risk colliding with the real accept/expire/reassign transitions on that table. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by persisting the ignored-id set to `AsyncStorage` (already a dependency, same pattern used by `session.ts`) under a new `rider_ignored_offer_ids` key: one `useEffect` loads and parses it into the existing `ignoredOfferIds` state on mount (falling back to an empty set on any read/parse failure), and a second persists the set back to storage on every change, guarded by a `ignoredOffersLoadedRef` so the persist effect can't fire (and overwrite real storage with an empty set) before the initial load has actually completed. The existing pruning logic inside `fetchOffers` (dropping ignored ids no longer present in the server's offer list) and the Ignore-button handler were untouched — both already only ever call `setIgnoredOfferIds`, so persistence follows automatically. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems as already documented in Build/Test Status, none new).
- **`vehicle_type`/`vehicle_number` were never rendered as text anywhere in the rider profile UI**, despite the backend already returning both (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `getProfile`) — `vehicle_type` was declared on the app's `Profile` type but unused in any JSX, and `vehicle_number` wasn't even in the type at all, confirming riders/admins reviewing rider identity had no way to see either value in the app itself. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding `vehicle_number` to the `Profile` type and rendering both as read-only `FieldRow`s (matching the existing "Phone" row's read-only pattern — editing either requires the separate dedicated vehicle-type/document-upload flow, not this simple name/email/address edit form) in `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`'s vehicle info card, right below the vehicle photo. `vehicle_type` is title-cased for display (raw values are lowercase, e.g. `'scooty'`); missing values show "Not set"/"Not provided" rather than blank. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems, none new); confirmed both icon names used (`motorbike`, `card-text-outline`) exist in the installed `MaterialCommunityIcons` glyph map. Not click-tested live.
- **Hardcoded admin support phone number duplicated in two places** (`app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s pending-verification banner and `app/pending-verification.tsx`'s "Need help?" link), both showing a single number with no name attached. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new `ADMIN_CONTACTS` list in `constants/config.ts` (name + phone per admin, plus a `formatPhoneForDisplay()` helper), listing the 3 admins riders can actually reach (Tias Mondal, Rounak Jana, Raj Shaw — the user explicitly excluded a 4th active super_admin, Parthiv Dasgupta, from this list). Both screens now render one tappable `tel:` row per admin (name + number) instead of a single bare number. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems, none new; `home.tsx` had zero warnings both before and after).
- **`lib/authService.ts` was entirely dead code** — confirmed zero importers anywhere in the app via a full repo grep; its unused `sendOTP`/`verifyOTP` functions logged raw phone numbers and API URLs to the console, a latent PII-in-logs risk if it had ever been wired back in. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by deleting the file outright rather than fixing the logging in place, since nothing calls it (the app's real OTP flow lives in `otp.tsx`/`phone.tsx` via a different path). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 23 pre-existing problems, none new).
- **`components/MapViewWrapper.tsx` was unused dead weight** — confirmed zero importers anywhere in the app; the app bundled `react-native-maps` and a Google Maps API key for no actual in-app map usage (it only deep-links out to the native Maps app for navigation). **Fixed 2026-07-31** by deleting the component outright, uninstalling the now-unused `react-native-maps` dependency (`npm uninstall`, updates lockfile), removing `app.json`'s `android.config.googleMaps` block (its `apiKey` was still a literal, never-replaced `"GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY_PLACEHOLDER"` string), removing the dead `GOOGLE_MAPS_CONFIG` export and its `validateConfig()` warning check from `constants/config.ts`, and removing the two now-meaningless Google Maps env var lines from `.env.example`. This also cleared 3 lint findings for free (`react/display-name` x2, `no-require-imports` x1), dropping the rider app's lint baseline from 23/4 to 20/2. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint`; confirmed via grep that no reference to `MapViewWrapper`, `react-native-maps`, or `GOOGLE_MAPS_CONFIG` remains anywhere in the app.
- **Double-submit race in `otp.tsx`'s verify flow** — same root cause as the earlier-fixed accept-offer bug: `handleVerify`'s guard checked React state (`loading`), which doesn't take effect until the next render commits, so a fast double-tap on "Verify Code" (or the auto-submit `useEffect` firing alongside a manual tap) could pass the check twice and fire two concurrent `verify-otp` requests before the button actually disabled. **Fixed 2026-07-31** with the same synchronous `verifyingRef` guard pattern used for the accept-offer fix, checked and reset around the async request independent of React's render timing. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 20/2 baseline, none new, none in `otp.tsx`).
- **Dead `isActivated` session field threaded through every session-save call site.** Confirmed via a full repo grep that it was write-only — set in 4 places (`otp.tsx` x3, `signup.tsx` x1, the latter a hardcoded literal `false` not even sourced from the API response) but never once read from a stored session anywhere in the app; the real approval/verification gate is `resolveAuthenticatedRoute()`'s fresh `checkRiderVerification(token)` backend call, not this cached client-side flag — a genuine trap for anyone assuming it was load-bearing. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by removing the field entirely: from `session.ts`'s `UserSession['user']` type, `otp.tsx`'s `VerifyOtpResponse.user` type, and all 4 write sites. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 20/2 baseline, none new); confirmed via grep that zero references to `isActivated` remain anywhere in the app.
- **3 small lint findings, fixed together (2026-07-31):** (1) raw unescaped `'` in JSX text — `profile.tsx` ("Couldn't load your profile") and `pending-verification.tsx` ("isn't required for cycles or e-bikes") — both switched to `&apos;`. (2) `Array<T>` → `T[]` house-style fix in `delivery/[orderId].tsx`'s `items` type and `otp.tsx`'s `inputsRef` type. (3) `app/_layout.tsx`'s conditional `require("expo-notifications")` — left as-is functionally (it's a deliberate Expo-Go-compatibility guard: a static `import` is hoisted and would always execute regardless of the `isExpoGo` check, loading a module that's unsupported/throws in Expo Go), just given a scoped `eslint-disable-next-line` with a comment explaining why, rather than forcing a static import that would break the guard it exists for. (The other `no-require-imports` finding, in `components/MapViewWrapper.tsx`, was already cleared by that file's earlier deletion.) Dropped the rider app's lint baseline from 20/2 to 15/0. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint`.
- **2 more small lint findings, fixed together (2026-07-31):** (1) `app/documents.tsx`'s dead `saveSession` import — confirmed via grep it's never called in the file (only `getSession` is used); removed. (2) `app/(tabs)/orders.tsx`'s `useFocusEffect` callback listed `tab` as a dependency it doesn't reference directly — removed, since the callback's other dependency, `fetchOrders`, already changes identity whenever `tab` changes (it's declared with `[token, tab]` itself), making the direct listing redundant; the sibling plain `useEffect` two lines above correctly keeps `tab` in its own deps (it also needs to trigger the loading spinner on tab switch, which the focus-effect callback doesn't). Dropped the rider app's lint baseline from 15/0 to 13/0. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint`; confirmed tab-switch refetch behavior is unchanged by tracing both effects' dependency chains.
- **All 13 remaining `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` "missing dependency" warnings, resolved individually (2026-07-31) rather than bulk-suppressed** — per the tracker's own caveat that each needed its own look before assuming safe. 11 of the 13 were animation-only refs (`fadeAnim`/`slideAnim`/`pulseAnim`/`scaleAnim`/`subtitleFade`/`dotsFade`/`logoScale` — all `useRef(new Animated.Value(...)).current`, which React guarantees is referentially stable for the component's lifetime) across `earnings.tsx`, `home.tsx` (x2), `orders.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, `otp.tsx`, `phone.tsx`, `signup.tsx`, `index.tsx`, and `OfflineBanner.tsx` — genuine false positives, fixed by adding the refs to their effects' dependency arrays (zero behavior change, confirmed already-established as safe elsewhere in this same codebase). 2 were `router` (`useRouter()`) in `_layout.tsx` and `index.tsx` — confirmed via grep that `router` is already listed in other effects' deps elsewhere in the same files/app, establishing it's already treated as stable; added the same way. The remaining 2, both in `delivery/[orderId].tsx`, were the one genuinely non-trivial case: `loadSequence` (a `useCallback` whose own deps include `orderId`, so its identity *does* change if the route param ever changed) was missing from two effects that call it — a real, if rare, stale-closure risk (an interval poll or mount-effect silently keeps calling an old `orderId`'s `loadSequence` after navigating to a different order). Fixed by adding `loadSequence` to both effects' deps; this required moving the `loadSequence` `useCallback` declaration to *before* both effects in source order, since a dependency array is evaluated during render (not deferred like the effect body) and referencing an identifier before its `const` initializes throws a temporal-dead-zone `ReferenceError` at runtime — caught before shipping by reasoning through the evaluation order, not by a failed test (this app has no test infrastructure). Dropped the rider app's lint baseline from 13/0 to **0/0** — a fully clean `expo lint` run for the first time this session. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Live bug reported by the user: an existing rider's phone number ("already there in `app_users`/`delivery_partners`") was still routed to "New User Registration" on OTP login.** Traced to `auth.controller.ts`'s role-scoped (shopkeeper/delivery_partner) account lookup, which only ever tried 2 phone formats — exactly what the client sent (always `+91XXXXXXXXXX`) and digits-only-with-the-91-prefix — unlike the customer login flow's own `customerPhoneLookupVariants()`, which tries every plausible stored format (bare 10 digits, with/without `+`, with/without `91`, a leading-`0` variant, etc.) specifically to avoid this exact class of bug. An account whose stored phone happened to be the bare 10 digits (no country code) — e.g. entered a different way than through the app's own signup — could never match either of the 2 formats tried, so a genuinely existing account was invisible to login and got silently routed into signup instead. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by generalizing the customer-only helper (renamed `customerPhoneLookupVariants` → `phoneLookupVariants`, doc comment updated) and switching the role-scoped lookup to match against the full variant set via `.in('phone', variants)` instead of two sequential exact-match attempts; removed the now-dead `normalizePhone()` it replaced. Verified live: re-ran the exact query the new code generates directly against the database and confirmed it now finds a real delivery_partner row that the old 2-format lookup would have missed. `tsc --noEmit` clean, backend vitest suite (17/17) clean.
- **Second, independent bug on the same "Existing User" login path, found immediately after the fix above didn't fully resolve the user's report.** `GET /delivery-partner/profile` merges an `app_users` select (`id, name, email, phone, created_at`) with a `delivery_partners` select that only ever uses `user_id` as a filter, never as a returned column — so the merged response object had `id` but **never** a `user_id` key at all. The rider app's own `hasRegisteredRiderProfile()` (`otp.tsx`, gates the existing-user login branch) checks `Boolean(profile.user_id && profile.name)` — since `profile.user_id` was always `undefined`, this check silently failed for **every** existing rider, 100% of the time, regardless of phone number or account state, always showing "This phone number is not registered as a delivery partner" even for a fully legitimate account. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by adding `user_id: req.riderId!` explicitly to the `getProfile` response — purely additive, no existing consumer could have relied on a conflicting value since the key was never present before. `tsc --noEmit` clean, backend vitest suite (17/17) clean. (Diagnostic logging was also added to `verifySignupTicket()`'s rejection branches — log-only, doesn't change the client-facing error — while investigating a related, separately-reported "signup failed / phone not verified" 403 during the same debugging session, to make the next occurrence of *that* specific error immediately diagnosable from server logs instead of requiring another guessing pass.)
- **In-app location-permission explanation added, so the OS's own "Allow all the time" dialog no longer appears with zero context** — requested directly by the user. Previously `app/(tabs)/home.tsx` called `requestForegroundPermissionsAsync()` then immediately `requestBackgroundPermissionsAsync()` on every mount with no explanation shown first. **Fixed 2026-07-31** with a new `LocationPermissionModal` component (matches the existing `ProfileChangeOutcomeModal` visual pattern), shown once ever (persisted via an AsyncStorage flag so it never reappears after the first time) — since a rider only ever reaches Home after being verified (`resolveAuthenticatedRoute` gates it), this already lands "right after verification" as requested. The modal explains why background access is needed and to select "Allow all the time" on the next screen; tapping "Continue" is what actually triggers the OS permission sequence, which is unchanged otherwise (foreground requested first, background only after, per Apple's own guidance already documented in the surrounding code). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems, same as before).
- **No "Go Offline" button while online — only the top-right Switch could take a rider offline** — reported directly by the user; confirmed the asymmetry: a "Go Online" button appeared in the status banner while offline, but no equivalent existed while online. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by adding a matching "Go Offline" button (red outline, shown only while `isOnline`) right alongside the existing "Go Online" button, calling the same `handleToggle(false)` handler — no changes needed to the toggle logic itself, which already worked correctly regardless of which UI element triggered it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Notification bell badge didn't clear after "Mark all as read"** — reported directly by the user. The backend's `markAllNotificationsAsRead` was already correct (confirmed by reading it — properly flips every notification's `is_read` for the caller's own `recipient_type`/`recipient_id`); the bug was client-side only: `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s bell-badge unread count refreshed on a 30-second poll with no refresh-on-return-to-screen, so marking everything read on the separate notifications screen and navigating back to Home could leave the red badge showing for up to 30 seconds — read by the user as "never clears." **Fixed 2026-07-31** by extracting the fetch into a stable `useCallback` and adding a `useFocusEffect` that refetches the instant the Home tab regains focus, on top of the existing 30s poll (unchanged, still the fallback for staying on the same screen). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **`driver_order_offers`'s anon-open Realtime RLS policy** — `USING (true)`, meaning any anon-key client could subscribe to any `driver_id`'s offer events by supplying that UUID as the channel filter, since real per-row scoping wasn't possible when this table's RLS was first set up (riders had no Supabase Auth session at the time, documented as an accepted tradeoff in migration `20260806000000`). That's since changed: a later, unrelated fix (`20260819000000`) minted each rider a narrowly-scoped Supabase Auth bridge session at login specifically so Realtime's RLS check has a real `auth.uid()` to evaluate, used by the sibling `delivery_partners`/`partner_own_record` policy — this table just hadn't been revisited to use the same mechanism. **Fixed 2026-07-31** with migration `20260915000000`: dropped `anon_read`, added `driver_own_offers` (`auth.uid() IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (... delivery_partners WHERE user_id = driver_id AND auth_user_id = auth.uid())`), scoping each driver to only their own offer rows. Confirmed via a full repo grep that every non-Realtime reader/writer of this table (backend controllers, delivery simulation) already goes through `supabaseAdmin` (service_role, bypasses RLS) — the rider app's own Realtime subscription was the only client-side consumer, so nothing else could break. That subscription (`app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s offers-channel effect) previously used a plain `getSession()` call for the channel filter's driver id, which wouldn't have populated the client's `auth.uid()` — switched it to `restoreRiderRealtimeSession()` (the same call the sibling rider-status effect already makes) so the bridge session is actually restored onto the client before subscribing; without this the tightened policy would have silently zeroed out realtime offer delivery for every rider, leaving only the 6s poll fallback. Applied to production and verified live — an anon-key REST request to `driver_order_offers` now returns `[]` instead of every driver's rows. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (same 13/0 baseline, none new).
- **Rider profile screen duplicated the deliveries/earnings stats already shown (and better contextualized) on the Earnings tab.** Requested directly by the user. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by removing `profile.tsx`'s `totalDeliveries`/`totalEarnings` state, the `fetchStats` call that populated them (an extra `orders?status=completed` fetch on every mount/focus, now eliminated), the stats-row JSX, and its now-unused styles. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **No way for a rider to control which push notifications they receive, and no permission-request explanation before the OS dialog** — requested directly by the user ("have a preference section as well... setup properly"), mirroring the shopkeeper app's existing `NotificationSettings.tsx`. Deliberately **not** a verbatim copy: the shopkeeper version's 4 toggles (`newOrders`/`dailySummary`/`payments`/`systemAlerts`) are saved but never actually checked before sending a push (a known, already-flagged anti-pattern in this same tracker) — building the same thing again for riders would have shipped a second set of non-functional switches. **Fixed 2026-07-28** with only 2 categories that are genuinely enforced server-side: `newOrders` (gates `notifyRiderNewOrder`/`notifyRiderOrderOffer`) and `profileUpdates` (gates `notifyRiderProfileChangeReviewed`), via a new `isRiderNotificationEnabled()` check in `notification.service.ts` that only gates the push send (the in-app notification-list entry still always persists, so a rider who disabled a category still sees it next time they open the inbox) — missing/unset preferences default to enabled. `notifyRiderApproved` is deliberately left ungated, matching `notifyStoreApproved`'s existing precedent that approval notifications aren't optional. New `GET`/`POST /delivery-partner/notifications/preferences` endpoints (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`) read/write `app_users.notification_preferences`, mirroring the shopkeeper app's own preferences endpoint shape. New rider-side screen `app/notification-preferences.tsx` (linked from a new "Notification Preferences" row on the profile screen) shows live permission status with an "Enable" button and the two toggles, disabled until permission is granted — reusing the same conditional `expo-notifications` require-guard already established in `_layout.tsx` for Expo Go compatibility. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` (0 problems) on the rider app, `tsc --noEmit` and the full vitest suite (17/17) on the backend.
- **Shopkeeper app's home screen had no notification bell at all** — reported directly by the user, who assumed one was missing after comparing against the rider app's home screen (which already has one); confirmed via grep the shopkeeper app had zero bell/badge UI anywhere, despite already having a full notification inbox (`app/notification-inbox.tsx`) and backend endpoints (`GET /store-owner/notifications`, mark-read/mark-all-read). **Fixed 2026-07-28** by adding a bell icon with an unread-count badge to `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s header, linking to the existing inbox — refreshed via the same pattern already used elsewhere in this file for `activeOrderCount` (a `useSmartPoll` 15s/30s poll plus a `useFocusEffect` refetch on screen focus), so returning from the inbox after marking notifications read clears the badge immediately with no reappearance, matching the rider app's equivalent fix. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean, `expo lint` (no new problems — same pre-existing baseline), and the full Jest suite (21/21).
- **Vehicle photo on the profile page was editable with no Edit-mode gate** — unlike the main profile picture (already correctly gated on `editing`), the vehicle photo thumbnail in `profile.tsx` was tappable and uploadable at any time. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by mirroring the same `editing`-gated pattern (disabled + hidden camera overlay + "Tap Edit to change"/"Not added" copy when not editing) already used for the profile picture.
- **Rider vehicle number (`delivery_partners.vehicle_number`) was never actually collectible anywhere** — `signup.tsx` never asked for it despite the backend's `signupComplete` already accepting one, and the profile page only ever displayed it read-only, permanently showing "Not provided." Investigated per the user's question ("where are we adding the rider vehicle number and photos?") and confirmed via grep this was a genuine gap on both candidate screens. **Fixed 2026-07-29**, per the user's explicit choice to pair it with the Vehicle Registration (RC) document instead of adding a duplicate field: `deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `saveVerificationDocument` now mirrors the RC document's own number onto `delivery_partners.vehicle_number` whenever it's saved, and `deleteVerificationDocument` clears it back to null if that document is removed (so it can't go stale). `app/documents.tsx`'s RC number field is now labeled "Vehicle Registration Number" with a hint that it also sets the profile's Vehicle Number; `profile.tsx`'s empty-state text now points there ("Set in Documents → Vehicle Registration") instead of a dead-end "Not provided." The admin panel needed zero changes — `DeliveryPage.tsx`'s existing "Vehicle Number" column and `DeliveryDocumentReviewModal.tsx`'s existing per-document number display both already read live off the same columns/table, so the mirrored value shows up automatically. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and full vitest suite (17/17) on the backend, `tsc --noEmit` + `expo lint` (0 problems) on the rider app.
- **Incoming-order offer card was missing most of the information a rider needs to decide whether to accept — no store phone numbers, no per-store item counts, and no customer contact info at all.** Requested directly by the user. `GET /delivery-partner/available-orders` (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`'s `getAvailableOrders`) only ever selected `stores(id, name, address, latitude, longitude)` and `customer_orders(..., delivery_address, delivery_latitude, delivery_longitude, ...)` — no `stores.phone` (present on the table, just never selected — the sibling `getPickupSequence` endpoint already selects it for the *post-accept* screen), no query against `order_items` at all, and no customer name/phone of any kind. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by extending `getAvailableOrders` to mirror `getPickupSequence`'s already-established pattern: added `phone` to the `stores` select;
