added a new `order_items` query (`.in('customer_order_id', orderIds)`) grouped by `${order_id}:${store_id}` into a per-store `items[]`/`item_count`; added a `customer_orders.customer_id` → `app_users(name, phone)` join for the placing customer's own contact info, with `receiver_name`/`receiver_phone` (the "order for someone else" override) taking precedence when set — same precedence `getPickupSequence` already uses for `receiver_*`, just now also falling back to the real customer instead of leaving both null. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx` (rider app): extended the `Offer`/`OfferStore` types with the new optional fields (kept optional so the UI degrades gracefully rather than crashing against an old cached/un-migrated backend response), and the offer card now shows a tappable `tel:` link per store plus "N items to pick," a total-item-count badge next to the store-count badge, and the customer's name + a tappable `tel:` link under the delivery address. **Same-day review also caught and fixed a pre-existing, separate bug in the same card:** the driver→customer distance (`d2cust`) was computed from `offer.stores[0]` (the *first* pickup stop) instead of the *last* one — correct only for single-store orders; for any multi-store order this understated/misrepresented the actual final leg of the route (driver goes through every store in `sequence_number` order before reaching the customer, so the relevant distance is from the last stop, not the first). Fixed by switching to `offer.stores[offer.stores.length - 1]`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both the backend and rider app. Not click-tested live (would need a live pending offer to inspect the rendered card).
- **No way for a rider to upload the 3 vehicle photos (front/side/rear) required for delivery verification, and PDF wasn't accepted as an upload format** — both requested directly by the user, the latter after noticing the backend already allowed `application/pdf` in `ALLOWED_DOC_MIME_TYPES` while the rider app's picker only ever offered images. **Fixed 2026-07-29**:
  - **New required documents:** added `vehicle_photo_front`/`vehicle_photo_side`/`vehicle_photo_rear` to the shared `DOC_TYPES` (`backend/src/utils/deliveryPartnerVerificationDocuments.ts`) and the rider app's `REQUIRED_DOC_KEYS` — unlike Vehicle Registration (RC), these are **not** gated by vehicle type: cycle/e-bike riders skip the RC requirement but still need all 3 vehicle photos, per the user's explicit spec. Added to `DOC_TYPES_WITH_NO_NUMBER_FIELD` (image-only, no document-number field, same treatment as the back-side identity docs).
  - **Postgres function updated:** migration `20260916000000` bumps `mark_rider_verification_submitted_if_ready`'s required-document counts from 6/7 to 9/10 (cycle-e-bike/bike-scooty) to account for the 3 new mandatory docs. Applied to production and verified live (confirmed the deployed function body reflects the new counts).
  - **PDF upload support added client-side:** `app/documents.tsx` now offers a choice (new `expo-document-picker` dependency, installed via `npx expo install` for SDK-54 compatibility) between "Choose Image" and "Upload PDF" for identity/legal documents (Aadhaar, PAN, Driving License, RC), via a small picker modal matching this app's existing centered-card modal convention (not a full animated bottom sheet, to keep the diff scoped) — mirroring the shopkeeper app's `upload-documents.tsx`, which already had this exact feature built. The 3 vehicle photo slots deliberately skip the picker and go straight to the image picker (a PDF makes no sense for an actual photo), with their own narrower "JPG, PNG, WEBP" disclaimer instead of the identity-docs' "...+ PDF" one. Added a PDF preview chip (file icon + filename, same as the shopkeeper app) since `<Image>` can't render a PDF's own content.
  - **Old single vehicle photo, removed:** per the user's explicit choice ("remove it"), the profile page's old single `vehicle_image_url` upload was deleted entirely (state, handler, JSX row, styles) now that vehicle photos live on the verification page as 3 required, admin-reviewed documents instead — also removed the now-fully-dead `uploadVehicleImage` helper from `lib/storage.ts`.
  - **Admin wiring:** per the user's explicit choice (full admin approve/reject review, same as every other verification doc), confirmed both `DeliveryDocumentReviewModal.tsx` and its backing endpoints (`getDeliveryPartnerVerificationDocuments`/`reviewDeliveryPartnerVerificationDocument`) already iterate the shared `DOC_TYPES` array generically — adding the 3 new types to that one shared file was sufficient to wire up admin review with zero endpoint changes. Added their labels to the modal's local `DOC_LABELS` copy and the shared `admin/src/utils/docLabels.ts`, and suppressed the modal's "No number provided" line specifically for these 3 image-only types (previously would have shown confusingly for every photo since they have no number field at all).
  - Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and full vitest suite (17/17) on the backend, `tsc --noEmit` + `expo lint` (0 problems) on the rider app, and `tsc --noEmit` + a full `vite build` (clean) on the admin panel.
- **Follow-up, same day: the 3 vehicle photo slots didn't accept PDF** — when PDF support was added to the documents page, the vehicle photo slots were deliberately routed straight to the image picker (reasoned that a PDF doesn't make sense for an actual vehicle photo), but the user asked for PDF there too. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing that special-casing entirely: `handleUploadPress` now shows the same "Choose Image / Upload PDF" picker sheet for all document types including the 3 vehicle photos, and the narrower photo-only disclaimer was removed in favor of the single "JPG, PNG, WEBP, PDF" disclaimer used everywhere else. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).
- **Live bug reported by the user: tapping back from the documents page to the pending-verification page immediately bounced back to documents again** — `pending-verification.tsx`'s `checkApprovalNow` (run on mount, every 10s poll, and every realtime update) auto-redirected to `/documents` any time `documentsUploaded` was false, which is exactly the normal state for a rider still mid-upload who deliberately navigated back to check their status. **Fixed 2026-07-29** by removing that auto-redirect entirely — the screen's existing "Upload Documents" button is now the only way it sends a rider to `/documents`; the poll/realtime check now only ever forwards on genuine verification (`result.verified`).
- **Broader ask from the same report: free back-and-forth navigation between signup details, documents, and verification status for any rider who has completed signup but isn't yet approved** — previously each of these 3 screens was reachable only in one direction (signup → documents → pending-verification), with no way back to signup at all once submitted, and no shared way to jump between them. **Fixed 2026-07-29**: new shared `components/VerificationNavBar.tsx` (Details / Documents / Status pills, `router.replace` between them since they're peer screens, not a drill-down stack) added to the top of `documents.tsx` and `pending-verification.tsx`. `signup.tsx` gained a **read-only view mode**: when reached via direct navigation (not fresh off OTP verify, i.e. no `phone`/`signupTicket` params) with a session where `needsSignupCompletion` is already `false`, it fetches the rider's saved profile and renders the same Name/Email/Address/Vehicle Type fields as disabled/read-only (`editable={false}`, muted styling, vehicle-type buttons non-interactive) instead of a blank editable form, with the nav bar shown and the submit/"Go back" (which clears the session — must not be reachable in this mode) buttons hidden, replaced by a note that changes are only possible post-verification via the Profile page. Pre-signup-completion access to documents/pending-verification remains blocked exactly as before — that gate is enforced separately by `_layout.tsx`'s existing `needsSignupCompletion` redirect effect (forces back to `/signup` for any other segment while true), untouched by this change. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems, after fixing 2 new unescaped-apostrophe errors caught by the same lint run).
- **Follow-up, same day: an already-approved rider missing the newly-required vehicle photos had no way to be caught** — `mark_rider_verification_submitted_if_ready` only re-checks completeness when a document is uploaded/deleted, so a rider approved *before* the 3 vehicle photos became required would stay `is_approved`/`status='active'` forever with no re-check ever firing again, even though they no longer meet the current requirements. Requested directly by the user. **Fixed 2026-07-29** with a new `demoteRiderIfDocsIncomplete()` check (`deliveryPartner.controller.ts`), called on every `GET /delivery-partner/profile` (which the app polls constantly via the verification gate and home screen) — if an approved rider's uploaded-document count falls short of the current required count (derived from `DOC_TYPES.length`, adjusted for the vehicle-type-conditional RC document, so it can never drift out of sync with the doc list itself), it flips `is_approved: false`, `approved_at`/`approved_by: null`, `status: 'pending_verification'`, `is_online: false`, and clears `verification_submitted_at` — the exact same fields the admin panel's own "Revoke" action sets (`DeliveryPage.tsx`'s `toggleApproval`), so admin and rider app can never disagree about this rider's state. Also notifies admins so the demotion isn't silent. Self-limiting: once demoted, `is_approved` is already false, so later calls bail out on the very first check with no repeated writes. The rider app's existing route gate (`resolveAuthenticatedRoute`/`useRiderVerificationGate`) then naturally redirects them to `/documents` to upload the missing photos, with no further client-side change needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and full vitest suite (17/17); confirmed live that no currently-approved rider exists yet in production, so this is dormant until it's actually needed, not already firing on real accounts.
- **Home screen's online-toggle gate checked only `status`, not `is_approved` — a second, looser notion of "can this rider work" than the shared verification hook's `isRiderVerified()`.** Found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit. `app/(tabs)/home.tsx` fetched its own copy of the profile and gated the online switch purely on `driverStatus !== "active"`, never checking `is_approved` the way `lib/riderVerification.ts`'s `isRiderVerified()` does — every known mutation path (admin revoke, `demoteRiderIfDocsIncomplete`) keeps `status` and `is_approved` in lockstep today, but this was a second, independent check rather than relying on that always holding. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — both of Home's existing lightweight profile fetches (mount effect and the "poll while not active" effect) now also capture `is_approved` into a new `isApproved` state; a new `canGoOnline = driverStatus === "active" && isApproved` derived value replaces the bare `driverStatus === "active"` check at all three call sites (`handleToggle`, the online-switch's `disabled`/`thumbColor`, and the "Go online" prompt banner). Deliberately scoped to reusing the fields Home's existing fetches already return, rather than pulling in `useRiderVerificationGate`'s full hook (which would also fetch verification documents and run its own independent poll/`AppState`/focus listeners, duplicating network calls against the same shared cache the tabs-layout instance already maintains). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean and `expo lint` (0 problems).

## Customer app (`nearandnowcustomerapp`)

### Session handling (fixed 2026-07-16, undated severity)

- **`apiClient.ts`'s 401 handling didn't clear the session or update auth state**, leaving the customer stuck in a broken "looks logged in, everything 401s" state. **Fixed 2026-07-16** — a `setSessionExpiredHandler` registration between `apiClient.ts` and `AuthContext.tsx` now clears the session and flips `isAuthenticated` on any 401 from anywhere in the app, plus a global redirect-on-expiry watcher in `app/_layout.tsx`.
- **Session TTL was a fixed 30-day window from login, not a real sliding window** — a daily user could still get logged out on day 31. **Fixed 2026-07-16** — changed to a genuine 25-day sliding window, renewed on every authenticated request.
- **Session renewal only fired on ordering-related API calls, not general app usage** (including the app's own cold-start "am I logged in" check) — a customer who opened the app daily but hadn't ordered in 25 days would still get logged out, contradicting the stated intent. **Fixed same day** via a new `GET /api/customers/session/ping` route called on cold start and app-foreground.
- Two smaller renewal-code bugs (missing `role='customer'` filter on the renewal write; unhandled-throw risk on the expiry-cleanup write) were **fixed the same session**.

### Critical (new)

- **The Supabase service-role key was structurally required client-side** for `addressService.ts`, `authService.ts`, `product/coupons.tsx`, and `distanceUtils.ts`, and the same gap enabled a **cross-user IDOR** (no session-to-userId binding, trusted a bare `userId`). **Found already resolved** on the 2026-07-24 re-verification pass — all four files now route through `apiFetch` to the authenticated backend, with ownership enforced server-side.
- **Session tokens stored in plain AsyncStorage.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** (found via QA-audit cross-check) — moved to `expo-secure-store` across all 4 call sites, with a one-time legacy-AsyncStorage migration so existing sessions survive the update.
- **Order totals were entirely client-computed and written directly via the service-role client.** **Fixed 2026-07-16** — `createOrder` now routes through the backend's `POST /api/orders/place`, the same server-trusted pipeline the website uses.
- **`orderService.ts`'s order placement took a bare `user_id` with no session check (IDOR).** **Fixed 2026-07-16** alongside the fix above — `requireCustomer` now gates the route and the client-sent `user_id` is ignored.

### High (new)

- **Order-status push notifications only fired on final "delivered."** Client was already fully wired; the backend only ever called the notification service from `markDelivered`. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by adding the same notify pattern to `store_accepted`, `ready_for_pickup`, `delivery_partner_assigned`, and `order_picked_up`, all best-effort/non-blocking.
- **Cart was never cleared after a successful order** — only the *failed/cancelled* branch cleared it, backwards from the intended behavior, and confirmed worse than documented (a 40s "add more items" window kept showing stale items even after expiring). **Fixed 2026-07-24** by clearing the cart on both the COD and paid order-creation paths.
- **Checkout had no idempotency guard** — a fast double-tap on "Place Order" could create two identical orders, since the disabled-button state didn't take effect before a second tap could land, and the backend had no dedup either. **Fixed 2026-07-24** with a synchronous `useRef` lock client-side, plus a server-side check for an existing identical order from the same customer in the last 30 seconds.
- **`discount_amount` was hardcoded to `0` regardless of an applied coupon**, and **coupon expiry was fetched but never enforced.** **Fixed 2026-07-23**, as part of the backend coupon-discount fix — real discount now computed/persisted server-side, and expiry is re-validated at order-creation time regardless of a stale client-cached coupon.
- **Google Maps key called directly from the device, bypassing the backend's own proxy** (found via QA-audit cross-check, 2026-07-16). Confirmed direct calls to `maps.googleapis.com` with `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` at `app/profile-setup.tsx:130`, `app/location/select-map.tsx:134,216,287`, `app/location/add.tsx:149,168`, `app/location/edit.tsx:115,132` — shipped a billable, extractable Google Maps API key inside every installed app instead of routing through the backend's `/api/places` proxy. **Fixed 2026-07-26** — added a new `lib/placesService.ts` wrapping the existing `apiFetch` client and rewired all 8 call sites (reverse-geocode, geocode, autocomplete, place-details) across the 4 files to go through the backend proxy instead; deleted the now-dead `lib/mapsEnv.ts`. Required two small backend-side additions to `places.controller.ts` to avoid regressions: `sessiontoken` passthrough on `autocomplete`/`placeDetails` (preserves Google's session-based billing for the autocomplete→details pair) and `place_id,name` added to the `placeDetails` `fields` list (the app's `select-map.tsx` reads `result.place_id` from the details response). The native iOS/Android map-tile-rendering key in `app.config.js` is a separate, properly-restricted key and was left untouched. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend` and the customer app; not click-tested live on-device.
- **No cart quantity ceiling and no stock/bounds check anywhere** — `CartContext.tsx` placed no upper bound on quantity, and neither real order-creation path (`orders.controller.ts`'s `createOrder`, used by this app, and `database.service.ts`'s `placeCheckoutOrder`, used by the website) validated `quantity` at all — only unit price was ever re-derived server-side, so any positive/negative/fractional/huge quantity was inserted as-is. Note: `master_products` has no live inventory/stock-count field at all (removed from the schema; see the store-owner app's already-documented `setAllProductsOffline`/`restoreActiveProductsOnline` no-ops) — the closest real, admin-controlled equivalent is `master_products.min_quantity`/`max_quantity`, which nothing anywhere enforced. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new shared `backend/src/utils/quantity.ts::validateQuantity()` rejects non-integer/non-positive quantities and enforces each item's `min_quantity`/`max_quantity` bounds, wired into both `createOrder` and `placeCheckoutOrder` (both already fetch `master_products` for price re-derivation, so this piggybacks on the same query — no extra round-trip); violations return 400, not 500. `CartContext.tsx` also got a client-side `MAX_QUANTITY_PER_ITEM = 99` hard cap in `addItem`/`updateQty` as defense-in-depth/UX — the backend check is the real boundary. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend + customer app, and the existing 17/17 backend vitest suite.
- **`getProductById` never filtered on store approval/active status, unlike every other product-fetch function in this file.** Found 2026-08-04 during a deep dive following commit `64cf747` in the sibling `near-and-now` repo (see Open Issues history, above, for the full original writeup, including the confirmed order-misrouting consequence via the backend's unmatched-item fallback). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — since this screen can be reached with no location context at all (deep link, shared link, stale cached list), it can't use the `nearbyIds` filter every other product-fetch function here relies on. Instead, `getProductById` now checks the fetched id against `storeService.getAllActiveProductIds()` — the existing, already-cached, location-independent "carried by any approved + online store" set already used by `home.tsx`/`categories.tsx` for the same base filter — and returns `null` (the screen's existing not-found state) if the product isn't in it. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`getAllProducts()` used unfiltered (no `nearbyIds`) in the checkout "recommended items" strip, same root cause as the `getProductById` gap above.** Found 2026-08-04, same review. **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `app/support/checkout.tsx`'s cold-cache fallback path (used when the home screen's memory cache isn't warm) now fetches `storeService.getAllActiveProductIds()` and passes it as `nearbyIds`, matching the base filter every other product-fetch path in the app already applies. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`getAllProducts()` also used unfiltered on the order-confirmation screen's "add more items" suggestions, same gap as the checkout item directly above.** Found 2026-08-04, same review. **Fixed 2026-08-04** with the identical fix — `app/order/confirmation/[id].tsx` now fetches `storeService.getAllActiveProductIds()` and passes it as `nearbyIds`. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **"Need help with this order?" on the live-tracking screen linked to a route that didn't exist.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). `app/order/track/[id].tsx` navigated to `router.push("/support/help" as any)` — the `as any` cast was masking the fact that expo-router's typed routes would have caught this, since no `app/support/help.tsx` exists (`app/support/` only holds `cart`/`checkout`/`payment-options`/`search`). **Fixed 2026-08-04** by pointing it at the real support screen, `/settings/support`, and dropping the `as any` cast — it now typechecks cleanly on its own, confirming the route is real. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Bill/Order Summary omitted every fee/discount line item on the order detail and confirmation screens.** Found 2026-08-04 (see Open Issues history, above). **Fixed 2026-08-04** — `lib/orderService.ts`'s backend query for a single order already selects `*` on `customer_orders` (confirmed via `database.service.ts`'s `getOrderById`), so the gap was purely client-side: `BackendCustomerOrder`/`Order` types and `mapBackendOrder()` silently dropped `discount_amount`, `tip_amount`, `gstin`/`gstin_business_name`, and `receiver_*` on the floor — extended both to carry them through. `app/order/[id].tsx` and `app/order/confirmation/[id].tsx` now render Platform Fee/Handling Charges (fixed constants — not persisted per-order since they haven't varied since this fee model, matching the live checkout screen's own display convention) and an informational "GST charges" line (new shared `constants/fees.ts::calcFeeGst()` helper, extracted from the same logic below), plus Coupon Discount/Delivery Partner Tip when the order actually has them. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **The customer app double-charged GST on the Platform Fee + Handling Fee, inconsistent with the website for an identical cart.** Found and fixed 2026-08-04, discovered while building the fix directly above (not part of the original 2026-08-04 audit — the audit only caught the *missing display* of these fields, not that the underlying total itself was wrong). `constants/fees.ts`'s `calcOrderTotal` added `gst = (platformFee + handlingFee) * GST_RATE` **on top of** the fixed ₹9.50/₹5.50 fee totals; but the website's `checkoutCalculations.ts` (the reference implementation, per its own comment "matches backend invoice.service.ts logic exactly") treats those same fixed amounts as already GST-inclusive, reverse-extracting GST from them for display rather than adding it — and the backend's own `placeCheckoutOrder` trusted-floor check (`database.service.ts:1024-1027`) was already written assuming the no-extra-GST model, independently confirming which side was wrong. Concretely: for a ₹100 cart, the website billed ₹115.00 and the customer app billed ₹116 for the identical order — the app was silently overcharging every customer by ~₹0.75–1 per order via a double-counted GST. **Fixed 2026-08-04** by making `gst` a purely informational breakdown (`calcFeeGst()`, reverse-extracted the same way the website does) instead of an additive charge — `projected`/`finalPayable` no longer add it a second time. `app/support/checkout.tsx` and `app/support/cart.tsx` both already displayed `gst` as a separate line purely for information (never summed it themselves), so their UI needed no change — only the underlying total calculation was wrong. Also confirmed, while investigating: delivery fee is correctly ₹0 on both the customer app (`DELIVERY_FEE = 0`) and the website (`deliveryChargeForDistanceKm()` always returns 0), and neither app re-charges GST on the item subtotal itself (each product's own price already has real per-product GST baked in server-side; this was fixed historically, see commit `7514ba0` and the website's `checkoutCalculations.ts` comment) — both already correct, no action needed there. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app; backend's trusted-floor check confirmed compatible with the corrected (lower) total via manual reconciliation of the formula.
- **Verified (not a bug): both apps already correctly redirect away from checkout when the cart becomes empty.** Checked 2026-08-04 on user request ("when the customer removes anything from the cart from the checkout page, it should automatically collapse the checkout page and go to ... the homepage"). `app/support/checkout.tsx:104-108` already does exactly this — a `useEffect` gated on `isHydrated && items.length === 0` calls `router.replace("/(tabs)/home")`, and removing the last item via the checkout screen's own quantity stepper (`incrementQty`) correctly drops it from `items` (see `CartContext.tsx`'s `incrementQty`/`updateQty`, both remove-at-zero). The website's `CheckoutPage.tsx:91-96` has the identical pattern (`cartItems.length === 0` → notify + `navigate('/shop', { replace: true })`). No code change needed on either platform.
- **Invoice screen was entirely fabricated client-side; PDF download was a stub.** `app/order/invoice/[id].tsx` built a fake invoice object (`orderToInvoice()`, `lib/invoiceService.ts`) with a hardcoded placeholder GSTIN (`29XXXXXXXXXXXXX`), a flat re-guessed 5% tax split, and no real discount tracking; `handleDownload` just showed a "coming in next update" alert. The real, already-working backend pipeline (`GET /api/invoices/order/:orderId/customer`, used correctly by the website's `OrdersPage.tsx`) was never called. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — the screen now calls that endpoint (auto-generates the PDF on first access if missing) and opens the real signed PDF via `Linking.openURL`, showing a summary card (invoice number/date/order ref/grand total) instead of trying to re-render the PDF's content natively. `lib/invoiceService.ts` had zero other callers and was deleted. Verified end-to-end against a real order and a real customer session token — the endpoint returns the expected shape and the signed URL serves a genuine `application/pdf` (confirmed via direct `curl`, not just code review).
- **Five old carried-over items, all confirmed still present and fixed 2026-07-28:**
  - **Logout didn't clear the cart or unregister the device's push token** — a shared/reused device kept the previous customer's cart and kept receiving their order notifications. Fixed: `AuthContext.logoutUser()` now clears the push token server-side (`token: null`, called before the local session is wiped, needs the still-valid token to authenticate) via a small backend change (`registerPushToken`/`updateCustomerPushToken` now accept an explicit `null` to clear, distinct from an omitted token which is still a 400). `CartContext` now watches `AuthContext`'s `isAuthenticated` true→false transition and clears the cart — safe with no circular-dependency issue since `CartProvider` is already nested inside `AuthProvider` in `app/_layout.tsx`.
  - **`trackingService.ts`'s `fetchOrderTrackingFull()` caught every error type** (network failure, 500, 404, timeout) **and returned a uniform `null`**, so the tracking hook's "Order not found" message fired identically for a genuinely missing order and a transient network blip. `apiFetch` already throws well-differentiated messages per status (404 → "Resource not found.", 5xx → "Server error..."); removed the swallowing `catch` so the caller's own catch block gets the real reason.
  - **Cold-start push notification taps could silently drop their deep link** — `router.push()` called before the root Stack has actually mounted its routes no-ops. `usePushNotifications.dev.ts` now waits for `useRootNavigationState().key` before processing `getLastNotificationResponseAsync()`, guarded by a ref so it only ever fires once per app session.
  - Rolled into the push-token item above: **no push-token unregister on logout**.
  - **Dead "Order Placed!" success overlay in `checkout.tsx`** — `setShowSuccess(true)` was never called anywhere; the real post-order flow already navigates straight to `/order/confirmation/[id]`. Removed the unreachable state/animation/JSX/styles rather than wiring up a redundant duplicate of an already-working confirmation screen.
  
  Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and customer app.
- **Wallet "Pay now" retry had no double-tap guard — real double-debit risk.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup — `app/orders.tsx`'s `handleRetryPayment` and `app/order/[id].tsx`'s equivalent both called `payOrderWithWallet(order.id)` directly, bypassing `usePaymentFlow` entirely, so the button's `disabled={paymentPhase !== "idle"}` never actually reflected an in-flight wallet call). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by adding a local `walletPaymentInFlight` ref (mirroring `usePaymentFlow`'s own `inFlight` ref, which only guards its Razorpay path) around both wallet-retry call sites — checked and set synchronously before the `await payOrderWithWallet(...)` call, cleared in a `finally`. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app; this app has no test infrastructure to extend, and the fix wasn't click-tested live on-device (would require physically double-tapping a real payment-pending order).
- **No `ErrorBoundary` anywhere in the app.** Found 2026-07-28 (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup — confirmed via `grep -rl "ErrorBoundary"` returning zero hits; any unhandled render exception crashed the whole app with no fallback UI, unlike the rider/store-owner apps which already had this fix). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by porting the rider app's `components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` verbatim (same class-component structure, same "Something went wrong" + Retry-button fallback UI), swapping only its color references from the rider app's `Colors`/`Spacing`/`BorderRadius` theme module to this app's own `constants/colors.ts` (`C`) plus inline spacing values, since this app has no equivalent spacing/radius constants module. Mounted at the outermost level of `app/_layout.tsx`'s `RootLayout`, wrapping `GestureHandlerRootView` and everything inside it (including `AuthProvider`), so a render exception thrown by a provider itself is caught too, not just something inside `AppShell`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; this app has no test infrastructure to extend, and `expo lint` couldn't be run at all — a pre-existing, unrelated tooling misconfiguration in this repo (errors on a nonexistent `src` directory pattern, confirmed unrelated to any file touched here — same issue already noted on two other fixes above).
- **`cart.tsx`'s and `checkout.tsx`'s new empty-cart redirect (added 2026-07-31 in Rounak Jana's `c89b9ff`) raced against cart hydration, incorrectly bouncing a customer with a real, non-empty cart to Home.** Both screens' new `useEffect(() => { if (items.length === 0) router.replace(...) }, [items.length])` fired on first render whenever `CartContext.items` hadn't finished loading yet from `AsyncStorage` — reachable via a deep link straight into `/support/cart`/`/support/checkout` (`scheme: "nearandnow"` is configured) or Expo Router restoring saved navigation state on a cold relaunch. Same bug class already fixed on the website's `CheckoutPage.tsx` via its `hasLoadedCart` guard, not carried over here. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by destructuring `isHydrated` from `useCart()` in both screens and gating the redirect on `isHydrated && items.length === 0`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live (would need a real deep link or killed-app relaunch onto one of these screens to observe directly).

### Medium (new)

- **OTP verify screen had no synchronous double-submit lock, and discarded all but the last digit on paste/SMS-autofill.** Found 2026-07-28, and reported again directly by the user in real-world testing before this fix (see Open Issues, above, for the full original writeup). **Fixed 2026-07-29** by porting the shopkeeper/rider apps' already-working fixes verbatim: (1) a `verifyingRef` synchronous guard around `handleVerify`, mirroring this same app's own `checkout.tsx`'s `placingRef` pattern for the identical race. (2) `handleChangeDigit` now distributes a multi-character value (SMS-autofill banner tap, or a manual paste) across the remaining boxes from wherever focus is, instead of keeping only `clean[clean.length - 1]` and silently dropping the other 5 digits — matching the shopkeeper/rider apps' `handleDigitChange` logic exactly. (3) Added the missing `textContentType="oneTimeCode"` (iOS) and `autoComplete="sms-otp"`/`importantForAutofill="yes"` (Android, first box only) props so the OS's autofill suggestion strip has a chance to appear at all — also removed each box's `maxLength={1}`, since neither working reference implementation sets it and it risks clipping an autofilled multi-character delivery at the native layer before the new distribution logic ever runs. `tsc --noEmit` clean; this app has no test infrastructure to extend, and wasn't click-tested live on-device (would need a real SMS-autofill prompt or a physical double-tap to observe either fix directly) — verified by direct comparison against the shopkeeper/rider apps' equivalent, already-proven-working screens instead.
- **Hardcoded flat ₹25 delivery fee.** **Fixed 2026-07-23**, alongside the backend/website fix — changed to ₹0 as a launch-goodwill promo (old value preserved in git history).
- **GST was removed from the mobile app's checkout total in Rounak Jana's `c89b9ff` (2026-07-31), but not from the website's — same cart totaled differently across platforms.** `constants/fees.ts`'s `calcOrderTotal()` hardcoded `gst = 0` (previously 5% on Platform Fee + Handling Fee) and the "GST charges" bill row was removed from both `cart.tsx` and `checkout.tsx`, while the website's `checkoutCalculations.ts` kept charging 5% GST on platform/handling fees unchanged. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by restoring `GST_RATE = 0.05` in `constants/fees.ts` (`gst = (platformFee + handlingFee) * GST_RATE`, matching the website's `FEE_GST_RATE`) and re-adding the "GST (5%)" bill row to `cart.tsx`'s Bill Summary and its info-modal breakdown, plus the "GST charges" `BillRow` to `checkout.tsx`'s Bill Details — both sourced from the same `calcOrderTotal()` `gst` field the removed rows had used before. The delivery-fee wording change from the same commit ("Always free — ₹0") was deliberately left as-is — out of scope for this fix, just the GST removal. `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live.
- **Secure-storage migration didn't handle `SecureStore` itself throwing** — could silently reproduce the exact plain-storage bug it was meant to fix. **Fixed 2026-07-18** by wrapping the `SecureStore` reads in their own try/catch with a fallback to the legacy AsyncStorage check, distinguishing a real failure from a plain never-logged-in guest.
- **Payment reconcile loop's `'paid'`-only check missed a real terminal state mid-poll** — narrower than originally described: `reconcile()` (`hooks/usePaymentFlow.ts`) is only ever entered after the backend's `verifyPayment()` call itself throws (network error, backend hiccup, signature mismatch), not on a plain gateway cancel/fail, which already short-circuits immediately without polling at all. But within that narrower window, Razorpay's `payment.failed` webhook (`backend/src/services/payment.service.ts`'s `processWebhookEvent`) can legitimately write `payment_status: 'failed'` to `customer_orders` while the poll is running, and the loop only checked for `'paid'`, so a customer whose payment definitively failed during this window still saw "Confirming with bank…" for the full remaining 10s. **Fixed 2026-07-26** by also checking for `'failed'` and returning immediately with the same `{status: 'pending', reason: 'failed'}` shape the gateway-fail path already uses — `checkout.tsx`'s existing generic `reason`-keyed title/message map picks it up with no UI changes needed. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; no test file (no existing test harness for this hook to extend).
- **"Add more items" window didn't actually attach items to the placed order** — `app/order/confirmation/[id].tsx`'s countdown promised "Add items now and they'll be delivered with this order!", and a second, independent 40s countdown box on `app/order/track/[id].tsx` made the same promise, but neither had any backend connection at all — `handleAddToCart` just called the ordinary `addItem()` into the shopping cart, and the tracking screen's "Add more items" button navigated to the home screen with zero link back to the order. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by building the real backend the promise needed, scoped deliberately to just the confirmation screen: new `order_addition_requests` table (migration `20260905000000`) plus two endpoints (`orders.routes.ts`: `POST /:orderId/add-items/create-payment`, `.../verify-payment`, both `requireCustomer`) that re-price submitted items server-side (same GST-aware formula as checkout, see Reference below), reject if more than 35s have passed since `placed_at` (a few seconds' grace over the client's 30s countdown), and open a **separate** Razorpay charge for just the delta — the original order's own payment is never touched. Only on confirmed payment does a new row-locked function (`apply_order_addition_request`, atomic per this project's established pattern for "two dependent writes must not partially apply") insert the real `order_items` and roll the order's totals forward. The confirmation screen's countdown was corrected to the real enforced 30s and now automatically opens the Razorpay sheet for the delta when the window closes if the cart has anything in it (the checkout screen already clears the cart on successful placement, so anything present is genuinely new); cancelling/failing that payment just leaves the items in the ordinary cart, same as before this feature existed. The tracking screen's duplicate promotional box was **removed rather than wired up** — unlike the confirmation screen, that screen has no guarantee the customer's cart is empty when they arrive (it could be genuine unrelated shopping for a future order), so auto-charging it into an old order would have been a real trust risk, not just extra work. New backend endpoints scoped to only allow adding items from a store already part of the order (no new store_order creation) — an item from a store not already in the order is rejected with a clear message. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and customer app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean; not click-tested live (needs a real device + Razorpay sandbox to exercise the actual payment sheet). Both new migrations applied to the live database the same session.
  - **Same-day deep-dive review found and fixed 4 more bugs in this feature before it saw any real traffic:** (1) **Severe** — `createAdditionPaymentOrder`'s Razorpay order notes reused the `internal_order_id` key that `processWebhookEvent`'s `payment.authorized`/`captured`/`failed` handlers all key off to update `customer_orders`; `payment.authorized` has no idempotent-skip guard at all, so on essentially every real transaction this would have silently overwritten the *main* order's `payment_status` back to `'authorized'` and stomped its `razorpay_payment_id` with the addition payment's id instead. Fixed by renaming the notes key (`addition_order_id`) so every handler's `internalOrderId` guard correctly no-ops for these events. (2) `resolveTrustedItems`'s comment claimed it "prefers a store this order already has more items from" when a product is available at more than one of the order's stores, but the code just took `options[0]` — arbitrary, DB-row-order-dependent selection with no such preference actually implemented. Fixed by fetching existing per-store item counts once per batch and sorting options accordingly, with a deterministic tiebreak. (3) `createAdditionPayment` had no guard against two concurrent/retried calls for the same order each creating an independent `order_addition_requests` row with its own live Razorpay order — a screen remount (app killed and reopened, Fast Refresh in dev) re-firing the confirmation screen's once-only effect could double-charge. Fixed with a check-plus-partial-unique-index pattern (new migration `20260906000000`, same shape as the `store_profile_change_requests` race fix above). (4) The confirmation screen's 30s countdown was purely mount-relative (a plain `useState`/`setInterval`), not anchored to the order's actual `placed_at` like the backend's own window — a remount long after the real order placement would show a fresh, misleading "30s left!" for a window the server would actually reject as closed. Fixed by re-deriving `timeLeft` from `order.created_at` once loaded. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and customer app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean. The new migration applied to the live database the same session.
- **Plain Order Detail screen (`app/order/[id].tsx`) had no polling fallback at all — realtime is silently, permanently dead here**, not just occasionally flaky: the app never signs in via `supabase.auth` (its own phone-OTP JWT instead), so `auth.uid()` is always NULL and the `customer_own_orders` RLS policy that gates this screen's realtime channel always evaluates false. Unlike the premium tracking screen (where a poll fallback is genuinely a fallback, since that screen's data comes through a backend endpoint, not Realtime's client-side RLS path), this screen had zero live-update path at all — status/payment changes only ever appeared after a manual pull-to-refresh. **Fixed 2026-07-27** by adding an 8s poll (`STATUS_POLL_MS`) that calls the existing lightweight `getOrderPaymentStatus()` (`GET /api/tracking/orders/:id`, already ownership-checked server-side) rather than re-running `loadOrder()`'s full `getUserOrders()` scan of the customer's entire order list — merges `order_status`/`payment_status` into local state using the exact same shape as the existing realtime handler, so the status badge/timeline update identically regardless of which path caught the change, and flashes the same `autoRefreshing` indicator. Stops polling once the order reaches a terminal status (checked via a ref-mirrored snapshot, kept outside the `setOrder` updater to avoid a side effect inside it, matching this codebase's established pure-updater convention). The existing (permanently inert) realtime subscription was left in place rather than removed — harmless, and would start working for free if the underlying RLS/auth mismatch is ever fixed. **Self-caught before finishing:** the first version of this poll had no `cancelled` guard around the `await getOrderPaymentStatus(id)` call — `hooks/useOrderTracking.ts`'s driver-location poll already establishes the pattern of checking a `cancelled` flag after the await specifically so a resolve-after-teardown can't write into state on an unmounted screen or after `id` changed; this poll was missing it. Fixed by adding the same flag before finalizing. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live (would need a real in-flight order status change to observe).
- **No stale-heartbeat/signal-lost indicator on the live tracking map.** `DriverLocation.updated_at` was fetched every 2s but never compared against the current time except inside a rarely-opened marker popup — the "Live tracking" pulsing green badge stayed green regardless of staleness, so a rider's phone losing signal or the location background task crashing left the map showing a frozen bike icon with a "Live" pulse and no warning to the customer. **Fixed 2026-07-27** in `app/order/track/[id].tsx` — a `now` state ticking every 5s (independent of whether new location data ever arrives, since a genuinely stuck poll wouldn't otherwise trigger a re-render) drives a staleness check against `primaryDriverLocation.updated_at`; past a 60s threshold (`STALE_DRIVER_LOCATION_MS`, chosen against the rider app's typical few-seconds-per-update cadence) the pulse and "Live tracking" text switch to an amber "Signal lost · last seen Xs/Xm ago" state instead. No change needed to `useOrderTracking.ts`/`trackingService.ts` — `updated_at` was already being returned, just never checked against wall-clock time on this screen. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean; not click-tested live (would need to actually stop a rider's location updates mid-delivery to observe the transition). `expo lint` could not be run for either of these two fixes — pre-existing, unrelated tooling misconfiguration in this repo (`expo lint` errors on a nonexistent `src` directory pattern, confirmed unrelated to any file touched here).
- **Wallet screen was a fully non-functional shell** — hardcoded ₹0.00 balance, "Add Money" just showed an alert. **Fixed 2026-07-27** as a real feature, per explicit user decision (a smaller "mark it clearly unavailable" option was offered and declined): new `wallet_transactions` ledger table + `app_users.wallet_balance` cached column (migration `20260910000000`), with atomic `credit_wallet()`/`debit_wallet()` SQL functions (row-locked, same pattern as this codebase's other atomic-write functions). New backend `GET/POST /api/wallet/*` endpoints (`wallet.controller.ts`/`wallet.routes.ts`, `requireCustomer`-gated): `GET /` (balance), `POST /topup/create` + `/topup/verify` (real Razorpay top-up — reuses `payment.service.ts`'s signature-verify/capture/`razorpayRequest` machinery, with a distinct `wallet_topup` notes marker so the shared webhook handler's `internal_order_id`/`addition_order_id` guards correctly ignore these events, same lesson as the addition-payment feature), and `POST /pay-order` (pays an already-created pending order out of the wallet balance instead of opening Razorpay). Frontend: `lib/walletService.ts` client; `app/wallet.tsx` now shows a real fetched balance and a real top-up flow (Razorpay sheet → verify → balance updates), mirroring `usePaymentFlow.ts`'s structure; `'wallet'` added to `PaymentMode` (`lib/paymentSelection.ts`); a new "Near & Now Wallet" rail added to `app/support/payment-options.tsx` (deliberately distinct from the existing "Wallets" rail, which is Razorpay's *external* wallet aggregator — PhonePe/Amazon Pay/Paytm — not this balance), greyed out when the balance can't cover the cart total; `app/support/checkout.tsx` gained a `mode === "wallet"` branch parallel to `cod`/online (create order pending → pay from wallet → same confirmation-screen/cache-busting path as a successful online payment, or same saved-order-retry-later path on failure); both retry-payment call sites (`app/order/[id].tsx`, `app/orders.tsx`) branch to `payOrderWithWallet` instead of Razorpay when `order.payment_method === 'wallet'`. Refunds for wallet-paid orders needed separate handling, since there's no Razorpay payment to reverse: `database.service.ts`'s order-cancellation refund branch and `payment.controller.ts`'s admin `resolveItemRefund` (unavailable-item refunds) both gained a `payment_method === 'wallet'` branch that credits the wallet instead of calling `processRefund()`; `shopkeeper.controller.ts`'s `flagUnresolvableItemsForRefund` was updated to flag wallet-paid orders as refund-eligible too (previously only online-Razorpay-paid orders were, so a wallet-paid order with an unavailable item had literally no refund path — the admin notification even said so explicitly: "no online refund to process"). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and customer app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean. Migration applied to production and verified live (column, table, and both functions confirmed present). Not click-tested live (needs a real device + Razorpay sandbox).
  - **Same-day deep-dive review (done twice, on explicit request the second time) found and fixed 4 more bugs before this saw any real traffic:** (1) **Severe** — `payOrderWithWallet` originally did a check-then-act on `customer_orders.payment_status` (SELECT, then later UPDATE), with the actual debit happening via `debit_wallet()` in between — two concurrent calls for the same order (e.g. a double-tapped "Pay Now" retry) could both read `'pending'`, both pass the check, and both debit the wallet, since `debit_wallet()` only row-locks `app_users`, not the order itself. A real double-charge risk. Fixed with a new `pay_order_with_wallet()` SQL function (migration `20260911000000`) that locks the `customer_orders` row `FOR UPDATE` *first* — a concurrent second call blocks until the first transaction commits, then sees `'paid'` and aborts cleanly instead of debiting again — doing the ownership check, not-already-paid check, debit, and status update all in one transaction, same shape as `review_store_profile_change_request`. (2) `credit_wallet()`'s top-up idempotency was a plain check-then-act (SELECT for an existing `razorpay_payment_id`, then UPDATE+INSERT) — safe against an actual double-credit even under true concurrency (the losing call's INSERT hits the unique index and its whole transaction, including its balance UPDATE, rolls back automatically), but the losing concurrent caller would have seen a raw Postgres unique-violation error instead of a graceful "already credited" response. Fixed by moving the update+insert into a nested block with an `EXCEPTION WHEN unique_violation` handler that returns the current (unaffected) balance instead of propagating the error — handles the sequential-retry case and true concurrent races identically now. (3) **Critical, found on the second deep-dive pass** — `verifyTopup` verified the Razorpay signature (proving paymentId/orderId/signature are internally consistent) and that the payment was captured, but never checked that the payment was ever actually *created as a wallet top-up for this specific user*. Since the idempotency guard is keyed only on `razorpay_payment_id` for `reason='topup'`, any authenticated user could submit the `(paymentId, razorpayOrderId, signature)` of **any** captured Razorpay payment anywhere in the system — including their own unrelated order payment — to `/api/wallet/topup/verify` and mint free wallet credit equal to that payment's amount, as long as that exact payment id hadn't already been used for a topup. In the worst case a customer could pay for a normal order and then also "redeem" that same payment as a wallet top-up, getting money-for-nothing. Fixed by fetching the full payment entity and checking `payment.notes.wallet_topup === true` and `payment.notes.user_id === req.customerId` — both stamped on the Razorpay order at creation time by `createWalletTopupOrder()` specifically so they can be verified against Razorpay's own trusted record rather than anything client-supplied, the same shape as the main checkout's `payment.order_id === razorpayOrderId` strict check. (4) On the same pass, `app/support/payment-options.tsx`'s new wallet row showed "Unable to load balance" during the ordinary loading window too, not just on an actual fetch failure — no distinct loading state had been added, unlike `app/wallet.tsx`'s own (correct) `loadingBalance` state. Fixed with a `loadingWalletBalance` state so the row reads "Loading balance…" first and only shows the error text if the fetch genuinely fails. All four fixes applied to production the same session and verified live (`pay_order_with_wallet` confirmed present) or via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and customer app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), clean. **Known pre-existing gap, not introduced by this feature and not fixed here:** `resolveItemRefund`'s "already resolved" check and `cancelOrder`'s "already cancelled" check are both themselves unlocked check-then-act reads — a genuine (if rare, requiring near-simultaneous duplicate admin/customer actions) double-refund race exists for the *original* Razorpay refund path too, not just the new wallet one; flagging for awareness since the wallet refund branch added here inherits the same pre-existing risk profile symmetrically, rather than introducing a new one.
- **`GET /api/payment/methods` (saved payment methods, feeding `app/support/payment-options.tsx`'s "Preferred Payment" section) turned out to already be fully implemented — but with zero authentication.** Investigating why the frontend's own `EXPO_PUBLIC_SAVED_METHODS_ENABLED` flag was already `true` in `.env` (suggesting someone expected this to already work) led to `payment.controller.ts`'s `getSavedMethods`, which trusted a bare `?user_id=` query param with no session check at all — anyone who could guess or enumerate a user id could fetch that user's real saved card network/last4/issuer and UPI VPA, live in production, regardless of the app's own feature flag (a direct request bypasses it entirely). **Fixed 2026-07-27** — added `requireCustomer` to the route (`payment.routes.ts`) and changed the controller to derive the user from `req.customerId` (the authenticated session), never the query param; the frontend (`lib/razorpayService.ts`) no longer sends `user_id` at all, since the backend never reads it now. The rest of the pipeline this feature needed was already correctly built: `payment.service.ts`'s `createPaymentOrder` already attaches a Razorpay `customer_id` with `remember_customer: true`, and `getSavedMethods` already correctly maps Razorpay's Customer+Tokens API response. No feature-flag change needed — it was already on. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and customer app, and the existing backend vitest suite (17/17), both clean.
- **GSTIN input at checkout had no format validation before being persisted and printed on the customer's own tax invoice.** **Fixed 2026-07-27** on both the mobile app and the website (same shared backend), plus a server-side check — the actual trust boundary, since client-side validation alone doesn't stop a modified client or a direct API call. `app/support/checkout.tsx` (mobile) and `CheckoutPage.tsx` (website) both gained a `GSTIN_REGEX` (`^[0-9]{2}[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1}[1-9A-Z]{1}Z[0-9A-Z]{1}$`, the standard 15-char Indian GSTIN format) with live inline error text under the input (distinguishing "N more characters needed" from "doesn't match the format") and a submit-time block if invalid. `backend/src/routes/orders.routes.ts`'s `placeCheckoutSchema` (`POST /api/orders/place`, used by both apps) now enforces the same regex via Zod, accepting empty/undefined as "no GSTIN provided." Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend, frontend, and the mobile app; the frontend's full suite (26/26) and backend's (17/17), both clean.
- **Stale fee-info modal copy on the cart screen contradicted the live-computed bill shown one line above it.** Found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit. `app/support/cart.tsx`'s info modal hardcoded "Fixed ₹25.00 per order" for delivery and "18% on Platform Fee + Handling Fee... = ₹2.70" for GST — leftover from before `constants/fees.ts` changed `DELIVERY_FEE` to `0` (launch-goodwill promo) and `GST_RATE` to `0.05`. The bill row directly above (`GST (18%)`) already showed the correct 5%-computed value, so the modal directly contradicted the number the customer had just looked at. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — both the modal and the `GST (18%)` bill-row label now import `PLATFORM_FEE`/`HANDLING_FEE`/`DELIVERY_FEE`/`GST_RATE` from `constants/fees.ts` directly instead of hardcoding values, so they can never drift out of sync with the actual computed bill again; the delivery-fee line now reads "Free for now — launch promo" when `DELIVERY_FEE` is `0` rather than a stale "Fixed ₹X" line that would be wrong the moment the promo ends either way. `app/support/checkout.tsx`'s equivalent info alerts don't state a percentage/figure, so no change was needed there. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app.
- **Regression-adjacent finding from a same-day regression review of the fix above: `checkout.tsx:692`'s "Claim GST credit up to 18% on the order" visibly contradicted the 5%-computed GST now correctly shown by the fix above.** This line predates the `cart.tsx` fix and wasn't touched by it, but that fix's correction to the *other* GST reference on the same flow exposed this one as the sole remaining stale "18%" reference (confirmed via grep — no other stale ₹25/2.70/9.50/5.50/18% literals remained anywhere in the cart/checkout flow). Investigating further found the website (`frontend/src/pages/CheckoutPage.tsx:1278`) had already fixed the *same* underlying copy bug on 2026-07-30 — not just correcting the percentage, but removing the misleading "claim a credit" framing entirely, since the platform doesn't grant any GST credit; attaching a GSTIN just gets the business a compliant invoice they can use to claim their *own* input tax credit with their tax authority. **Fixed 2026-07-28** by matching the website's already-corrected copy exactly: `checkout.tsx`'s GSTIN card now reads "Get a GST-compliant invoice for input tax credit claims," bringing the mobile app in line with the website instead of just patching the number. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Applied coupon was never revalidated against `min_order_value` after the cart changes.** Found 2026-07-28 during the 6-repo deep audit. `app/product/coupons.tsx`'s `isApplicable()` only checked `min_order_value` at apply-time, gating the "Apply" button; `context/CartContext.tsx`'s `discount` memo computed purely from `appliedCoupon.type/value/max_discount` with no `min_order_value` field on its local `Coupon` type and no recheck against `subtotal` afterward — so removing an item after applying a coupon could leave a no-longer-eligible discount silently still applied, with `coupon_id` still sent in the order payload. **Fixed 2026-07-28** — added `min_order_value?: number` to `CartContext.tsx`'s `Coupon` type (already present on the coupon objects passed into `applyCoupon` from `coupons.tsx`, just not typed/used) and a new `isCouponEligible` memo (`subtotal >= appliedCoupon.min_order_value`, defaulting to eligible when a coupon has no minimum), exposed on the context. The `discount` memo now returns `0` whenever `!isCouponEligible`, and `checkout.tsx` withholds `coupon_id` from the order payload in that state (previously sent unconditionally) rather than relying on the backend to catch it. The coupon is deliberately *not* auto-removed — it stays "applied" so the customer can add items back and reclaim it — but `checkout.tsx`'s "Savings Corner" row now shows a red warning ("`{code}` no longer applies · add ₹X more to reapply") instead of a false "applied · −₹X saved" message whenever this state is hit. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app.
- **Order-confirmation screen found the just-placed order by scanning the customer's entire order list client-side (`getUserOrders().find(o => o.id === id)`) rather than fetching it directly** — the full-list endpoint can be a beat behind immediately after creation (cache, replication lag), and when the scan came up empty, the screen had no error/retry state at all: `loading` just flipped to `false` with `order` still `null`, and the render below only ever used `order?.field` optional chaining everywhere, so the customer saw a mostly-blank "Order Placed Successfully!" page with no items, no total, and no indication anything was wrong. **Fixed 2026-07-27** — new `getOrderById()` in `lib/orderService.ts` calls the existing, already-ownership-checked `GET /api/orders/:orderId` directly and reuses the existing `mapBackendOrder()` mapper (identical raw shape, since both endpoints join `store_orders(order_items)`); the confirmation screen now calls this instead of scanning, and a real `loadError` state renders a "Couldn't load your order details" screen with a Try Again button (re-triggers the fetch) and a link to My Orders, instead of silently rendering a broken success page. The same direct-fetch replaced the second full-list scan used to refresh the order summary after the "add more items" flow completes. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Payment-options screen understated the total by the tip amount.** `app/support/payment-options.tsx:144`'s `calcOrderTotal(subtotal, totalItems, 2, discount).finalPayable` had no tip term at all, while `checkout.tsx`'s actual charge is `baseFinalPayable + tipAmount` (tip is applied as a separate step after `calcOrderTotal`, not inside it). **Failure scenario:** customer picks a tip on checkout, then taps "Pay using" to open payment-options — the total shown there (and the wallet-insufficient-balance check, and the "Preferred Payment" quick-pay amount) was understated by the tip, and a wallet balance that covered the tip-inclusive total could incorrectly show as insufficient or vice versa. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by threading `tipAmount` through as a navigation param: `checkout.tsx`'s `PayMethodRow` now takes a `tipAmount` prop (passed from `CheckoutScreen`'s existing `tipAmount` memo) and routes to `/support/payment-options` with `params: { tip: String(tipAmount) }` instead of a bare `router.push` with no params. `payment-options.tsx` reads it via `useLocalSearchParams` (parsed/clamped to `>= 0`, defaulting to `0` if absent or malformed) and now computes its total by mirroring checkout's exact unrounded formula — `calcOrderTotal(...).projected`, then `Math.max(projected - discount, 0) + tipAmount` — instead of consuming `calcOrderTotal`'s own already-rounded `finalPayable`, which checkout never actually uses either. Deliberately did **not** add a `tipAmount` param to the shared `calcOrderTotal()` util in `constants/fees.ts`, since its other two call sites (`cart.tsx`, `checkout.tsx` itself) have no tip concept and checkout already deliberately keeps tip addition as a separate step outside that function — changing its signature would have been a wider, riskier change for no benefit. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app. Not click-tested live (would need a real cart + a selected tip to observe the corrected total on the payment-options screen).
- **Cart/checkout quantity steppers: rapid double-tap could net +1 instead of +2 (or -1 instead of -2).** `app/support/cart.tsx` (~125-137) and `app/support/checkout.tsx` (~615-630) both called `updateQty(id, item.quantity ± 1)`, reading `item.quantity` from the render-time closure, while `CartContext.updateQty` sets an *absolute* value, not a delta — two taps landing before a re-render both computed the same target (`N+1`) from the same stale `item.quantity` (`N`), so the second tap was a silent no-op instead of reaching `N+2`. Confirmed the same pattern existed at every other stepper call site in the app: `app/product/[id].tsx`, `app/support/search.tsx`, and the `ProductCard`-style components backing `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/order-again.tsx`, and `app/category/[slug].tsx` (8 call sites total). **Fixed 2026-07-30** by adding a new `incrementQty(productId, delta)` method to `CartContext.tsx` that reads the item's *current* quantity from inside the `setItems(prev => ...)` updater itself (not from a caller-supplied snapshot) — so N rapid taps always net exactly N regardless of React's batching/render timing — and rewiring every stepper `onPress` across all 6 files to pass a fixed `±1` delta instead of a precomputed absolute target. `updateQty(productId, qty)` itself was left untouched in the context (still absolute-value, still exported) since nothing else needs it removed, it's just no longer used by any stepper. The three prop-drilled `onUpdateQty` callback contracts (`home.tsx`'s `ProductCard`/`FrequentlyBoughtSection`, `order-again.tsx`'s `ProductCard`, `category/[slug].tsx`'s `ProductCard`) were each changed from `(p, qty) => void` to `(p, delta) => void` to match, removing their own `(cartItem?.quantity ?? 1) ± 1` closures in the same pass — those inner components had the identical race one layer further down, not just the two screens originally reported. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app (0 errors across all 6 changed files); not click-tested live (would need a physical rapid double-tap on a real device to observe the race directly).
- **Three stale-fetch races on rapid navigation, same class already fixed on the website (`SearchPage.tsx`) but never applied here.** `app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s `fetchFresh` had no internal request-id/cancellation guard at all — each location-change effect's own `cancelled` closure flag only prevented a *superseded effect from starting a new `fetchFresh` call*, it did nothing to stop an *already-in-flight* call's late-arriving response from overwriting fresher state once it resolved, so two quick location changes could let the older fetch resolve last and silently repaint the home grid with stale-location products. `app/product/[id].tsx`'s `fetchProduct` and `app/category/[slug].tsx`'s `fetchData` had the identical gap with zero guard of any kind — opening product/category A then quickly B could let A's response land after B's and show A's data under B's route. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by introducing the same request-sequence-guard idiom this codebase already uses elsewhere (`app/support/search.tsx`'s `requestIdRef`, a monotonically-increasing ref-counter compared after every `await` before any state-setting call): each of the three files gained a `useRef(0)` counter, incremented once per fetch, with every `setState` call gated by `if (myId !== requestIdRef.current) return;` after each `await`. `home.tsx`'s guard was placed inside the shared `fetchFresh` function itself (not at each of its several call sites) so every caller benefits uniformly without touching the surrounding effects. No `AbortController` introduced — matches the existing codebase convention of a pure ref-counter compare, not real request cancellation. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean on the customer app; not click-tested live (would need rapid real navigation between two products/categories/locations to observe the race directly).
- **`phone.tsx` required a valid email just to request an OTP, contradicting its own copy.** `isValid = phone.length === 10 && emailValid` gated the "Continue" button on a non-empty, regex-valid email, while the helper text right below it said "Used for order receipts. You can verify it after logging in" — implying optional/deferred. A user entering only a phone number got a permanently disabled button with no explanation. Confirmed safe to make optional: `sendOTP()` on this screen never sends email anyway (phone-only), `otp.tsx` already tolerates an empty email (`email || undefined`), `AuthContext.verifyOTPCode`'s `email` param is already optional, and `checkout.tsx`'s own email usage pulls from the persisted account record, not this screen's local state. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by changing the gate to `phone.length === 10 && (email.trim().length === 0 || emailValid)` — phone still required, email optional when blank, but still blocked on a malformed non-empty email (so a typo isn't silently accepted either). Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`app/support/search.tsx` results list was missing `keyboardShouldPersistTaps`.** Unlike `home.tsx` (`"handled"`) and several other list screens in this app (`wallet.tsx`, `onboarding.tsx`, `select-location.tsx`, `profile-setup.tsx`, `settings/profile.tsx`, `checkout.tsx`), this `FlatList` defaulted to RN's `"never"` — tapping a result while the keyboard was still open just dismissed the keyboard instead of registering the tap, requiring a second tap to actually open/add the product. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a one-line addition, `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, matching the existing convention exactly. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Order-tracking screen's 5s fallback poll ran unconditionally alongside the realtime channel**, doubling the request rate even when realtime was healthy and already covering every update. **Fixed 2026-07-27** in `hooks/useOrderTracking.ts` — the realtime channel's own `.subscribe()` status callback now sets a `realtimeConnectedRef`, and the fallback interval (still ticking every 5s) skips all but every 6th tick once realtime is confirmed `SUBSCRIBED`, becoming a true ~30s backstop instead of a duplicate poll; if the channel ever drops (status flips away from `SUBSCRIBED`), every tick immediately does real work again until it reconnects, with no gap in coverage. The separate 2s driver-location poll in the same hook was deliberately left unconditional — unlike the order-status realtime channel, there's no corresponding realtime subscription for `driver_locations` in this app (only the website has that), so this poll isn't duplicating anything; slowing it down wasn't part of this fix. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Widespread fire-and-forget `.catch(() => {})` patterns across cache writes and best-effort pings (24 call sites: cart/coupon/location/address/order-history persistence, session pings, push-token clearing, notification read-receipts, catalog cache pre-warming) meant a real, recurring failure was completely invisible in production** — indistinguishable from the ordinary, expected case of "nothing to report." **Fixed 2026-07-27** with a new shared `lib/logSilentFailure.ts` (`console.warn` with a descriptive context string) — every one of the 24 sites now logs its own specific failure instead of swallowing it silently; behavior is unchanged (still fire-and-forget, non-blocking) since this is visibility-only, not a retry/error-surfacing change. Two `SplashScreen.preventAutoHideAsync()`/`hideAsync()` calls in `app/_layout.tsx` were deliberately left as pure no-ops — those are expected-to-sometimes-fail native lifecycle calls (e.g. "already hidden"), not a cache/data concern, and logging them would just be noise. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **29+ more scattered raw `console.log`/`warn`/`error` call sites, no structured error tracking.** **Fixed 2026-07-31** across 16 files by splitting them by what each one actually was: 3 sites (`hooks/usePaymentFlow.ts`, `lib/trackingService.ts`, `lib/orderService.ts`) were genuinely fire-and-forget/degraded-continuation patterns (return a fallback, no UI change) that had just never been migrated to the existing `logSilentFailure()` helper from the fix above — moved onto it. The remaining ~14 sites *do* already surface to the user (an `Alert`, a `setError`/error-state render) but had no separate logging layer at all beyond the raw `console.error`; consolidated those into a new sibling `lib/logError.ts` (same shape as `logSilentFailure`, distinct name/doc-comment so the two don't get confused — one is "silent, no UI change," the other is "already shown to the user, this is just where it also gets logged"). A handful of ambiguous cases were resolved by reading the actual catch block rather than guessing from the message string alone: `app/notifications.tsx`'s fetch/mark-read failures set no error UI at all (→ silent), `app/location/select-map.tsx`'s reverse-geocode/current-location/autocomplete failures degrade quietly with no alert (→ silent) while its "place details failed" case does `Alert.alert` (→ surfaced), and `app/order/invoice/[id].tsx`'s share-failure also fires on an ordinary user-cancelled share sheet with no UI change either way (→ silent, not a real error). Neither new helper adds a third-party dependency — each is a single call site future Sentry/Bugsnag wiring could hook into later, a decision left to the user rather than made unilaterally here. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean (`expo lint` can't run at all in this repo — pre-existing, unrelated stray `src/` directory issue, already documented in Build/Test Status).
- **`lib/supabase.ts` only logged a "⚠️ CRITICAL" console warning if Supabase credentials were missing, then kept going** — invisible on a real device (nobody's watching Metro logs on a shipped build), so a misconfigured build would silently limp along with every Supabase call failing deep inside individual screens instead of one clear signal. An `assertSupabaseConfigured()` helper already existed for exactly this but was never actually called anywhere — dead code sitting next to the bug it looked like it was meant to prevent. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by replacing it with an exported `isSupabaseConfigured` boolean and adding an early check in `app/_layout.tsx`'s `RootLayout` that renders a dedicated "Configuration Error" screen instead of the app shell when it's false (hiding the native splash first so the message is actually visible) — the two module-level cache-prewarm calls above it in the same file already had their own `.catch()` handlers, so they can't throw ahead of this check even in the broken-config case. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **`invoiceService.ts` hardcoding literal placeholder GSTIN/phone strings as a silent fallback** — **found already resolved.** No file by that name exists in this app (the closest real code, the backend's `invoice.service.ts`, is what actually generates invoices for both apps); its `seller_gstin`/`seller_fssai`/`seller_pan`/`seller_cin` fields already fall back to an empty string, rendered as a plain "N/A" wherever displayed, never a fake-looking value like `'29XXXXXXXXXXXXX'`. Confirmed via a repo-wide grep for that literal string and for the field names — no reachable code path produces anything but an honest "N/A."
- **Biased shuffle** (`Array.sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5)`) **used for "suggested products."** **Fixed 2026-07-31** in `app/order/confirmation/[id].tsx` — replaced with a proper in-place Fisher-Yates shuffle, so all remaining candidate products have an equal chance of landing in the shown 6, instead of whichever ones the inconsistent comparator happened to favor. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **ETA display didn't handle a running-late order** — if `order.estimated_delivery_time` was already in the past, `app/order/track/[id].tsx` still rendered a plain "Estimated delivery by {time}" with no indication anything was off. **Fixed 2026-07-31** with a derived `isRunningLate` check (`estimated_delivery_time` timestamp < now) that switches the row to an amber "Running a little behind — was expected by {time}" state (`clock-alert-outline` icon, confirmed present in the installed glyph map, `C.warning` color) instead of the normal on-time phrasing; the normal case is completely unchanged. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Order-confirmation's "add more items" countdown timer and its order-fetch were independent effects** — previously flagged as awareness-only/not exploitable (the backend independently re-validates ownership and timing regardless of what the client had loaded), but investigating further found the race is real, not just theoretical: the countdown's `setInterval` starts from a hardcoded constant (`ADD_MORE_WINDOW_SECONDS`), completely independent of whether the order-load effect has resolved, so a slow fetch genuinely can still be in flight when the timer expires. **Fixed 2026-07-31** by adding `loading` to the add-items effect's guard condition (`if (!timerExpired || addItemsStartedRef.current || loading) return;`) and its dependency array — the flow now can't start until the order fetch has settled one way or the other, closing the gap even though it was never exploitable. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Android bundling failed outright with `Error: ... VirtualViewNativeComponent.js: Unable to determine event arguments for "onModeChange"`** — reported directly by the user via a real `expo start`/Metro error, not found during audit. Root cause: `node_modules/react-native@0.81.5` (matching `package.json`) had a stray nested duplicate, `node_modules/react-native/node_modules/react-native@0.86.2`, baked into `package-lock.json` by an unrelated dependency-cleanup commit (`17dc010`, "Update package dependencies..." — removed a few unused packages and regenerated the lockfile, which is what introduced the mismatched duplicate). Metro's codegen picked up the newer nested copy's `VirtualViewNativeComponent.js`, which uses Flow syntax the `0.81.5`-matched `@react-native/codegen` doesn't understand, failing the bundle before the app could even start. **Fixed 2026-07-30** with a clean reinstall (`rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json && npm install`) — `package.json` itself was untouched, so this simply forced npm to re-resolve without the stale duplicate; confirmed a single `react-native@0.81.5` remained afterward and a full `expo export --platform android` completed cleanly (1822 modules, no codegen errors) where it previously failed outright.
- **`npm audit` reported 27 high-severity vulnerabilities after the reinstall above** (the clean install surfaced findings a broken/stale prior lockfile had been masking). Investigated each: 25 of the 27 are one interconnected chain rooted in a single `brace-expansion` ReDoS advisory, propagating through old `minimatch`/`glob`/`eslint`/React Native's own build tooling (`@react-native/codegen`, `community-cli-plugin`, `dev-middleware` → `chromium-edge-launcher`) and Jest/Babel test plugins — all devDependencies/build tooling, confirmed via the `expo export` bundle contents that none of it ships to a user's device; the only non-breaking fix path for that chain requires major bumps to `eslint` (→10.x) and, critically, `react-native` (→**0.86.2** — the exact mismatched version just removed above, which would reintroduce the bundling failure) or `expo` (→57.x, a 3-major-version jump). **Deliberately left open** — the real-world exploit path (an attacker controlling a glob pattern fed into a local build/lint command) doesn't meaningfully apply to this app, and forcing those bumps risks far more than it fixes; revisit as a coordinated Expo-SDK-57 upgrade later, not a partial forced patch now. The 2 remaining findings were real and independently fixable: **`sharp`** (0.34.5, dev-only — used solely by `scripts/generate-icons.js`) had real libvips CVEs (CVE-2026-33327/33328/35590/35591) fixed below `0.35.0`. **Fixed 2026-07-30** by bumping `sharp` to `^0.35.3` (`npm install sharp@^0.35.3 --save-dev --legacy-peer-deps`, the `--legacy-peer-deps` needed only to bypass an unrelated pre-existing `react`/`react-dom` peer mismatch, not anything caused by this bump) — dropped the audit's high-severity count from 27 to 25. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` clean.

---

## 2026-07-28: Billing Info feature build + bug fixes

New feature (not a bug fix): added a "Billing Info" verification step to both mobile apps — store owner (owner name read-only, owner photo, bank account number, IFSC code, branch name, passbook/cheque photo) and rider (driver name read-only, driver photo, UPI ID). New backend endpoints `GET/POST /store-owner/stores/:id/billing-info` and `GET/PATCH /delivery-partner/billing-info`; new DB columns `stores.bank_account_number`/`bank_ifsc_code`/`bank_branch_name`/`bank_passbook_storage_path` and `delivery_partners.upi_id` (migration `20260917000000_billing_info.sql`). The passbook/cheque photo deliberately reuses the private `store-documents` bucket but is stored via its own `stores` column rather than a new `store_verification_documents` row, specifically so it can never be miscounted toward the 7-document "ready for review" threshold. Both apps' `VerificationNavBar` gained a 4th "Billing" tab.

### Supabase schema & migrations
